# ReachKit — MVP Specification

The nine features below are the product. Anything not needed by one of them is out of scope (§11).
UI is `docs/DESIGN.md` — this file does not describe pixels. What is on each screen, in what order and with what wording, is that screen's board named there: structure and wording, never a pixel baseline.
Every customer-visible sentence is a key in `src/lib/presentation/copy/keys/`. A missing sentence is drafted and shipped, never `TODO(copy)`; the owner corrects the wording (owner, 2026-09-16, #759). A mail carrying the marker still sends nothing.
Pinned numbers live in `src/lib/config/constants.ts`. Each section states the rule that is current; a line a later ruling replaced is deleted, and git keeps the history.
A new dated ruling is its own file under `docs/spec/decisions/`, headed by the section it belongs to, and reads as part of that section; `node scripts/spec-decisions.mjs` lists them per section (owner, 2026-10-18, #1525). The lines already in a section stay there.

## §0 Terms

| name | block/warn | outcome protected | ruling |
|---|---|---|---|
| Goal | warn | Users pay ReachKit for content that improves their SEO and GEO ranking. Each day the system picks the best content for the business, from what it knows of it, the posts the site already has and what its competitors rank for, and publishes a new post or updates an existing page (§7). Everything is right-sized to the business's footprint (§6), so a site that ranks for almost nothing still gets small, winnable targets, never an empty calendar. Every change serves this goal. | owner, 2026-09-16 |
| Current focus | warn | The work in hand is the measurement: a site's scan returns the correct competitors, market, keywords to work towards and AI answers to optimise for (§6). That context and the site's own information build the daily page (§7). | owner D35, 2026-10-03, #1210 |
| Every kind of customer | warn | A rule in market understanding, the knowledge base, competitors, terms, measured searches or topics is general: it holds for a plumber, a restaurant, a shop and a German bakery as it holds for reachkit.app. A change to these stages passes the customer panel (`npm run panel`) before the owner is asked to measure. | owner, 2026-10-07, #1304 |
| SEO / GEO | warn | Google organic findability of the customer's domain / whether ChatGPT, Google AI Mode and AI Overviews name them. | SPEC, 2026-09-16 |
| AI | warn | The customer-facing name of the AI measure: the tab, the report section, the mail section and the verdict bar are all "AI". "GEO" is a gloss, once on the landing and once in the report's method, never a label. A sentence about what the engines do says "AI answers". Routes, keys and types keep `ai-answers`. A line elsewhere that says "AI answers tab" or "AI Visibility" means this tab. | owner, 2026-10-15, #1444 |
| Visibility Score | warn | One 0–100 number: how easily buyers find you on Google and in AI. `Score = round(∛(Foundations × Answerability × Presence))`, `Presence = max(1, 0.5 × SearchPresence + 0.5 × AIPresence)`, over the halves that were read (§2). Each factor 0–100. Bands: invisible 0 · hard-to-find 25 · findable 50 · dominant 75. A score always carries its range. The factors are **Readiness** (code `foundations`) and **Content** (code `answerability`); Presence shows as its halves **Google** (`SearchPresence`) and **AI** (`AIPresence`), so the verdict draws four bars; the formula is unchanged by the names. Readiness or Content unmeasured means no score. Shown on the free report and as Overview's first tile (§4). | owner, 2026-10-15, #1444, #1457; range E9, 2026-10-24 |
| AI bar counts what the AI card counts | warn | An AI answer counts for the site where it mentioned or linked it. That one predicate, `namesOrLinks` in `src/lib/measure/drivers.ts`, decides the AI bar, the AI card's x of N and its row marks. `AIPresence = max(1, 100 × answers that mentioned or linked the site ÷ answered AI Overviews read)`. On the free report the AI bar is `round(10 × x ÷ N)` of the card beside it; on a paid scan the page questions' AI Overview cells stay in AIPresence. An unread AI is never 0, and AI answering none of at least 4 searches leaves Presence to search presence. The range is the Wilson interval of the one rate. | ux-designer and seo-master, owner-delegated E5, 2026-10-24, #1622 |
| Market | warn | What the business is judged in; customer text says "market", never "category". It fixes the twelve searches, the competitors and the volumes. For a paying site ReachKit identifies and maintains it from evidence, re-checked each scan; the founder may override it but never has to. The free report's visitor may correct it once (§2). | owner D28, 2026-10-02, #1186; words #1444 |
| Site profile | warn | Page inventory (URL, title, h1, purpose), site name, products and claims, brand-voice summary. Up to 100 pages from the sitemap and internal links. The free scan stores inventory and name; voice is paid-only, shown at setup. | SPEC, 2026-09-12 |
| Competitor (code: rival) | warn | Customer text says "competitors", never "rivals", and "market leaders" where it means the same. One of the 3–5 real competitors ReachKit identifies from evidence and re-checks once a month; the founder may override them but never has to (§5). A publisher, aggregator or platform in a top ten is a source, not a competitor. | owner D24, D28, 2026-10-02, #1161, #1186; monthly #1700; words #1444 |
| Sources are not competitors | warn | A government's, army's, public service's, university's or school's host, read by its suffix, is never a competitor wherever it ranks. A job page is read by its host, address or title. A job seeker's search, one whose titled top ten is at least half job pages, gives no competitor and proposes no Write page on the free report or the paid plan; a skipped search counts as no measurement, never a zero. A business whose own category is about jobs, recruiting, staffing or training keeps those searches. A source row (`isSourceRow`, `src/lib/market/rivals/domains.ts`) is one reading for every holder, and the Google table's "Who's first" names no source. No list of hosts is added. | seo-master and architect, 2026-10-31, #2194 (D24 extension; no owner ruling needed) |
| Search (code: question) | warn | One of the twelve buyer searches the scan measures, called "searches" in customer text, never "questions": searches this business could answer, chosen by business fit first and demand second, right-sized per §6. They stay the Google searches; the AI measure asks tracked buyer prompts instead. | SPEC, 2026-09-16, 2026-09-29; owner D19, 2026-10-03; words #1444 |
| Tracked buyer prompt | warn | One of the about 25 prompts a buyer would put to an answer engine, generated from the site's structured market and asked every week; in the app, "questions we ask AI". The AI measure is the share of their answers that mention or link the site, a 4-week rolling mention rate with its margin, never an AI position (§6). | owner D19, 2026-10-03; words #1444 |
| Business fit | warn | 0–3, how much the business's own offering is the answer to a search: 3 it is the answer, 2 it plainly helps, 1 it can only be mentioned, 0 no relation. Judged against the knowledge base (§6). | SPEC, 2026-09-29 |
| Knowledge base | warn | What the business sells and how: each offering with its features, prices and uses; who it serves; the problems it solves; how it differs from each competitor; its proof; its buyers' common questions; integrations and places. Every item carries the page it was read from, or says the founder gave it (§5). | SPEC, 2026-09-29, #1133 |
| Footprint | warn | How many keywords the site itself ranks for, and each competitor's count: the presence right-sizing scales with (§6), not the score's Presence factor. An internal input only, never shown as a score or a lead. | SPEC, 2026-09-16; owner D22, D24, 2026-10-02 |
| Opportunity | warn | Write (missing page) · Improve (owned URL) · Fix (technical) · Earn (first-party citable asset on their domain, never outreach). | SPEC |
| Asset | warn | What one day publishes: a new post, a new page, or an update to an existing page. At most one a day. | SPEC |
| Veto | warn | A finished page waits, visible and stoppable, before it publishes. Default 24 h, range 1–7 days, never zero. | SPEC |
| One vocabulary | warn | One word per idea on every surface: free report, app, mail, landing and meta. In an AI answer a site is **mentioned** or **linked**, never named, cited or quoted. A Google position is **the first page** or **the first two pages**. Checking again is a **scan** ("Scan again"). The Google ladder reads Live, Found by Google, Showing, First page, with no Listed rung. Section, tab and mail names equal the bar names: **Google**, **AI**, **Competitors**. The public header's button says **Free scan**. Keys nothing in `src/` reads are deleted. | owner, 2026-10-15, #1444; ladder and button, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| Short titles | warn | Every heading, card title, finding line, tab, mail subject and meta title is at most 6 words, one idea and no em-dash; a number goes in the figure and any explanation in one line under it of at most 12 words. Meta titles join with "·". Notices and leads over 20 words are cut to the one fact the reader needs. | owner, 2026-10-15, #1444 |
| Plain words | warn | Every customer-facing string, from the landing to every mail, follows O6's twelve rules: sentences of 8 to 12 words and at most 15; at most 2 sentences a paragraph; lead with what the reader gets or does; plain words, never SEO, index, crawl, CNAME (outside setup's DNS steps), derivation, pass, re-score, supply, spares, veto, draft, opportunity, keyword, prompt or fetched; "you" and "we"; no asides; numbers from the facts; each thing once a screen; honest claims only, never "recommends you", never ChatGPT on the free report, never a ranking promise; one name a concept; an error says what happened and what to do. Customer pages' own text, engine names, legal wording, owner-only keys, `keys/de/` and the Hosted, Review, AppCosts and Legal boards are exempt. `scripts/lib/plain-words.mjs` is the one parser; `audit` and `tests/ui/design/strategy.test.ts` compare each touched key file and board with the pull request's base, and a count may fall and never rise. A rule-4 word with no hit left is a retired name, zero tolerance, on keys and boards. | owner O6, 2026-10-31, #2145; base count, architect, 2026-11-01, #2222 |
| The canvas is the board set | warn | The signed-off canvas in `docs/design/boards/canvas/` (published Version 22) is the board set `docs/DESIGN.md` › Boards links. The round boards stay in the tree, marked superseded. A board amended by hand is amended in its `.dc.html` text, and that text wins over a render not exported since. Where a board and a SPEC rule disagree, the SPEC rule wins. | owner, 2026-10-19, #1581; hand edits 2026-10-20, #1716; 2026-10-22, #1790; 2026-10-24, #1983 |
| Canvas samples | warn | The Settings header sample reads "Harbor Plumbing · Austin". The Monday mail's AI row shows the pages AI linked this week, and reputation is monthly. The sample short week (6 pages plus a skipped day) stays. Examples keep `.example` domains, so no screen shows a stranger's site. | owner, 2026-10-19, #1581; `.example` Q2, 2026-10-20, #1716 |
| Board drawings | warn | Every score gauge is a full ring, and an unscored gallery card draws only "Not scored". A row of squares or dots is one proportional bar. A page sketch is a band with lucide `FileText`. Icons follow E26–E31: one `BookOpen` per hosted index group, number-only step tiles, no section-heading tiles on Legal or FAQ tiles, `Pencil` for Edit this page, `Building2` for Your business, `MapPin` for the Maps note and setup's place. The editor toolbar is Heading · Bold · Italic · Link · List · Quote · Table. Veto's failed chip is warning-edged `MessageCircleWarning`; its expired chip is neutral. | ux-designer, owner-delegated E16–E36, 2026-10-24, #1983; V4 #2002 |
| The parity check | warn | `scripts/design/parity.mjs` runs inside an existing gate, never a fifth. A screen pull request never raises the missing count on the routes it touches, and an issue that claims a board brings that board to 0 missing. | owner, 2026-10-20, #1716 |
| New 0¢ figures | warn | The report's competitor tallies and all of its missing pages; the best mover; who your buyers see first; the count of fine pages; Billing's "What you got this month". | owner E1–E3, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| Overview leads with today's page | warn | Overview shows today's page first, then the ladder; the other screens keep their numbers first (§4). Overview draws no module its board does not show: no DoneThisWeek, ProcessTimeline, WhatWeRead, MainSiteLink, MisstatedFacts or LinkedTiles. | owner E4, 2026-10-19, #1581; Q12, 2026-10-20, #1716 |
| Plan is a 7-day calendar | warn | The Plan screen is a 7-day calendar (§7). | owner E5, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| Sign-in preview | warn | Sign-in shows what is waiting inside (§3). | owner E6, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| Stop is a ghost button | warn | Stop is a ghost button in the warning colour. | owner E7, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| A stopped topic is dropped for good | warn | Every screen that tells a stop says, in its board's words, that ReachKit won't write this topic again: the stop page, Overview and Plan. | owner Q6, 2026-10-20, #1716 |
| Hosted page links and drawings | warn | Hosted pages carry Book and Quote links and inline drawings, subject to the seo-master's check. An offering page shows a date only, with no byline, and keeps the made line. A removed page on a live site answers 410 with a short visitor body. | owner E8, E9, 2026-10-19, #1581; R1, 2026-10-22, #1715 |
| One true check count | warn | The count of checks a page passed comes from code, and each check row is named for the rule the battery runs. | owner E10, 2026-10-19, #1581; R4, 2026-10-22, #1748 |
| Setup has three steps | warn | Setup is Site · Check · Connect (§5). | owner E11, 2026-10-19, #1581; Q5, 2026-10-20, #1716 |
| No VAT invoice question | warn | The FAQ has no VAT invoice question while Stripe Tax is deferred (§1). | owner E12, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| Compare, teardown and legal summary | warn | The Compare chart carries a dated caption and a trademark line; the Plausible teardown a neutral sketch, a direct link and a "checked" date; Legal an "In short" summary that reads "Cookies only to sign you in and keep your account safe. No tracking or advertising cookies.", with §10 Cookies naming the sign-in cookie, the 30-minute export cookie and the three settings the browser keeps. A lawyer confirms before selling. | owner E14–E16, 2026-10-19, #1581; Q4, 2026-10-20, #1716 |
| The German answer | warn | The FAQ's language answer is built from the supported countries, as the landing renders it: German sites are measured and written in German, the English-speaking countries in English, anywhere else in the nearest of these in English, and the report says so; the website and dashboard are in English. Landing, Landing on a phone and How it works carry it. | owner G1, 2026-10-20, #1694 |
| Report lists every missing page | warn | The report lists pages 6 onward open, under "All N, nothing hidden"; with exactly one missing page it draws no such line. The report's other closed sections stay closed. | owner Q3, 2026-10-20, #1716; R3, 2026-10-22, #1599 |
| Page-ready mail until the first Monday | warn | The page-ready mail is on until the first Monday mail, then off. The Mail tab's row says so, keeps a customer's own setting and names the Monday mail's per-page Stop. | owner D3, 2026-10-19, #1581; Q10, 2026-10-20, #1716 |
| A waiting page names no go-live time | warn | While the site is unconnected, the waiting-page card says the page goes live once the setting works, and names no time. | owner Q11, 2026-10-20, #1716 |
| Offering label | warn | "service or product page" is the label of an offering page, general to every customer. | owner R2, 2026-10-22, #1702, #1617 |
| Billing names its real control | warn | The billing card's cancel line names its real control, filled from `settings.billing.manage`, on the Plan row. | owner R24, 2026-10-23, #1853 |
| Pages read at weeks 2, 4 and 8 | warn | Each page is read at weeks 2, 4 and 8, with adaptive depth, at no added cost (§6). | owner D2, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| Pages kept 30 days | warn | After cancelling, pages are kept 30 days, with the export (§3). | owner D5, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| Google and Bing visits | warn | Google and Bing visits are counted, after a light trust check (§7). | owner D6, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| Daily spend ceiling | warn | The product spends at most 1,000¢ a day plus 100¢ per paying site (§6). | owner D7, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| Labs fallback for Showing | warn | "Showing" may come from a labelled Labs fallback (§7). | owner D8, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| No Search Console | warn | No Search Console or Bing Webmaster for now; IndexNow continues. The Search Console code is kept inert, with no keys, for 90 days from 2026-10-19 (§11). | owner D9, 2026-10-19, #1581 |
| Weekly cap 40¢ | warn | `CAPS.WEEKLY_C` stays 40¢; about 33¢ per site per week is the real figure. | owner W1, 2026-10-20, #1716 |
| Deep slots 12 | warn | `DEEP_SLOTS` stays 12. | owner R6, 2026-10-22, #1719, #1780 |
| Branch protection not strict | warn | Branch protection stays `strict: false`. Release QA's GO needs a green `typecheck · lint · unit` run on the current `origin/main` head for a pull request whose changes show `ui=true` or `unit=full`. | owner R7, 2026-10-22, #1785 |
| Weekly review timer | warn | `rk-weekly.timer` runs the weekly review on Mondays at 07:00 UTC. | owner R8, 2026-10-22, #1631 |
| Every SPEC section is rewritten | warn | A section's rewrite folds its dated lines and decision files into one table of rules with the columns name, block/warn, outcome protected and ruling, and the decision file that records the rewrite lists the files it replaces. Only the owner makes a rule `block`; `blockRules` in `scripts/budgets.json` holds each section's count. | owner C7, 2026-11-08, #2527, #2513 |

## §1 Landing and marketing

**User gets** One field, no account: type a domain and scan. Why AI answers matter, what ReachKit does, how to start. One price on `/pricing`: €49/month, VAT included, cancel in one click. Privacy, terms and imprint one click from every public page.

| name | block/warn | outcome protected | ruling |
|---|---|---|---|
| One field | warn | The landing has exactly one text input and one submit. No sign-in or payment comes before submit. Every other call to action brings that field into view with the cursor in it. | SPEC |
| Any written domain | warn | Any written domain (scheme, `www`, path, case) reaches one report. A malformed value stays on the page with the typed text and one error line. | SPEC |
| One plan | warn | One plan only: no tiers, seats, annual plan or add-ons. Businesses only, month to month: no consumer or contract-term copy. | SPEC; owner, 2026-11-06, #2452 |
| One offer component | warn | The offer is one component, used on the report and on `/pricing`; only the Start target differs. | SPEC |
| Same chrome on every public page | warn | Every public route, the report, `/veto/{token}`, `/opt-out/{token}` and `/signin` included, has the same header and footer. The header is the mark, *How it works · Pricing · About · Scan library*, *Sign in* and the solid *Free scan*. The footer holds the brand, the rights, the removal address, Product (with *How it works* and *About*), Learn (*Articles*, *Scan library* and the library indexes) and Legal. The report keeps its copy-link beside the call to action. | owner, 2026-09-15, #714; 2026-10-10, #1396; 2026-10-15, #1458; 2026-10-18, #1512; four links 2026-11-06, #2452 |
| Every control acts | warn | Public pages use the app's tiles, bars and wording. Every control on them acts or is not drawn; the report's removed and refused arms offer a way back to the landing. | owner, 2026-09-20, #908 |
| Sign-in and stop are splits | warn | Sign-in (with its link-sent and old-link arms) and stop-this-page (with its stopped arm) are full-width splits: the interaction on the left, the accent panel filling the right half from header to footer. | owner, 2026-09-20, #937 |
| One mark | warn | The mark is Round 9 *Fade*, in the chip: one drawing at every size, the wordmark beside it in the product's own face, no second lockup or colourway. | owner, 2026-09-21, #982 |
| Landing order | warn | The landing is boards PubLanding and PubLandingPhone of canvas v37, with Tidyslot (`EXAMPLES.software`) as the one example business on the whole page. In order: the hero beside the story card (on a phone the card comes after the hero); three steps, each beside its picture, linking How it works; *Why you should care*; *What €49 a month gets you*, eight cards and the price band; the questions, the first one open; the newest articles; the closing band, whose one button returns to the field; the footer. | owner, 2026-10-19, #1596; 2026-11-01, #2246; v37 2026-11-06, #2452 |
| The hero | warn | The headline is exactly "Get found where your competitors are and customers search." The subline is "We research your business and competitors, then write pages that get you found, on autopilot." Under the field: the offer ticks, the count of sites scanned and the six kinds. The `<title>` stays "Get found on Google and AI · ReachKit". The Why heading is "Buyers ask AI what to use." | owner O1–O3, 2026-10-31, #2174; headline 2026-11-06, #2452 |
| The story card | warn | Four frames of at most four visible words each: competitors get found, we research, we write pages, you get found. It is drawn as the board draws it, with one *Live* mark, an empty slot at `border-base-content/50` (at least 3:1), a screen-reader-only title and no caption. Its search is the one Tidyslot search that fits its bar at 1280 and 390. | owner, 2026-10-31, #2174; 2026-11-01, #2231; 2026-11-03, #2348; 2026-11-06, #2452 |
| Six kinds of business | warn | "Works for any business with a website" shows the six `BUSINESS_KINDS` with the Scan library filter's words and icons, under the landing's hero and How it works' head. It is a list label, not a title. | owner, 2026-11-01, #2246; 2026-11-07, #2271 |
| Sites scanned from 100 | warn | The hero's proof row counts distinct domains listed in `free_scan_listing`, not counting ReachKit's own or the customer panel's. Below 100 it is not drawn; from 100 it reads the count rounded down to a ten, as "120+ sites scanned · with the free scan". It says sites, never customers. | owner, 2026-11-01, #2249; 2026-11-06, #2452 |
| Our own report only | warn | No public page features another company's report as proof. The landing draws no report row. About's proof card is ReachKit's own free report, from the approved list in `src/app/(public)/_how/examples.ts` (only `reachkit.app` until a customer consents), drawn only while it is listed with a stored score, and states no score. | owner, 2026-11-01, #2265; 2026-11-03, #2281, #2299; 2026-11-06, #2452 |
| Edit or stop any page | warn | The eighth plan card is "Edit or stop any page": each page waits `{hours}` hours before it goes live. | owner, 2026-11-06, #2452 |
| No guarantee | warn | There is no money-back guarantee, refund question or gate note on any public page. | owner, 2026-11-06, #2452 (#1598 closed) |
| VAT lines stay | warn | Tax is not reworded: every "VAT included" or "tax included" line stays as it is. Pricing's card and the report's offer read "a month · tax included" and "Cancel any time from Settings." | owner, 2026-11-01, #2266; 2026-11-06, #2452 |
| Walkthrough waits for its video | warn | No walkthrough band, heading or placeholder is drawn until the owner's video file is in `public/` (`WALKTHROUGH_SRC`). | owner, 2026-09-22, #734; 2026-11-01, #2246; 2026-11-06, #2452, #1030 |
| ReachKit's own articles | warn | The footer's *Articles* link and the landing's newest three article titles, each a plain link in the server's HTML with *All articles* beside them, are read at render from the stored live publications of ReachKit's own site. With none stored, neither is drawn. | owner, 2026-10-18, #1512 |
| Who ReachKit is, in markup | warn | The landing states an Organization (`https://reachkit.app/#organization`, the wordmark, the home, the mark as logo, no `sameAs`) and a WebSite. Each comparison, teardown and answer states an Article (its heading, the dates it prints, that Organization as author and publisher) and a breadcrumb of the links it draws. Every indexable public route names `/share-card` as `og:image`, except `/` and the report. A title that names ReachKit takes no `· ReachKit` suffix. The 404 has a title. | owner, 2026-10-18, #1512 |
| The pace | warn | The promise is "up to {weekPages} pages a week, new or updated". No public sentence, mail or app line says or implies one new page a day; at most `PACE.NEW_PAGES_PER_WEEK` are new in seven days. | owner, 2026-10-10, #1393; A1 2026-10-19, #1596 |
| No typed figure | warn | Every number on the public pages is a slot filled at render from `src/lib/config/constants.ts` and `HARD_RULES`, through `src/app/(public)/_how/facts.ts`. A public claim is held against the constant or the code path that makes it true. | owner, 2026-10-10, #1396, #1393 |
| Only what it does today | warn | Public pages state only what the product does today. They describe autopilot: a page goes live on its own, and the customer may edit or stop it first; no sentence says a page waits for the customer to read it. "ReachKit rewrites" a title or description is said with "on WordPress". The landing's preview draws no score. | owner, 2026-10-10, #1396, #1393 |
| What is not measured | warn | `/pricing` says what €49 buys once, and lists "What ReachKit doesn't do" once under the plan card, with the line that Perplexity is not measured (§11). | owner D7, 2026-09-29, #1154; O6 rule 8, 2026-10-31, #2173 |
| How it works and About | warn | `/how-it-works` and `/about` are public, indexed and in the sitemap. About says who builds ReachKit, why, the rules it keeps, and links How it works; its founder and newest page are stored rows, not drawn where none is stored. | owner, 2026-10-10, #1396 |
| How it works page | warn | Board PubHowItWorks: the head (headline, lead, the free scan, the six kinds, four steps), then `#research`, `#competitors`, `#questions`, `#pages`, `#true`, `#found`, `#you-do`, `#value`, `#faq` and `#start`, one picture of Tidyslot per section. Nothing is folded but the questions; there is no example switch. The free scan is offered in the head and at the close, the checkout once in `#value`. It says once that pages are written with Claude, an AI model, with no model version or prompt. `<main>` holds at most 1,200 words. | owner, 2026-10-20, #1597; 2026-11-07, #2271, #2299 |
| Every old address answers | warn | Every address an earlier ReachKit published answers again: `/compare` and its 24 comparisons, `/teardowns` with its teardowns and RSS feed, `/answers/{question}`, and the free tools, which read one public page through the scan's guarded reader at no cost. Each article is corrected to what ReachKit does today, its facts about another company dated and sourced. Addresses with a page today 301 to it; those with nothing 410. The middleware decides after the hosted edge, so a customer's page is never moved. `tests/app/library/old-paths.json` lists every address and `scripts/old-paths.mjs` checks a deployment. | owner, 2026-10-15, #1458 |
| Library pages keep their ruled words | warn | Where a library board drops a ruled line, the ruling wins: the Compare caption keeps its dated hedge, and every tool comparison keeps its trademark line and "All comparisons". Only the ChatGPT comparison takes the board's words. A teardown's sources keep their "label (host)" form; "All teardowns" stays under each teardown. A tool's breadcrumb carries the link to `/tools`. The legal text does not change for a design. | owner, 2026-11-03, #2280, #2281, #2282 |
| One solid button per screen | warn | On a library or tool page the close's Scan is the one solid button; a tool's "Check this page" and the free scan card's "Scan my site" are soft. | owner, 2026-11-03, #2282 |

**Done when** `Example.com`, `https://www.example.com/pricing` and `EXAMPLE.COM` hit the same report. `/pricing` states €49/month VAT included and has one checkout control. `/privacy`, `/terms` and `/imprint` are in the landing footer.

## §2 Free scan

**User gets** A scan of any domain with no account. Named stages with elapsed times (no spinner, %, countdown). A permanent public report: score, band, limiting factor, AI-answers card, Google-search card, technical issues (§9), the DIY method, one first-page proposal. Full first page mailed on request.

| name | block/warn | outcome protected | ruling |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cost and time | warn | A free pass costs at most 12¢ and runs at most 50 s (target 40 s) under the 60 s platform limit, and outlives the HTTP response. It builds the site profile (up to 100 pages) inside the same cap; the weekly pass refreshes it. | SPEC; 2026-09-12 |
| Repeat and cool-down | warn | The same domain inside 7 days gets its stored report and spends nothing. A failed scan has a 24 h cool-down. | SPEC |
| Free scan bounds | warn | At most 5 scans per IP an hour, 1 in flight per IP, 200 a day, 20 per IP-hash in 24 h and 3 per domain in 24 h, whoever asks. All are counted in admission, and each refuses in writing with the wait until the oldest counted scan ages out. `domain_blocks` is the takedown list, not a rate limit. | SPEC; 2026-09-18, #885 |
| Fail closed | warn | A free-scan bound or the day's spend that cannot be read refuses the scan with the paused line and logs the step. Every paid call reads the day's spend again first; an unreadable total skips the call and the pass holds. | owner, 2026-09-16, #792 |
| Roll forward only | warn | A stored report carries the version that wrote it; `readStoredReport` lifts it forward, never back, and throws on what it cannot lift. Production is never rolled back: a bad build is fixed by the next deploy (revert on main, deploy that), and a deploy that fails to build is never aliased. Procedure: `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §1. | 2026-09-18, #887 |
| Seed ladder | warn | The free scan walks the deep pass's seed ladder (§6): up to 3 extra seeds, head term then vocabulary, plus the ranked rows already bought, with the 50 → 20 → 10 steps. It buys the next seed while it holds fewer than four searches and the searches' share of the cap still affords that seed and a SERP for every search selected. A market that yields fewer is reported with what it yielded. | owner, 2026-09-17, #835; 2026-10-04, #1235 |
| Google reads beside the crawl | warn | A pass that reads its market from the home page alone starts its Google reads beside the crawl and waits for the crawl only before the report is composed. A pass that concludes its market from crawled pages waits for the crawl first. | owner, 2026-10-14, #1419 |
| Unread searches finished later | warn | A pass that stores searches it did not read (`not_attempted`, `undeterminable`) while the cap still holds money marks the report (`finishingReads`) and sends `scan/finish-reads`. The job reads only those searches, once, SERP cache first, on the pass's own ledger, writes them in and re-scores through `rescore_scan`. A search that fails again stays unmeasured. Meanwhile the report says "Still reading 3 searches…" for at most ten minutes. Stored scans are not read again. | owner, 2026-10-15, #1440 |
| Cut-off factor | warn | A factor a ceiling cut off reads "—" with no band word, one line per unmeasured driver; measured cards still render. | SPEC |
| Retry this part | warn | A card a ceiling cut off offers "Retry this part", which re-runs only that part: the market is read back off the stored report, every other measurement comes from the ledger at 0¢, and the original pass and its retries share one 12¢ cap, each with its own 50 s. A pass that spent the cap is offered no buying retry. A retry never leaves the report with less on it. | owner, 2026-09-22, #715 |
| Measurement line | warn | Every report state that shows a figure says in one always-visible line where Google was searched (`report.measurement`; elsewhere than US English, §6). | owner, 2026-09-22, #715 |
| Reused answers | warn | A second scan inside §6's SERP window is answered from the ledger at 0¢, and its answers are marked "Reused from {date}", the ledger row's own date. | owner, 2026-09-22, #715 |
| One market correction | warn | The market may be corrected once within 7 days; the correction reuses the cached AI answers and says so. | SPEC |
| Unreadable home page | warn | A home page that cannot be read gives no score, no competitors and no invented number, and one line saying the home page could not be read. | SPEC |
| No error page as the home | warn | A home read with a 4xx or 5xx, an empty body, a bare string or a short rate-limit page is no home document (`notTheSiteOf`, `src/lib/measure/challenge.ts`). It is asked once more after `Retry-After` (1 s where none is given, never past 3 s); a second miss ends the pass `site_unreadable`. Such a page never enters the crawl, the knowledge base or the passages. A profile stored from one is re-derived at 0¢ from a stored good read, else at the next scheduled pass. | 2026-10-14, #1427 |
| No bot-protection page as the site | warn | A protection's own page (Cloudflare, Akamai, DataDome, PerimeterX, Imperva, Sucuri, AWS WAF, Vercel, F5) is told from the response, never from a status alone (`src/lib/measure/challenge.ts`), and is no page of the site anywhere. On the home page the vendor's rendered read is asked once, for 0.15¢ and at most 5 s. Where that fails, the site's pages are unmeasured (no score, no band), the report says the site's bot protection stopped the reader, the pass ends `complete` and the site stays listed. A protection's name is never the site's name. `scripts/smoke.sh` fails a gallery card named for a protection, and a blocked report whose market was not read from search. | owner, 2026-10-10, #1379; #1392 |
| No report kept off a protection page | warn | A current report is judged at read time by the home document it was measured from (the newest `fetches` row at or before its home facts). One measured off a protection's page reads as the pass would store it today and is not protected by the re-scan window; its gallery card shows no score. Only a home document with a refusing status, a mitigation header or at most 100 kB is read whole; a read the ledger cannot answer leaves the report as stored. | owner, 2026-10-10, #1383 |
| Market from the search listing | warn | Only where bot protection, or a home page too slow to read, kept the reader off the site, the profile is read from the site's ranked searches and their Google titles and snippets, already bought (`src/lib/scan/search-footprint.ts`, one `readMarket` path). The searches and AI answers then run as for any site, and the report says the market came from Google search (`report.market.from-search`). Score parts stay unmeasured. Fewer than 10 ranked searches, fewer than 5 beyond the brand's name, or no category read means no market. The site is measured in its address's country and language. | owner, 2026-10-10, #1387 |
| A ranked site gets a market | warn | Where the pages were read but the model gave no profile, the profile is read from the search listing while half the market stage's time is left (`report.market.from-listing`). A suggestions purchase that did not answer leaves the ladder going on the ranked rows (`suggestionsUnread`). A stored report with no market for a ranked site is re-measured once it is 24 h old (`MARKET_REMEASURE_H`). | 2026-10-15, #1450 |
| Slow home page | warn | A free pass's plain home read gives up after 5 s (paid passes keep 8 s); the vendor's browser is then asked once and waited on 3.5 s. Where it fails, the site is `home_timeout` and its market is read from the search listing. | 2026-10-15, #1449 |
| Model answers read generously | warn | A profile list past its cap (audience 2–4, competitors 5, vocabulary 12, brand tokens 6) is cut to the cap in the model's order and kept. A missing field, a wrong type, an extra field or a list under its floor fails and is not re-asked for a size miss. A pass that read no market names the step in `scan_pass`, and the report says it could not be built. | 2026-09-18, #898 |
| A placeholder names nothing | warn | Empty, `unknown`, `n/a`, `none`, `null` or anything wholly in angle brackets is no category, name or profile field: it is dropped when the model answers it, when a report is read and in every stored scan. A reading with nothing left says *Not read yet*. | owner, 2026-10-10, #1377; 2026-10-17, #1479 |
| Company name | warn | The name is read from `og:site_name`, `application-name`, structured data's Organization or WebSite, then the title, split at a title's separators, keeping the part that spells the domain. Calls to action, page sections and product categories ("Sign up for free", "HRIS"), placeholders and a protection's name are no name (`src/lib/site-profile/site-name.ts`, no site named in it). The fallback is the domain's label, cased as the site writes it. `storedSiteName` is the one reader of `site_profiles.site_name`. | 2026-10-10, #1380, #1384; 2026-10-17, #1479 |
| Report date | warn | A report and its figures are dated when its scan started; a cached page read keeps its own date for the home facts only. The re-scan and correction windows count from the scan's date. On a blocked site the bot-protection line is the one notice, above a refusal in force. | 2026-10-10, #1392 |
| Reports are noindex | warn | Reports are `noindex` forever and not in the sitemap; nothing is blurred or paywalled. | SPEC |
| Searches show text only | warn | The twelve searches show their search text only, with no per-search volume. | SPEC |
| Score parts | warn | The verdict draws the four parts Website basics, Answers questions, Google and AI, as `n/10`, closed under "How this score is split"; the gap card says "Biggest gap: Website basics" or "Biggest gap: Answers questions". | SPEC; owner R4, 2026-10-31, #2165 |
| Score shows its range | warn | The report and Overview show a score's estimate as its range only ("somewhere between {low} and {high}"), never a confidence word. The stored `verdict.estimate` and the rough/close rule decide only the width. | ux-designer E9, owner-delegated, 2026-10-24, #1971 |
| A score always says how sure | warn | AI presence's rates are over the read results that showed an AI answer; where AI answered none of at least 4 read, Presence is search presence alone and the card says AI does not answer these yet. Where the pages were read there is always a score with a 95% Wilson range; an unread Presence half counts as mid-scale and spans its scale. Wider than 8 points or under 6 Google results is rough, else close. Foundations or Answerability unmeasured is no score. | owner, 2026-10-15, #1439 |
| Google bar counts what Google card counts | warn | `SearchPresence = 100 × first-page count / measured searches`, over the presence card's SERPs, a local-pack place counting as first page. No measured search means not measured, never 0. The Labs reach no longer enters the score; ranked rows are still bought for competitor bands and winnability. | owner R10, 2026-10-23, #1798 |
| What a mention is | warn | Every Google read keeps the AI Overview's text, up to 4,000 characters. An answer mentions the site where its text names the domain or the brand as a whole word, a cited address on another site has the brand as a path segment, or a cited host carries the brand. A brand that is a common English word counts only where it is used as a name. One rule decides it wherever an AI answer is read. | owner, 2026-10-14, #1420 |
| The score stays here | warn | The Visibility Score is the free report's one-off grade for strangers; the app does not lead with it (§4). | owner D22, 2026-10-02, #1172 |
| Report fills in as it runs | warn | Named stages with elapsed times stand on the left, and each section appears in place as its stage finishes, a daisyUI `skeleton` holding its place: no full-page wait. While the address is read, the waiting state is boxes in the shape of the first screen. | owner, 2026-09-20, #908; 2026-10-07, #1328 |
| Report order | warn | Under the header (domain, scanned and searched line, "An independent report by ReachKit. {company} has not endorsed it." and what we read you as) comes the headline, then the score with its band and range, *Who gets your buyers* over the AI and Google cards, *Pages you're missing*, the top one or two found fixes open, then the offer. Lists, the remaining fixes, the method and the first page start closed in a `details` that opens with no script; a link to one opens it. Nothing is dropped, blurred or kept for a payer, and a section the scan did not produce says so. | owner, 2026-10-07, #1328; R12, 2026-10-31, #2166; C1, 2026-11-08, #2520 |
| Who gets your buyers | warn | The headline names a competitor ("Buyers are sent to {holder}") only where it was found on at least 2 searches and more than the site, each search counted once across top ten, local pack and AI answer; else "Buyers find you most often", or "Buyers can't find you yet" only at zero with no competitor. Then "You came up {n} times in {m} Google results and AI answers." and "We found {count} pages your site is missing. The first one is free." An unmeasured card gives no title and no count, never 0. | owner R12, 2026-10-31, #2166; architect and seo-master, 2026-10-31, #2192 |
| Report board | warn | The report follows board PubReport (canvas version 36), where these rulings beat it: price "VAT included", no money-back line while the launch gate is closed, a missing page keeps its search under its question (REQ-093 c3), the first page starts closed, and the biggest gap's rule is `--warn-rule`. "We read you as" leads with the kind in the singular, then the industry; where it sells is a MapPin chip, drawn only where a reach was read. | owner, 2026-11-01, #2267 |
| Email me this report | warn | The free page card's one address field is "Email me this report", the same `POST /api/lead`. An accepted address is sent §8's `report` mail at once with the report's link and score, a missing figure written as not measured; one report mail per address per scan, the lead throttle as before. | owner C1, 2026-11-08, #2519 |
| Free first page | warn | The free first page is written once per report by §7's stages from the domain's own passages and stored report, one write call sized to `CAPS.FREE_C`, no fix call. Short of what its type asks it is a Q&A; short of that there is no page. Every lead gets the same stored page; a refused page is recorded once and later leads get the "no page to write" notice. | owner, 2026-09-16, #826; D41, 2026-10-04, #1252 |
| One reading of the business | warn | The profile call also answers up to 4 offerings with shares, the kind (`src/lib/market/kinds.ts`) and the reach. The first offering is the category; a town is kept only for a local reach, and a national or international business is measured in its country. Each other offering with at least 0.2 of the site is a seed while the purse holds it (`affordsSeed`), and searches spread across offerings by share. `src/lib/business/understanding.ts` is the one reading on every surface: the customer's own words, then the paid market, then the scan's profile. | owner, 2026-10-15, #1454 |
| Reading of stored scans | warn | The re-score writes lines, kind and reach into each stored profile that lacks them, read only from what was stored (`src/lib/business/retrofit.ts`); no model, no purchase. What nothing stored decides stays unread. Lines, kind and reach stay in the scan's profile, not in `site_profiles`. | 2026-10-16, #1470; 2026-10-17, #1479 |
| Local searches name the offer | warn | A padded directory name ("a a a …") and a bare "near" are not searches. A business measured in a town is searched by what it offers: a search's head is a word of its category, offering, job or market, or a provider, service or meal word (`src/lib/locale/language/`). | 2026-10-15, #1449 |
| Local is what the site says | warn | A market is local where the pass was measured in a town, the profile read a town, or structured data names an in-person business. An older profile is local only where its offering is sold in person and a map shows on a search; the map is never the reason alone. | 2026-10-16, #1468 |
| Competitors in a local market | warn | A domain is a competitor where at least 2 searches find it or a local pack shows it. Schools, universities, marketplaces and domains whose every top-ten page is about others are sources; a tie at the last place on one search each is left out. | 2026-10-15, #1449 |
| Competitors elsewhere | warn | Outside a local market one search finds a competitor, a software maker's own roundups do not make it a source, a tie goes by Google's own order, and listing hosts (`LISTING_HOSTS`) are no competitor. Another town's map, a page of one place, an explaining search's citations, a namesake search and a directory page are not counted as a competitor. | 2026-10-16, #1465, #1468 |
| Lists are not competitors | warn | A cited address that a top ten of the same pass shows as a list (a "best", dated, "alternatives", "vs", "compared" or "reviews" page) is an article citation. No domain is named in any rule. | 2026-10-17, #1476 |
| Re-score every stored scan | warn | After a deploy that changes scoring, competitors or markets, `scan/rescore` scores every stored scan (free, deep, weekly) from its stored rows with the current code, buying nothing and asking no model. A factor it cannot measure shows no number. The original is kept under `report.previous` with `rescoredAt` and the commit; a second run changes nothing. | owner, 2026-10-14, #1426, #1433 |
| Re-score follows each scoring deploy | warn | A deployment carries its derivation, a fingerprint of the `src/lib` files the re-score reaches by static value imports (`scripts/derivation.mjs`). Finding no run at it, the tick asks one guarded apply, which scores in memory and writes only where no score moves over 15 points, no scored scan loses its number and no fewer scans can be read; else it holds (`rescore-held`) and lists the scans as stale. It reads only the home reads each report needs. A person's apply is unguarded. | 2026-10-17, #1481; owner, 2026-10-26, #2096; 2026-11-01, #2254 |
| No silently stale report | warn | A public report the last run at the deployed derivation lists as reading differently says, in one line under the verdict, when it was read and that the reading has changed since. | 2026-10-17, #1481 |
| Gallery lists every report | warn | Every finished free scan is a card on `/scans` until its owner asks through remove@reachkit.app; a removed or refused site is in neither the gallery nor the landing's strip. The gallery is indexed; its reports are not. | owner, 2026-09-24, #1058, #1059 |
| Logo route | warn | `/scans/logo/{domain}` serves the stored logo of a domain whose report page is served or whose scan is running, cached a day; a removed or never-scanned domain gets 404. | owner, 2026-11-01, #2267; 2026-11-02, #2269 |
| Gallery board | warn | `/scans` follows board PubScans. Its filter is *All* and the six kinds (`?kind=`), read by `kindOfProfile`; a category that decides no kind is under *All* only. A card reads "{score} of 100 {band}" or "Not scored", never 0. The toolbar is two rows on every width. At 390 the cards come first and the chips scroll on one row; from `sm` the filter wraps under its label. The strip keeps the context series colour, and "Across United States" is the where chip. | owner, 2026-10-10, #1377; 2026-10-23, #1732; 2026-11-02, #2269 |
| Scanning board | warn | The scan in progress follows board PubScanning: the site's mark asked on each stage, "What we read" leading with kind · industry, the report's lasting address with "Copy link", and on a phone each stage's status under its words so the list never jumps. | owner, 2026-11-02, #2269 |

**Done when** A stranger gets a scored report from `/` with no account; the same URL is the same report a day later; a production free scan is ≤ 12¢ and ≤ 50 s; an unreadable site says so and shows no score.

## §3 Pay and sign-in

**User gets** Stripe Checkout at €49/month tax-inclusive, no account beforehand, asking only for card details and email. After payment the browser lands on setup, signed in. Later sign-in is email only, and a valid link opens straight into the app. Card, invoices, cancel in Stripe’s portal from Settings.

| name | block/warn | outcome protected | ruling |
|---|---|---|---|
| One live Price | warn | There is one live Stripe Price, €49 a month tax-inclusive. The build Vercel runs (`npm run build`, its `prebuild` step `scripts/check-price.mjs`) reads the Price behind `STRIPE_PRICE_ID` and fails on one that differs from `PRICE_OBJECT_SPEC` or does not exist, naming the field or the key's mode, never the key or the price id. A crossed deployment never reaches READY. A vendor that could not be read is logged `unchecked` and the build goes on. Boot makes no Stripe call; it still refuses a binding that does not parse, a frozen clock and missing jobs bindings. | SPEC; owner R-A, 2026-11-07, #2485 |
| Checkout asks for card and email | warn | Checkout requires the card details and the email, and nothing else: the billing address only where Stripe needs it (`billing_address_collection: "auto"`), no tax ID field, no terms or business tick (`consent_collection` is not sent), no phone and no custom field. The billing country is recorded where Stripe gives one and is null otherwise; the account opens either way. | owner, 2026-11-07, #2462; 2026-11-08, #2526 |
| Account from the payment | warn | The payment webhook creates the account, never a signup form. Buying with no report still completes, and the site address is asked afterwards. A second payment from the same address buys no second subscription and says so. | SPEC |
| Checkout lands signed in | warn | Stripe's success URL is this deployment's `/auth/checkout`, which signs the payer in and sends them to `/setup`. Cancel returns to the page they started from. Never a host this process does not name. | SPEC, 2026-09-14 |
| Magic link only | warn | No password and no social sign-in. A link is single use and lasts 24 h (`SIGNIN_LINK_TTL_H`). | SPEC, issue 718 |
| Same link for 15 minutes | warn | A sign-in link asked for again within 15 minutes is the link already live, so a flood of requests mails the customer a link that still works. It is stored sealed under its own key and cleared when used or when the address changes. Once spent, or after 15 minutes, a request mints a new link and the old one stops working. Email-change links mint on every request. | owner default O2, 2026-10-31, #2098 |
| One answer for every address | warn | Sign-in copy is identical whatever the address and reveals nothing about who has an account: "If that address has an account, a sign-in link is on its way." | SPEC, issue 718; 2026-11-01, #2268 |
| Link opens the app | warn | A valid, unused sign-in or email-change link takes its holder straight in, with no confirm page. `/auth/confirm` renders nothing, and a `HEAD` answers `204`. A `GET` spends the link only on a person's page load: `Sec-Fetch-Mode: navigate` and `Sec-Fetch-Dest: document`, with `Sec-Fetch-User: ?1` or a valid `__Host-rk_signin_pending` cookie for the link's type. It lands on the link's allowed landing, else `/setup` on a first sign-in, else `/app`. Any other `GET` goes to `/signin` with the `link=open` marker (`signin.link_open`) before the token is looked up. Each open is logged with its signal (`fetch-user`, `pending` or `none`); the design is reconsidered only if dead links opened with `pending` pass 2% of person-opened links over 30 days. | owner C5, 2026-11-08, #2522 |
| Sign-in board | warn | Sign-in is one centred card per state, boards AuthSignIn, AuthCheckEmail and AuthExpired of canvas v36, built as drawn, with the landing footer. "New to ReachKit? Start a free scan →" is not on the screen. The dead-link line is "Links work for 24 hours, and only the newest one works. Nothing in your account changed.", the same whatever the reason. "Works until" is the send time plus `SIGNIN_LINK_TTL_H`, on the reader's clock. | owner, 2026-11-01, #2268 |
| Session read locally on a page load | warn | A page or layout render reads who is signed in from the session token's signature (`getClaims()`), once per request, with no Supabase network call; the account row still decides, so a tombstoned or unreadable account is no session. Every Server Function and Route Handler that writes keeps `getUser()` and refuses an ended session at once. Speed Insights stays off. | owner R-C, R-E, 2026-11-07, #2486 |
| Server-gated, global sign-out | warn | Account routes are server-gated. Sign-out is global: it ends every session the account holds. | SPEC |
| Billing numbers from Stripe | warn | Plan and price are ours; every other billing number is read from Stripe. Settings shows €49 and links to Stripe's portal for card, invoices and cancel. | SPEC |
| Paid means no outstanding invoice | warn | A site is paid for when its owner's `paid_through` is after now and the recorded `plan_status` is not `past_due`; Stripe's `past_due`, `unpaid`, `incomplete` and `incomplete_expired` are all recorded as `past_due`. While unpaid, the weekly scan, the daily draft and publishing stop for that site, and resume on the next tick after `invoice.paid` records `active`. Hosted pages keep serving to `paid_through` plus 30 days. An unreadable account is not paid. No retry, dunning or refund is added: Stripe's own retries and mails recover the payment. | owner C4, 2026-11-08, #2525 |
| Cancel keeps the paid month | warn | Cancel names the exact date access ends. A cancel at any time, the first 14 days included, ends nothing early: access runs to `paid_through`. A cancelled customer owes nothing. The export stays with no end date. | SPEC; owner, 2026-11-06, #2448 |
| Nothing refunded by itself | warn | The webhook calls `refunds.create` on no event; a paid month or first renewal is never refunded. Settings › Billing states no refund date, and no mail says a refund was made or promised. The rows already in `refunds` and `renewal_refunds` stay as history, and no migration drops either table. A refund the law requires is made by hand (`docs/walk/refund.md`). | owner, 2026-11-06, #2448 |
| Businesses only, month to month | warn | Terms §1: ReachKit sells only to businesses. Terms §4: cancel any time, access runs to the end of the paid month, and payments already made are not refunded unless the law requires it. There is no consumer withdrawal section, and §9 ends access at the end of the paid month. The public cancel answer names no refund and no digit: "Cancel any time from Settings, and you’re not charged again. Section four of our Terms says when your access ends." | owner R22, 2026-10-23, #1795; owner, 2026-11-06, #1590 |
| Delete | warn | Delete tombstones the account at once and purges it within 30 days. The danger zone unlocks only after the customer types the words on the screen. | SPEC, 2026-08-31; 2026-09-07 |

**Done when** A Stripe test-mode payment on this deployment creates the account and lands the payer on `/setup`, signed in, with no form before pay. No password control exists. Settings shows €49 and links to Stripe’s portal. Cancel names the end date.

## §4 Dashboard

**User gets** `/app` after sign-in. Nav: Overview, This week, Settings only. Overview opens with today's page, then how far the live pages have got, then this week and at most two “needs you” items. Google, AI and Competitors open with their numbers.

| name | block/warn | outcome protected | ruling |
|---|---|---|---|
| Three menu items | warn | The menu is **Overview · This week · Settings**. This week is the calendar's name everywhere: the nav label, the browser title ("This week · ReachKit"), the page's back link ("Back to This week") and every line that sends the customer there. Its eyebrow is "Week {n}", and the nav word while the week loads. The route stays `/app/calendar`. Billing keeps "plan" for the subscription. | SPEC, 2026-09-20, #908; owner R8, 2026-10-31, #2169 |
| Overview's four tabs | warn | Overview has four tabs, each its own address: Overview (`/app`), Google (`/app/search`), AI (`/app/ai-answers`) and Competitors (`/app/rivals`). Site fixes (`/app/issues`) and Costs (`/app/costs`) are screens of their own. | SPEC, 2026-09-20, #908; owner, 2026-10-15, #1444; O6, 2026-10-31, #2170 |
| Stored rows only | warn | Every screen reads stored Monday measurements and draft and publication rows. Nothing is recomputed or bought for the screen. A trend is 12 trailing weeks. | SPEC |
| No goals | warn | A figure shows its change against the reading before, never a fixed goal. "AI answers 6/12", "score 50" and "pages 30" are retired with `GOAL_VALUES`. | Numbers first | warn | Google, AI, Competitors, This week and a page's screen open with a short row of numbers the product already stores; what explains them and the tables follow. The five retired tab addresses answer a 308 to their new tab and anchor in `next.config.ts`. On a phone the tabs are 44px boxes, and the bar sticks under the top bar. | Your business | warn | Built to board SettingsIndex: "What we read from your site" and its three folds, the market conclusion's kind of business, and "Last read {date} · {n} pages". The paused lead is "Paused: no page goes live until you turn publishing on. Your pages are kept." | Account tab | warn | Built to boards SettingsBilling and SettingsAccount. *This month* gives the period and four figures. The Email row is the address with *Change email*, the invoices note under it and a pending change below it. Tailwind scans only `src/app` and `src/ui`, and daisyUI is given the components in use. The draft editor loads only when its edit arm opens; Overview's charts are not lazy-loaded, and `/app` and the draft view each hold a script budget. | owner, 2026-11-08, #2487 |

**Done when** Sign-in lands on `/app` with three nav items. No session on `/app` goes to `/signin`. A paid account before its first Monday shows no invented score or rivals.

## §5 Onboarding

**User gets** Three saved steps after payment, Site · Details · Connect: where the buyers are, the rivals and market category ReachKit identified, each with its reason, which the founder may override but never has to, with the twelve questions read-only, and the one DNS record for the hosted address on a subdomain label they choose. Brand voice shown and editable. Then straight into the app, where the deep pass and the first page arrive in the background.

| name | block/warn | outcome protected | ruling |
|---|---|---|---|
| Three saved steps | warn | Setup is three saved steps, **Site · Details · Connect**, built to the canvas v36 boards Setup1, Setup2, Setup and SetupZone. Every step has a white header band with the brand, the three steps drawn as bars over their one-word labels, and the pass's status line while one runs. The question is one reading column with no side panel. The action sits in a bar pinned to the foot of the screen, Back on the left from step 2 on, beside the step's one primary. Each step saves as it is left, so a reload resumes, and a resume point stored by the old five-step build reopens on the step that now holds it. Nothing a founder could set before is lost. | owner Q5, 2026-10-20, #1737; O6, 2026-10-31, #2171; owner, 2026-11-02, #2270 |
| Site | warn | Site asks the address, "Where your buyers are" (the country and the language they search in) and "City or area you serve", each filled with what was read of the site. "About 10 minutes, once" shows on this step only. The site's mark (`SiteMark`, 36px) stands beside its domain: the logo the scan kept, else the domain's first letter. | owner, 2026-11-02, #2270 |
| Country and language asked once | warn | The founder's answer to where the buyers are, and in what language they search, is what is stored and what every pass then measures in. A new address fills it again from that address. | owner D66, 2026-10-07, #1311 |
| City or area stands | warn | The city or area is empty for a business that sells across the country or online. The founder's answer is stored and stands: no pass replaces it. A field that was empty and never touched is no answer, and the first pass then reads the site. A new address fills it again from that address. | owner D67, 2026-10-08, #1318 |
| Details | warn | Details has one line, "{domain} · {n} pages read", with the site's mark; before the crawl has read a page it says so in place of the figure. Its four sections open only to change something and save to the same rows as before: *Your business* (what you do, the lists, the facts we can quote), *Your market and competitors* (the market, its twelve questions read-only, and the rivals, at most five), *Your voice* (the voice text and what we never say) and *Five things only you know*. "We never invent prices, people or reviews…" stays. Leaving Details saves them together and starts the deep pass, because Details is where the rivals are confirmed. While a pass runs, Details names when it started on the founder's clock. | owner Q5, 2026-10-20, #1737; O6, 2026-10-31, #2171; owner, 2026-11-02, #2270 |
| Connect is the third step | warn | Connect is one screen: the status line with its start ("Writing your first page · started 10:42 AM"), the record to paste, whose Name row is the subdomain label field, "Do this later" and the week after. Its primary "I've added it, check now" saves the step and then checks; "Do this later" saves it and goes into the app, and reads "We'll email you a reminder. Your first page waits for it." The fourth tick is "Cancel or export in Settings at any time." The week card's line is "Each page goes live on its day, by itself.", with no hour, because the address is not connected yet. The screen draws no "How it connects" and no "After you add it". `/setup/connect` stays the way back to the record from Overview and Settings until the address is live, and draws no picker, no Back and no status line. | owner, 2026-11-02, #2270; O6, 2026-10-31, #2171; Q11, 2026-10-20, #1595 |
| DNS only at launch | warn | The CNAME is the only connect path a site without a WordPress destination is offered, and the boards draw that wording. WordPress waits behind `WORDPRESS_CONNECT` in `src/lib/config/constants/publish.ts`, off, until it is proven on a real site (#324). A site that already has a WordPress destination keeps it unchanged. With the flag on, Connect asks "Where should pages live?" as daisyUI radios, WordPress replaces the record with "You'll connect it in Settings › Pages, with your site address, username and an app password." and the application-password help, and its primary is Continue. Switching WordPress on is an owner decision that restores the WordPress drawings. | owner, 2026-11-06, #2450; 2026-11-02, #2270 |
| White-label hosted address | warn | The hosted address is `<label>.<customer-domain>` after one CNAME. Saving adds the hostname through the Vercel Domains API. Settings says "live" or "waiting for DNS". Those pages carry the customer's own mark and no ReachKit mark (§7). | SPEC, 2026-09-12; 2026-09-29 |
| Live only where the customer chose | warn | The first page goes live to the location the customer configured. There is no ReachKit fallback address. | owner C3, 2026-11-08, #2524 |
| Pages wait for the destination | warn | The first page and the later drafts are written and wait while the destination is still pending: hosted DNS not yet resolving, or WordPress not yet connected. Publishing waits for a destination that works. | owner, 2026-09-16, #777 |
| WordPress credentials | warn | A WordPress destination takes the site, the username and an application password. The password is never echoed, and a refusal is the destination's health state, never a vendor sentence. | SPEC, 2026-09-12 |
| The record stays shown | warn | Settings shows the hosted CNAME record (name, type, value) for as long as the host waits for DNS, the same record setup showed. A calendar date held by a setting names the one setting that holds it, and a host waiting for its CNAME points at that record. | owner, 2026-09-16, #754 |
| The first page's mail carries the record | warn | For an address that is not connected, the first page's mail carries the record's type, name and value, so board OverviewDNS's line about the setting being in that mail is true. Until the mail part lands, `overview.connect.later` says what the mail does today. | owner R20, 2026-10-22, #1811 |
| The CNAME check | warn | The check is keyed on `<label>.<the site's own stored address>`, derived on the server and never a host the browser names. It attaches the hostname to the project idempotently and reports what the vendor says. Connect and Settings offer it beside the record while the host waits for DNS. | owner, 2026-09-16, #757 |
| Three answers to a check | warn | A check answers live, waiting for DNS, or could not ask (no token bound, or the vendor did not answer), never the second in place of the third. It asks at once, at most once per `DESTINATION_HOSTNAME_CHECK_FLOOR_S` per site; a press inside the floor counts down to zero with the button disabled, then enables it, and shows no answer as new. A deployment with no verification bound answers *not configured* on its own line, which asks nothing, spends no floor and does not say try again. An address changed but not submitted is not checked, and a label checked and not submitted stays on the project's domain list. The same block serves `/setup` and Settings. | owner, 2026-09-16, #757; 2026-09-17, #840 |
| Where the record is added | warn | An NS lookup on the zone of the site's own stored address (no vendor API, no credential, bounded, never blocking the record) names the provider where the nameservers are on a short closed list, names the nameserver where they are not, and falls back to the generic line where nothing answers. Every DNS line says "where you bought your web address", and the record name keeps "CNAME". | owner, 2026-09-16, #760; O6, 2026-10-31, #2171 |
| DNS guidance a founder can follow | warn | The record block on `/setup` and in Settings is one guide chosen from the NS lookup: Cloudflare, GoDaddy, Namecheap, Squarespace (the `googledomains.com` nameservers), Route 53, Vercel, else generic. It gives numbered steps, the record as a table in that provider's field labels with a copy button per value, a link to the provider's DNS page where one exists, and Name as exactly what that field takes (the host less its zone). Cloudflare alone gets one proxy instruction, DNS only, the grey cloud, with its reason in one line. Check connection asks public DNS first, with no vendor credential: found and pointing here, found and pointing elsewhere (the target shown; a proxy's address counts as elsewhere), or no record yet; a resolver that did not answer says so and is never "not yet". The Vercel domain verification stays the second step, in words that follow what DNS confirmed. Where the vendor says live, only the live line shows. | owner, 2026-09-17, #856 |
| Straight into the app | warn | Finishing setup releases the founder straight into `/app`. The deep pass is a background job; while it runs the app names its current step, live at least every 30 s, with no percentage and no promised duration, and refreshes itself when the pass and the first draft finish. The waiting strip's last phase is "First page live". A lost enqueue is retried, never silently dropped. | owner, 2026-09-16, #777, #782; O6, 2026-10-31, #2171 |
| The step named is the step written | warn | The step the side panel names is the stage the pass wrote. `checking_your_presence` is the only stage that sizes a rival, so it alone is drawn on *Sizing your rivals*; `asking_the_twelve` is drawn on *Finding pages worth writing* beside `scoring`. | owner, 2026-09-19, #903 |
| No promise of ReachKit's time | warn | No setup string states how long the scan, the pass or the first page will take. A step the founder does themselves may state its own time. | owner, 2026-09-21, #972 |
| Time-zone screen | warn | SetupZone is the heading and one status card with the spinner: "So every date shows in your time. A few seconds, once, with nothing to press." It is the one place a spinner stands outside a pressed button, because the screen itself is the wait (DESIGN › Busy and done). | owner, 2026-11-02, #2270 |
| The skeleton is the step that arrives | warn | While `/setup` waits it draws the step's own regions, on a phone and on a desktop: the step indicator, the question, the panel and the bar at the foot. They do not change shape when the step arrives. | owner, 2026-10-07, #1327 |
| The first page never pads | warn | The first draft is written from the deep pass's best ready opportunity, never from a filler. The pass uses the thin-market steps in §6, so a new or small site still gets one. A pass that still ends with no ready opportunity writes no first draft: it records *market too small*, and the app tells the founder why in its place. | owner, 2026-09-16, #777 |
| The first week up to the first Monday | warn | Setup writes the first page. Each date after it, up to the day before the first Monday weekly pass, gets a committed topic and shape, and is written the evening before its date. That Monday's pass commits a fresh week. | owner C2, 2026-11-08, #2521 |
| One claimed deep row | warn | The deep pass's `scans` row is claimed when setup accepts the founder's address. A stated market's suggested rivals (`competitors_domain`) are spent against that row, and the deep pass adopts it rather than inserting a second. A free upgrade's suggestions are its report's own rivals, bought for nothing. | SPEC, 2026-09-16 |
| A stopped or failed first pass is measured again | warn | The maintenance tick that re-sends a lost onboarding pass re-measures, as a re-measure under its bound, a site whose newest deep pass ended on a ceiling (`time_ceiling`, `spend_ceiling`) within 24 h of setup. It also re-measures, once, a site whose only ended deep pass is its first and ended `failed`, within `TIMING.deepPassBackstopH` of setup, under `REMEASURE.perDay` and `DAILY_SITE_C`, keyed on the failed pass (`scans_one_remeasure_per_pass`); a second failure, a weekly pass or a later pass is not re-run. Until a pass finishes, the side panel, Overview and the Calendar's supply line say the market is still being measured, never *market too small* and never a broader category. | owner, 2026-09-17, #855; 2026-11-06, #2430 |
| ReachKit identifies the market and rivals | warn | The market category and the rivals are concluded by ReachKit from evidence as soon as the founder's address is known, from what the scans have read of the site, spent against the claimed deep row, and re-checked each pass (§6). They arrive already chosen, each with its reason, so leaving the step as it is accepts them; setup asks no one to confirm them. An answer that differs is the founder's override. Settings states the same reasons, and says when the founder's own category or rivals stand instead. | owner D28, 2026-10-02, #1186, #1185 |
| Keeping ReachKit's rivals is not an override | warn | Only adding or removing a rival is the founder's choice. The saved rivals are measured against the rivals the step showed, not against the full concluded list, and a set put back to the concluded one is ReachKit's again; a later pass may re-conclude it (§6). No site's override mark is cleared by hand without the owner's approval. | owner D55, 2026-10-06, #1281 |
| Rivals are real competitors | warn | A site's rivals are only 3–5 real competitors. A publisher, aggregator or platform found in a top ten is a source and is never offered as a rival. | owner D24, 2026-10-02, #1161 |
| Setup always offers rivals | warn | The rivals the site's own pages name are offered first, for nothing, on either market. An inferred market whose report found no rivals falls back to `competitors_domain` on the founder's own domain, spent against the claimed row under `DEEP`, bounded by `TIMING.suggestCeilingS` and refused by the kill switch. The card opens seeking, and only a vendor that answers none, fails or is refused settles on "none found". | owner, 2026-09-17, #838 |
| An override is refused in one line | warn | An override that adds a sixth rival is refused with the limit named. A rival the founder adds that is not a domain, is unreachable, is their own domain or is a duplicate is refused in one line, and so is an override that empties the market. | SPEC; owner D28, 2026-10-02, #1186 |
| The broader-market chooser is held | warn | The chooser (`setup.remeasure.suggested`, `setup.remeasure.own`, `setup.remeasure.refused.empty`) and `setup.release.market-too-small` stay until the product picks the broader market itself, because the chooser is still the only way a thin market reaches a page. | O6, 2026-10-31, #2171 |
| Your voice | warn | Voice is paid-only, built at setup and editable there and in Settings; the inventory and the site name are shown as read. A site that reads gets a voice: the paid pass's voice call has room for a whole answer, and the deep pass seeds the founder's voice from it unless they wrote their own. Where no voice has been read yet, the box is empty and editable under a line inviting the founder to describe it, never a blank section. | SPEC, 2026-09-12; owner, 2026-09-17, #839 |
| The profile crawl is polite and bounded | warn | The profile crawl honours `robots.txt` per path and its `Crawl-delay` (capped at 1 s), holds at most 8 MB in total, reads up to 100 pages for the inventory and the checks of §9, and aborts reads still in flight at its time budget. | SPEC, 2026-09-12; 2026-10-07, #1314 |
| The knowledge base | warn | Details' *Your business* is the knowledge base (§0): what the site does, sells and promises, who it serves, the words it uses, and the facts that may be quoted, each with the page it was read from (`site_profiles.knowledge`). Every item can be removed and the founder can add their own; it is what pages are written from. A sentence is kept only where it is prose, and navigation, buttons and footers are never facts. Beside it the founder is asked five optional questions, one field each: who buys from you and why; what you do that rivals don't; what buyers ask before they buy; a result a customer got, in numbers if you have them; your name and one line about you, for the byline. Each answer is a fact with the founder as its source, stored with the site (`site_profile_edits.answers`, at most 600 characters) and leading the list it joins: audience, differentiators, questions, proof and the byline. Settings › Your business shows what was read, each item with its page, and asks the same five questions. | owner, 2026-09-29, #1127, D8; 2026-10-03, #1133 |
| The knowledge read is bounded | warn | One pass reads at most 20 pages, each in one small-tier call on at most 8,000 characters of the page's cleaned text, and spends at most 15¢ on them: a page is asked about only while the bound still holds the most its call can cost, calls in flight counted. The order, which the crawl also follows: the home page; each section's own page (what it costs, what it sells, who it is, what buyers ask and where it is, whom it has served, reviews, docs, changelog) in English, German, French and Spanish path words; the pages under those sections; the rest; legal, account and tag pages last. A page whose text has not changed keeps its stored reading and costs nothing. An item carrying a number the page does not carry is dropped. The writer's `profile` carries at most 6,000 characters of the base, giving up places, integrations, questions, problems, audience, proof and differentiators in that order. | 2026-10-03, #1133; 2026-10-07, #1314 |
| The weekly pass keeps it current | warn | Each weekly pass reads the knowledge base again from the pages its own crawl just read, asking only about a changed or new page. The read runs after the topic typing and the monthly competitor re-check, spends what they leave of the weekly 40¢ cap and never the reserve held for the typing, and stops at the cap or its own 15¢. A changed page it did not reach is left out of the base until a later week reads it. | owner, 2026-10-25, #1560 |
| What the knowledge base never holds | warn | A legal page (privacy, terms, imprint, cookies, by its address) and a gallery page (a path of `scans`, `gallery`, `showcase`, `directory` or `listings`, or anything under `scan/`, with or without a sort or filter) are never read for facts, and the crawl reads a gallery address last. The site's demo phrases are stored with its base (`site_profiles.knowledge.demoPhrases`, at most 500, newest first), each read adds to them, and a listed phrase is dropped wherever the site says it and is never quoted. An element marked as an example or a demo, a short caption that says it is one ("An example business", "Illustrative figures") with what it captions, and a line naming a business at a reserved example domain are dropped; `html`, `body`, `main` and an element holding nine tenths or more of what the page says are never dropped as an example, whatever their classes say. A passage is chrome only where it repeats on three or more of the site's pages and on more than half of them. | 2026-10-03, #1163; 2026-10-04, #1249; 2026-10-07, #1314 |
| Buyers' questions and audiences | warn | A heading, a navigation label or a call to action is never a buyer question; a question is kept where it ends in a question mark and has at least three words, and the same words as a line without the mark are a heading. A page's own markup questions are kept with no model call: each `Question` of its `FAQPage` or `QAPage` schema, a `summary`, a `dt` or an accordion button phrased as a question, a heading that asks one and opens with a word that asks, and a short question inside a section the page marks as its FAQ, never in a `nav` or `footer`, at most 20 a page, its own before the model's. An audience names a kind of customer: it is kept where a word is left once the words for anybody, in seven languages, and the words that add no kind are taken away. Both checks run again when the base is put together. The founder's own answers are never checked and come first. | owner D65, 2026-10-07, #1303; 2026-10-07, #1314 |
| An empty knowledge base is said | warn | A NUL is taken out of every document as it is read and every payload as it is ledgered. A page whose row cannot be written is left out and logged as `site_page_not_stored` with the reason, and the crawl keeps the rest. A pass that stored no site profile buys no knowledge read, and a pass whose base holds no page logs `site_knowledge_failed` with the reason and its counts. The D58 check `knowledge_base` fails where the base holds no page, on any pass; a count that could not be read fails nothing. The customer panel counts the questions of the pages the base reads, fails only where they ask some and the base holds none, and prints the pass's own reason for anything not measured. | 2026-10-07, #1314; owner, 2026-10-12, #1414 |
| Never a fact off our own page | warn | Facts come only from the customer's own pages and edits.
