A page is ReachKit's where a publication of a site of that domain was delivered at its address, live or since taken down, however it is spelled, or where it is on the host a live hosted destination serves. The rule holds for the grounding passages, the knowledge base, the claims, products and voice the paid pass reads (`site_profiles.claims`) and the customer's own `known_content` rows; a page of ours is a `reachkit` row only. A measurement, a comparison and the link map still read our pages. Where which pages are ours cannot be read, the reader reads nothing and what was stored stands. | owner, 2026-10-15, #1456 |

**Done when** Finishing setup reaches `/app` at once; the first draft follows in the background, or the founder is told the market was too small. A sixth competitor is refused. The CNAME for the chosen label is shown, and the destination reads waiting, then live. A voice edit persists in Settings. A wrong WordPress password shows destination health, no vendor text.

## §6 Weekly scan

**User gets** Every local Monday: new score, AI answers, Google positions, rival movement, a verdict on every published page, the next three things, or an honest empty hand.

**Rules**
- Hourly tick; fires 06:00 on each site’s local Monday — not “Monday UTC”.
- Cluster by parent topic. One cluster-day, at most one Write per cluster. Improve of an owned URL outranks a new page (2026-09-11).
- A day is filled only by an opportunity that passes readiness. Never pad the calendar (2026-09-11).
- “Not working” on a cluster: no new Write there for four weeks; Improve of the live URL stays allowed.
- Cap 40¢ per site per week; degrade with a stated reason; money spent is always ledgered.
- A week that measured nothing says so. Missing values omit the row, never repeat last week.
- Residual keyword pages need every extra gate, not volume ≥ 10/mo alone. Format pages: comparison / alternative / integration / template only (2026-09-10).
- 2026-09-15  Readiness (owner): a `keyword_page` is ready only when volume ≥ min, the band is `winnable`, business fit is at least 2 (2026-09-29), and no owned URL ranks for it (else Improve); otherwise `keyword_gate`. Parent topic is a mechanical `clusterKey()` of the query’s content words (lower-cased; stop words, brand tokens and trailing `s` removed; sorted and joined) — Improve uses the same key. A grounding fact is at least one passage from the site’s own measured page text; if none, every Write/Earn/Improve row is `no_grounding_fact` and supply is 0. Write order among types: `answer_page` > `comparison_page` > `format_page` > `keyword_page`. A `format_page` is ready only when its query contains comparison / vs / alternative / integration / template.
- 2026-09-15  Earn readiness (owner): a `listed_page` is ready only when the site’s own pages hold a passage of its asset’s kind — `comparison_table`: from a pricing, features or product page; `integration_page`: from a page whose URL, title or h1 matches the integration words that chose the asset; `original_data_page`: a passage carrying a numeral. Otherwise `no_grounding_fact`.
- 2026-09-16  Right-sizing law (owner, #777): a target is offered only if it is winnable *for this site’s presence*. The bar scales with the site’s own footprint and its rivals’ (§0): a site that ranks for little gets small, long-tail and AI-answer targets and never head terms; larger searches are admitted only as its own presence grows. This applies to the free report’s twelve questions, the deep pass and every weekly pass.
- 2026-09-16  Thin markets (owner defaults, #777; the owner may override on review). A pass that is short of twelve questions reads the market this way, stopping as soon as twelve survive:
  - Seeds, in order: the site's market category (§0) → a 2–3 word head term derived from it → the site profile’s vocabulary. At most 3 extra suggestion purchases per pass, inside that pass’s own cap.
  - 2026-09-17  Head term (#836): derived from the category’s noun phrase, not only the words before its first connective. Where fewer than two words come before the connective (“SEO and content marketing software”), the head term is the category’s last 3, then 2, content words (“content marketing software”). It is never the category itself and never one word.
  - Candidates are pooled from suggestions, the rivals’ ranked keywords and the site’s own ranked keywords, de-duplicated before selection. Ranked keywords already bought for sizing are reused, not bought again.
  - Volume floor steps 50 → 20 → 10 /mo, only as far as needed to reach twelve questions. Each question records the step it came from, and that step is the “min” readiness checks it against.
  - A paid pass (deep or weekly) ends with at least one ready opportunity, or records *market too small* and tells the founder why. Too few questions is never a silent empty calendar.
  - 2026-09-17  (#835, owner) The free scan uses this ladder too, stopping as soon as it has questions rather than twelve — four of them since 2026-10-04 (#1235, §2) — inside its own 12¢ cap (§2).
- 2026-09-17  Cold-start right-sizing (owner walk, #858; drafted defaults, the owner may correct the numbers). Volume alone is not a difficulty signal: the vendor's keyword difficulty (`keyword_properties.keyword_difficulty`, 0–100) is read on every suggestion (and on a ranked row where the vendor carries it) at no extra cost, and suggestions are bought against it. A search is offered only at or under the site's difficulty ceiling, `30 + 10 × log10(1 + own ranked)` (Winnable at or under `15 + 10 × log10(1 + own ranked)`), at most 100; a row the vendor gives no difficulty is read on volume. The demand ceiling's cold-start floors are 300/mo (Reach) and 100/mo (Winnable), were 1000 and 200; the multiples stay 10× and 2×. While that ceiling is at its floor, a search of 3+ content words scores 2× its intent score, so specific long-tail searches carrying the profile's words lead the twelve. The ladder steps volume down; it never raises either ceiling.
- 2026-09-17  Rivals are right-sized (owner walk, #858). A rival banded far is never presented as the site's rival: a paid pass keeps a domain it holds a far band for out of the report's rivals and lists near before middle before unsized; setup offers `competitors_domain`'s competitors banded by the counts that call returns (near, then middle, then unsized; never far — all far is "none found"); Overview draws the reachable tracked rivals as rows and names the far ones on one secondary line as market leaders, with the one control to change who the site is measured against, and says first when none is within reach. Every tracked rival is still measured. The free report buys no rival size (§6.4 never-list and its 12¢ cap, owner 2026-09-17), so its rivals carry no band and no reach claim.
- 2026-09-19  A pass sizes the rivals of the questions it asked (owner, #901, from the live re-measure of reachkit.app). The candidates are the domains its **own twelve top tens** hold, ranked by how often they appear and how well they rank; a paid pass sizes them inside its existing spend and time budgets — reusing anything already bought, including everything inside the rival cache window — bands them against the site, and lets them feed both the report's rivals and derivation's winnability. A rival sized for a question the pass no longer asks is not evidence about this market. Where the budget cannot cover every candidate the pass sizes the most promising and records the rest unsized in the report; it never silently reuses an unrelated set. Everything above stands: the free tier buys no rival sizing (§6.4 and its 12¢ cap), the `far` exclusion, the reachable-first ordering, the cache windows, and every tracked rival is still measured and is still what Overview draws. Drafted defaults, the owner may correct them: at most 12 candidates a pass (one per question it may ask, 28.8¢ at `RANKED_RIVAL_ROWS` on a first pass and free inside the 30-day rival window after), and 4¢ held back for the opportunity typing that follows the pass. No cap is raised.
- 2026-09-18  A vendor call is abandoned after **10 s** on every tier, and an answer that arrives late is kept (#875, #877). (Since 2026-10-07 a paid pass's SERP read waits 30 s: D63, below.) The 5 s free-path bound #875 shipped on the owner's ruling of 2026-09-17 was reverted the same day (owner, #877): the first free scan after it — `linear.app` — aborted 5 of its 7 SERP calls and was billed for all of them, against 13% aborted before the change, so these calls simply take longer than five seconds. The bound stays per call rather than per module, so a tier can be given its own figure once there is data to choose one; the durations of both answered and abandoned calls are recorded on the ledger row (#877), and the next value is an owner ruling with the p50 and p95 in front of it. #865's one retry after the sweep, and the rule that an answer arriving after its stage was abandoned is recorded while the report has not yet been composed — it was bought and paid for — and never after, both stand. No cap changes.
- 2026-10-02  **Market and rivals re-checked each pass** (owner, D28, #1186, #1185). Each pass re-checks the site's market category and rivals against the evidence it reads and keeps them current; a founder's override stands until the founder changes it.
- 2026-10-02  **How a pass keeps them current** (#1185, implementing D28; the numbers are drafted defaults the owner may correct). *Market*: before a paid pass buys anything for its market it concludes the structured market (who buys, the job, the category with 2–4 synonyms, differentiators, not-our-market) from the site's own pages, headline first, with the last report's answers, top tens and ranked searches as a check — and told which category those were bought under, since they may be the wrong market's. A newly concluded market replaces the standing one only when the pass before concluded the same market (category or synonym), or when the site's own words carry at least half again as much of its name; otherwise it waits as pending. The pass seeds and measures the market that stands after the check, buying the category and each synonym inside the existing three extra seeds (#1159). *Rivals*: once the pass's own answers are in, the rivals are concluded for that market — who its AI answers cite and who holds its top tens, where those sell the market's category, then the market's known competitors — never a publisher, aggregator or platform (D24); a standing rival concluded again keeps its place and new ones fill to five. (Since 2026-10-07 a rival is dropped only after two passes leave it out, and a new one may wait a pass: D65, below.) (Since 2026-10-20 a rival added or dropped starts no re-measure, and a weekly pass re-checks the rivals once a month: #1700.) Both calls run at temperature 0. `ranked_keywords` is bought ordered by estimated traffic (#1161). The offline proof is `docs/walk/market-inference.md`.
- 2026-10-02  **A new market is measured in its own words** (#1195, from the re-measure of reachkit.app of this date; the 8 prompts and the 25 s are the owner's, D29 below). A concluded market's synonyms are the 2–4-word names buyers search for the category, and they join the relevance guard beside the category, so a search the pass bought for the market is not dropped as off-topic. Only rivals concluded for the market in force add their ranked searches to the pool; a rival chosen under an earlier market adds none. A deep pass asks AI Mode up to **8** of the market's buyer prompts beside the twelve, built from templates over the structured market (category, each synonym, the buyer, the job, an alternative to each standing rival), never counted in the score until D19 takes effect; their answers, and the products they name, are evidence for the rivals. A paid pass's asks stop waiting **25 s** before its ceiling (`TIMING.paidPassAskReserveS`), so a slow engine queue costs that engine's cells, never the pass's scoring; a pass its ceiling still stops does not re-conclude rivals. No cap or ceiling moves.
- 2026-10-02  **A re-measure under a new market asks 8 buyer prompts** (owner, D29, #1127, #1199; approved as recommended). The pass that re-measures a site under a new market asks **8** buyer prompts built from the structured market, to find its rivals. They are not counted in the score. A paid pass's asks stop waiting **25 s** before its ceiling. Both numbers are the owner's and no longer drafted defaults.
- 2026-10-02  **Weekly passes ask no buyer prompts** (owner, D30, #1127, #1199). A weekly pass asks none of them until D19's cost is measured (#1162), so it stays inside the 40¢ weekly cap. The cost was measured on 2026-10-03 and D19 below is approved: once it is built, D19 says what a weekly pass asks.
- 2026-10-02  **A pass its ceiling stops never updates rivals** (owner, D31, #1127, #1199). A pass stopped by its time ceiling keeps the rivals it started with.
- 2026-10-02  Proof on production, 22:56 UTC (scan `2415236e`, #1197): the re-measure of reachkit.app ended `complete` in 75 s. Category: "AI discovery monitoring and content". Rivals: AEO Engine, MaxAEO and Meltwater, each with its reason. 2 on-market questions.
- 2026-10-02  **Rivals and sources** (owner, D24, #1161). A pass measures the site's 3–5 rivals (§0). A domain in a question's top ten that is a publisher, aggregator or platform is a source, not a rival. The sizing of the top-ten domains above (#858, #901) is an internal right-sizing input; it does not make a domain a rival, and footprint and rival size are never shown as a score or a lead (D22).
- 2026-10-05  **Market words come from search volume** (owner, D49, #1269; built by #1270): *"research what vocab, terms etc used, what is attractive and what drives geo/seo traffic hollistically"*. The words in a concluded market are checked against search volume. A pass that concludes a market proposes up to about ten on-market search terms buyers would type: the category, its synonyms, and outcome phrasings. It reads their volume in one keyword-overview call for the site's location (about 2¢, inside the pass's cap). The market keeps them as `terms`, plus `searchTerm`, the most-searched on-market term that is not polluted by another meaning. Tracked prompts, the context pack, a Q&A's questions, headings and titles use the searched terms. A label with no volume stays internal and is never printed. A founder's setup answer about audience outranks the inferred buyer. Found on reachkit.app (`docs/walk/titles-and-vocabulary.md`): its category "AI discovery monitoring and content" has no searches, while "AI SEO" has 6,600 a month in the US, "generative engine optimization" 4,400 and "AI visibility tool" 1,600. Its buyer is founders and small business owners, not B2B SaaS.
- 2026-10-06  **How D49 is built** (#1270; drafted defaults the owner may correct). The pass asks one model call (the `sonnet` tier; `haiku` proposed terms nobody searches) for up to ten terms and reads them in one Labs `keyword_overview` call: 1¢ a request and 0.01¢ a keyword, about 1.8¢ with the model call. A brand — the business or a rival — is never a term. A term the model marks as mostly meaning something else ("geo") is kept with its volume and is never the search term. The terms of a market that still stands are read again after 30 days, and at once when a new market is adopted; a read that fails changes nothing and the next pass asks again. Tracked prompts, the pass's buyer prompts, derived topics, the context pack, a Q&A's on-market test and the writer name the market by `searchTerm` (without a closing "tool" or "software") and its other searched terms. Where nothing proposed is searched, the category alone is used and its synonyms are not printed. The category a founder set themselves is never replaced. The questions a pass measures are still seeded from the concluded category. (Since 2026-10-07 they are seeded from the searched terms: D65, below.) Tracked prompts change at their own rotation rate (D33): at most a fifth of the set in 13 weeks, so a prompt built on the old label stays tracked for a while, and a Q&A never takes a stored question that carries a label nobody searches. Proof on stored evidence, 2026-10-06: reachkit.app's `searchTerm` is "generative engine optimization" (4,400 a month), beside "answer engine optimization" (2,400) and "AI search visibility" (390); its prompts read "Which generative engine optimization software is built for founders and business owners?".
- 2026-10-05  **Rivals and comparison targets need search demand** (owner, D50, #1269; built by #1271). Rival choice weighs a candidate's brand search volume alongside the evidence that names it: of the on-market candidates that AI answers or the top tens name, those buyers search for come first. A comparison topic is derived only where at least one named product has search volume. "{business} vs X" is never a target while the business's brand has none: the page is "X alternatives" or "A vs B vs {business}", where A and B are searched. Found on reachkit.app: AEO Engine has 70 searches a month and MaxAEO none, against Profound's 6,600, Peec AI's 2,400 and Otterly's 720. Its queued "ReachKit vs MaxAEO" was dismissed by hand on 2026-10-05.
- 2026-10-06  **How D50 is built** (#1271; drafted defaults the owner may correct). A pass asks for up to ten rival candidates, as before, and reads each one's brand volume with the business's own in one Labs `keyword_overview` call (about 1.2¢). It keeps the volumes for 30 days and buys only names it does not hold, so most weeks buy none. A brand is *searched* at 100 searches a month or more: AEO Engine's 70 is not, and the least-searched rival the owner names has 320. The rival conclusion says what a buyer types for each product and whether the bare name mostly means something else (an ordinary word, or a company best known for another product). Such a name is read only in its qualified form ("profound ai"), and a company best known for a product outside the market is a rival only under its own product name here. Searched candidates are chosen first, most searched first; of two searched alike, one an AI answer or a top ten names comes first. The places left go to the rest in the conclusion's order, a standing rival before a new one. A founder's own rivals stand (D28); their volumes are read, and the set is not changed. A proposed topic is "{rival} alternatives" for each searched rival (amended 2026-10-18 by issue 1520: only on observed demand, and for a business that serves one place not proposed without it). It is "{business} vs {rival}" only where the business's brand is searched; otherwise the three most-searched rivals are set against each other ("profound vs peec ai") and the page's title may add the business as the third name. A rival's price becomes "{business} vs {rival} pricing" only where the business's brand is searched. An open, unmeasured row for a comparison of names nobody searches is dismissed once the volumes are read; where they were never read, none is proposed and none is dismissed. A comparison that names no product ("geo vs seo") is not judged by this rule, and neither is a search the pass measured demand for. Setup's rivals are chosen the same way. Proof on stored evidence, 2026-10-06, with reachkit.app's override set aside: Profound (6,600), Peec AI (2,400) and Otterly (720) are kept ahead of AEO Engine (70) and MaxAEO (0); the topics are "profound alternatives", "peec ai alternatives", "otterly alternatives", "profound vs peec ai", "profound vs otterly" and "peec ai vs otterly", and none is "reachkit vs …". reachkit.app's own rivals are marked as the founder's, so no pass replaces them until that is lifted.
- 2026-10-06  **The rival conclusion runs on `sonnet`** (owner, D56; built by #1282). On the same evidence the `haiku` tier read Meltwater under its bare name — 14,800 searches for PR monitoring — in one run of three (#1278's proof). The second call of a pass's market conclusion, the rivals, is asked of `sonnet`, about 2.6¢ more a pass; the first, the market, stays on `haiku`.
- 2026-10-06  **A bare name its own domain qualifies is read in its qualified form; a dropped product still names a comparison** (#1285, from the first live run of D49–D56, scan `17f5bbff`; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). The rival conclusion did not mark "Brandi" (mybrandi.ai) as a name that mostly means something else, so it was read at 22,200 searches for the first name and chosen first; "brandi ai" has 260. Code no longer rests on the model's mark alone. A one-word name is read only in its qualified forms where its company's own domain holds the name with something added ("mybrandi.ai", "tryprofound.com", "beomniscient.com"), which is what a company registers when the bare word is an ordinary word or a first name. Where the conclusion gave no qualified form, the domain's own label is read ("mybrandi"). The rule applies when the volumes are bought and when the rivals are ordered, so a rival stored before it is corrected on the next pass without a new purchase. The conclusion's instruction now names first names beside dictionary words. An open comparison row is also judged where the product it names was read and not kept as a rival: it is known by the keyword its brand volume was read under, so "aeo engine alternatives" (70 searches) is dismissed although AEO Engine had left the rivals. Proof on stored evidence, 2026-10-06: reachkit.app's rivals are Profound (6,600), Otterly (880), Omniscient (590), Rankscale (480) and Brandi (260), in that order; the owner confirmed the first four the same day.
- 2026-10-06  **Tracked prompts are rebuilt once from the searched terms** (owner, D54, #1281; "D54–D56 approved as recommended"). A site whose stored prompts were built from a label other than the market's `searchTerm` (D49) has its whole set rebuilt once from `searchTerm`, the searched terms and the founder's audience. The rebuild happens on the next pass, and the 4-week mention rate starts again from that date; the restart is recorded so the rate never mixes the two sets. After that, D33 rotation applies as before. This replaces the 2026-10-06 line above where it says a prompt built on the old label stays tracked for a while. Found on reachkit.app: its 25 prompts were built on "AI discovery monitoring and content", and D33 would have kept most of that wording for months.
- 2026-10-06  **How D54 is built** (#1281; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). The stored set keeps the market name it was built from (`builtFrom`); a set stored before that is read by its wording, and counts as built from another label where none of its prompts says the search term. The rebuilt set is unchecked, like a first build (D33), its rotation count starts empty, and `rebuiltAt` is stored with it. *Once* is once per site: a search term that changes later moves the set at the rotation rate only. A market that holds no searched term rebuilds nothing. The set stays at D19's 25: a market with fewer than three other searched names asks its category another way in their place (owner, 2026-10-06), so the category kind is always five. Each week's stored prompts carry the set's `restartedAt`, and the rolling window reads only the weeks that carry the newest week's mark, so Overview, the weekly mail and update-first (D15) read the rebuilt set alone and show fewer than four weeks until it has them.
- 2026-10-06  **Prompts that name rivals follow the rivals; no prompt quotes the job** (#1285, from scan `17f5bbff`; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). A pass concludes its rivals from its own answers, so after the prompt set is built and asked. reachkit.app's rebuilt set therefore named three rivals the same pass dropped (AEO Engine, MaxAEO, Meltwater) in five of its 25 prompts, and rotation would have kept them for months. The comparison and branded prompts are now always the ones the rivals in force yield: they are replaced when a pass has concluded its rivals, and again before a pass asks the set. This is not rotation (D33): it does not count against the fifth in 13 weeks, the newcomers are not pre-checked, a prompt the rivals still yield keeps its date, and the rate does not start again. The pass that concluded the rivals stores its answers to the prompts it asked; the next pass asks the new ones. Because those prompts also name the market, a later change of search term rewrites them at once, and the other 19 still move at the rotation rate only. Use-case and problem prompts no longer quote the market's `job`, which is the conclusion's own sentence and not a buyer's ("understand where AI and search send buyers" stood in ten of reachkit.app's 25). The seven use-case prompts name the market by its searched names in turn, each with one constraint ("Which answer engine optimization tools can I use without hiring an agency?"); the three problem prompts name the search term ("I am getting nowhere with generative engine optimization. Which tools can fix that?"). A site whose set was already rebuilt takes the new wording at the rotation rate. reachkit.app's rebuild is run once more by hand, by clearing its `rebuiltAt`; nothing is bought until the owner measures again.
- 2026-09-16  Never pad (2026-09-11) still holds under these rules: the seeds, pool and volume steps widen which searches are measured; every other readiness gate stands unchanged.
- 2026-09-16  Market too small, told to the owner (#796): a site’s first (deep) pass mails the owner at once; weekly passes are one owner digest per Monday listing their scan ids, sent once that Monday has ended in every zone — never one mail per site per week.
- 2026-10-03  Why pages stall, on the same digest (#1184, part of #1127). The Monday owner digest also lists, per site, the pages of the week before — the seven dates before that Monday — that stopped before review out of the pages written, and how many pages each cause stopped: a writing rule by its name, a step that could not run, a page that adds too little, a delivery. A page several rules stopped counts once for each. Nothing new is stored: the count is read from each page's own record of why it rested (#880). A week with stalled pages and no thin market still sends the digest.
- 2026-10-06  **Spend is recorded and reported** (owner, D57, #1286; "D57–D60 approved as recommended"). Found on 2026-10-06: production spend was already in the `fetches` ledger call by call (about 90¢ that day), while about 70¢ of test spend made from the box was reported only in pull-request text, and a smoke test's scan could not be told from a customer's free scan. Four things follow. (1) **One spend log for testing**: every script on the box that makes a paid call appends one line per call to the box's spend log, `spend.jsonl` (its place is `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §8) — when, the issue or branch (read from the git branch), the script, the source, the cents. (2) **`npm run costs`** prints today's and the last 7 days' test spend from that log, by issue, against D34's €2 a day, and warns at €1.50. (3) **Smoke scans are tagged**: `scripts/smoke.sh` marks its scan and the scan route stores the mark on the scan row. (4) **The Monday owner digest has a cost section**, read from `fetches`, `scans` and `drafts`: per site the cost per measurement and the cost per page written, pages stopped included; the weekly total; free scans and smoke tests as separate lines.
- 2026-10-06  **How D57 is built** (#1286; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *The log.* A call that settled at nothing writes no line. The cents are the ledger's — the US cent the vendors bill in — held against €2 one for one, as the page test's ceiling already is. A day is a UTC day, like the product's own daily ceiling. *The mark.* `"smoke": true` in the scan start's JSON, stored as `scans.smoke`. A visitor could send it too: admission does not read it, so it buys nothing and only moves a scan to the other line of the owner's report. *The cost section.* It reports the week the digest already reports stalled pages for, the seven dates before that Monday. A measurement is a deep or weekly pass created in those seven UTC days and costs its `scans.cost_cents`; a page is a draft dated in them and costs its `drafts.cost_cents`; the site's total is the two together. Free scans and smoke tests are a total and a count each. The week's total is the `fetches` ledger's own sum, product-wide. A week with a scan or a page sends the digest even with no thin market and no stalled page; a cost read that fails leaves the section out and never holds the digest back.
- 2026-10-11  **What the vendors bill is recorded and reconciled** (owner, #1408; "yes go ahead with all five cost fixes"). Found on 2026-10-11: every cent figure recorded was an estimate from our own price list. For that day DataForSEO billed 37.4¢ against 64.4¢ recorded, and over the account's life it billed $69.75 against $13.29 recorded. (1) Every DataForSEO call stores the vendor's own charge beside the estimate: `fetches.billed_cents` in production and `billed` in the box's spend log. It is null where the response carries no charge, never a guess. The price list stays the figure known before a call, so the caps, the reservations and everything that decides whether to call are unchanged. (2) A timer on the box reads DataForSEO's balance, deposits and day total every hour, for free. `npm run costs` prints billed beside recorded for today and each of the last 7 days and warns when they differ by more than 10% or 10¢. (3) With `ANTHROPIC_ADMIN_KEY` on the box the same reading takes Anthropic's cost report and AI spend is shown billed against recorded; without it the command says AI spend is estimated from tokens. The writer's price was checked against Anthropic's pricing page that day and is right: $2 in, $10 out per million tokens. (4) Figures stay in US cents. D34's €2 a day is converted at a dated ECB reference rate in `constants.ts`, and the day is judged on billed spend where it is known and recorded spend where it is not. This replaces D57's reading that the cents are "held against €2 one for one". How it is operated: `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §8.
- 2026-10-17  **Cost per site, per day and per week, billed beside estimated** (owner, issue 1478: *"we also need … to review costs per user, per day and per week to have complete transparency"*; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found in the read-only review of 2026-10-17 (30 days, 1224¢ ledgered): the ledger knew a call's scan and nothing else, so a draft's calls looked like the pass's, a failed or timed-out call looked like one that answered, a free scan's spend joined no account, `scans.cost_cents` differed from its own ledger rows by 0.8–1.6¢ on 26 free scans, and what the vendors billed lived only in files on the box. (1) **Every ledger row names one site or one segment, why it was made, its draft and its outcome**: `fetches.site_id`, `activity` (free scan, onboarding, weekly, draft, rescore — a deep pass after the site's first —, smoke, test — a reserved example domain —, box), `draft_id` and `outcome` (ok, failed, timeout). The database fills the site, the activity and the outcome on insert from the scan and the stored payload, so no write path can leave one out; a draft's cost context names its own calls and, once the draft has a row, the draft. The rows already stored were filled the same way when it shipped, at 0¢: a model call of the draft pipeline, and a Google, news, volume or page read on the same scan between a draft's first and last model call, is that draft's, matched to the draft written for the site within fifteen minutes. (2) **A free scan's spend is the site's it became** (`sites.provisioned_from_scan_id`), on the owner's page and in `npm run costs`; the per-site daily cap (#885) still counts a site's own calls only. (3) **The roll-ups are the ledger's.** `scans.cost_cents` is the ledger's sum over the scan, its drafts' calls left out, plus what a retry carries from the report it retries (`carried_cents`); `drafts.cost_cents` is the sum over the draft's calls. The database computes both, whatever the process that spent wrote. A draft rewritten (#788) or edited carries every attempt's calls. (4) **What the vendors billed is stored**, one row per vendor per UTC day (`vendor_days`), read by the maintenance tick with no owner step: DataForSEO's balance at each day's first tick, a day's bill being its fall to the next day's with deposits added back; Anthropic's cost report where the deployment holds `ANTHROPIC_ADMIN_KEY`, and otherwise AI spend is said to be estimated; and one row a month for each fixed vendor — Stripe's fees from its balance transactions, in euro cents, and Vercel, Supabase, the job platform and Resend with no figure, because none reports a bill the product can read. Box spend is a row per domain where the box holds a database key; until then it stays in the box's spend log, now with the domain. (5) **The owner reads it on `/app/costs`**, owner-only (`OWNER_EMAILS`; any other account gets the 404) and not in the navigation: the seven UTC days to today, each site's and each segment's week estimated beside billed, each site's month at that pace with its share of the reported fixed costs set against €40.50 (€49 less 21% VAT), each site each day, the week by activity with its failed and timed-out calls, each vendor-day's recorded spend beside the vendor's own bill and the gap, and each day against D34's €2 judged on billed spend where known. Nothing that decides whether to call reads any of it: the caps, the reservations and the price list are unchanged (#1408), and no customer screen shows a cost, a vendor or an activity (§4, 2026-10-10). How it is operated: `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §8.
- 2026-10-06  **Every measurement checks its own health** (owner, D58, #1287; "D57–D60 approved as recommended"). At the end of every pass the pass records, on its own scan row (`scans.health`), pass or fail for four checks: (1) no rival's brand volume is polluted, which builds on #1285's fix; (2) every tracked prompt names only current rivals; (3) the market has at least one searched term with volume; (4) the mention rate states its sample size and margin. A failing check is listed in the Monday owner digest and on the scan's internal record. It leaves the pass's data stored, and it keeps the headline numbers (§4, D22) on the last healthy pass. No mention rate is shown until at least 2 weeks of the current prompt set exist; before then the count and its sample are shown ("0 of 46 answers"). (D63 added a fifth check, `serp_timeouts`, and #1314 a sixth, `knowledge_base`: §5 and below.)
- 2026-10-06  **How D58 is built** (#1287; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *When.* A deep pass, a weekly pass and a re-measure each check last, after their rivals are re-checked and their topics derived, against the market they leave in force. A free scan has no market of its own and records nothing; a pass from before this line holds no record and is never read as unhealthy. *Polluted.* A rival's stored volume is larger than the keywords its brand is read under hold (#1285's qualified names): Brandi stored at 22,200 where "brandi ai" has 260. *Names only current rivals.* The prompts are the ones the pass asked, or the stored set where it asked none. One fails where it is a comparison or branded prompt the rivals in force do not yield, or where it names a product a pass read as a rival candidate and did not keep. A pass that concludes new rivals asked about the old ones, so it fails this check; the re-measure a rival change starts at once (#1185) asks the new prompts and is the healthy pass that week. (Since 2026-10-07 the rivals' order is not read, and a pass that changed its rivals fails only where a prompt names one that left: D64, below.) *Searched term.* A term with volume that no other meaning pollutes. *Sample and margin.* The pass's own unbranded answers give a count and a 95% range; a pass that asks the prompts and read no answer fails, and the onboarding pass, which asks none, states none and passes. *The headline numbers.* The rolling window that the AI mention rate, share of voice and *Your pages cited* are read from (Overview, the AI answers tab, the weekly mail, update-first) leaves out a pass with a failing check; a healthy pass of the same week stands in for it, else the week is absent and the numbers are those of the last healthy passes. Search visibility of written pages and AI visits are not read from a pass's prompts and are not held back. *The digest.* The Monday owner digest lists every pass a check failed on in the seven days before it fell due, with the site, the scan and what each failing check found, and a digest that is due is sent for that alone. *Two weeks.* Until the window holds two weeks of the set the newest pass asked (a rebuild starts the count again, D54), Overview's tile, the AI answers tab and the weekly mail show the answers that named the site and how many were read, and no percentage, range or trend. The strings are `overview.tile.mention-rate.early`, `overview.tile.mention-rate.early.of` and `mail.weekly.mentionCount`. Proof on stored evidence, 2026-10-06: scan `17f5bbff`'s stored rows fail check 1 on Brandi and check 2 on five prompts — the four comparisons and one branded prompt that named AEO Engine, MaxAEO or Meltwater; its stored report holds five, not the six the issue counted — and pass checks 3 ("generative engine optimization", 4,400) and 4 (0 of 88 answers, 0–4%).
- 2026-10-06  **Context stays relevant: topics** (owner, D59, #1287). Every pass dismisses open, never-drafted topics that name a rival that is no longer current, or that use a term from the market's `notOurMarket`. A topic with a draft or a publication is never touched.
- 2026-10-06  **How D59's prune is built** (#1287; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *No longer current.* A product a pass read as a rival candidate and did not keep, known by the keyword its brand volume was read under, whatever that volume is: D50 keeps a searched product's comparison, and this dismisses it once the product has left the rivals. It is judged only where the volumes were read and the market holds rivals. A rival dropped before D50 read brand volumes is not known this way ("meltwater cost" on reachkit.app, which fit already scores 0). *A term from `notOurMarket`.* The phrase as the market holds it, or without its closing "software", "tools", "platforms", "apps" or "systems" where two or more words are left; one word alone is never a match, so "geo vs seo" stands. *Never drafted.* No draft row, in any state, was ever written from the topic. A search an operator retargeted a live page to (#1285) is not judged. Proof on stored evidence, 2026-10-06: "aeo engine alternatives" and "reachkit vs maxaeo" are dismissed; "profound alternatives", "otterly alternatives" and the retarget stand.
- 2026-10-07  **SERP reads wait longer on paid passes, a free scan asks again, and timeouts are a health check** (owner, D63, #1298; "D62 and D63 approved as recommended"). Found: DataForSEO's live Google SERP reads took 6–10 s, and 20–40% met the 10 s bound on most days (7 of 9 between 08:00 and 09:00 UTC on 2026-10-07), each billed at 0.2¢; two of the last five smoke-test free scans ended `degraded`. (1) On a deep, weekly or re-measure pass a SERP read waits up to **30 s**, never past the pass's own time ceiling; an answer that arrives late is still kept. (2) A free scan keeps the 10 s bound and asks a timed-out SERP read once more, where the free path's time allows, inside the 12¢ cap. (3) D58 gains a fifth check, `serp_timeouts`: a pass where more than 20% of its SERP reads timed out fails it, so the headline numbers stay on the last healthy pass. (4) The Monday owner digest's cost section (D57) adds the week's SERP timeout rate and what the timed-out reads were billed. This replaces the 2026-09-18 line's "10 s on every tier" for paid passes' SERP reads; every other vendor call keeps 10 s. (Since 2026-10-07 AI Mode reads wait and are counted as SERP reads: #1314, below.)
- 2026-10-07  **How D63 is built** (#1298; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *Thirty seconds.* The bound is on one request: a deep pass's live read, and each leg of a weekly pass's queued read. *The free scan's second ask.* #865's one more ask already existed, but it was held to the 13 s the asking stage has, which a read that took 10 s to time out had nearly spent, so it was rarely made and its answer was dropped. It is now held to the pass: it is asked once every question has been asked once, while the pass's 50 s leave room for one whole 10 s read and 2 s to store it, the report is not yet composed, and the asking's share of the 12¢ still holds it. When the asking stage's 13 s run out, no new question is started, and the pass waits for the reads already in flight and for these second asks before it scores, so a read that was bought is counted — unless fewer than those 12 s of the pass are left, where it scores at once as before; a free scan with a slow read therefore takes longer than the 40 s target and never longer than 50 s. The cost: a timed-out read is billed 0.2¢ of the 0.4¢ it reserved, which is the room the second ask is made in; a scan whose twelve reads all time out stops asking again when the asking's 5¢ is spent. *The check.* A pass's SERP reads are its own `fetches` rows for Google SERPs: every read that reached the vendor, a second ask of the same search included, and never an answer served from the cache. A timeout is a read our own bound ended; a queued task not ready at the pass's ceiling is not one. More than a fifth fails; a pass that made no read, or whose rows could not be read, fails nothing. A free scan records no health, as before. *The digest.* Product-wide for the seven dates the cost section reports: the share of SERP reads that timed out, with both counts, and the sum those rows were billed. The strings are `mail.ops.incident.fact.serp-timeouts` and `mail.ops.incident.fact.serp-timeout-cost`. Proof on stored evidence, 2026-10-07: the ledger's SERP reads for 2 to 7 October timed out 10 of 62, 1 of 16, 5 of 33, 4 of 14, 11 of 38 and 9 of 14, 8.0¢ billed for nothing in six days; answered reads took a median 4.8–6.3 s and a 95th percentile of 7.7–9.8 s, so 30 s is three times the slowest ordinary answer. The 08:00 UTC hour's 7 of 9 fails `serp_timeouts`; 3 October's 1 of 16 does not.
- 2026-10-07  **A production baseline: a pass sizes only on-market domains, keeps its rivals' topics, and can be replayed** (owner, D64, #1301; "D64 approved as recommended"). Found in reachkit.app's re-measure `d4bb3740` (2026-10-07, 70.19¢). (1) **Sizing.** A pass sizes its rivals, and of the other domains in its top tens only the ones its market holds as on-market: a product an AI answer names or one with brand demand (D50). It never sizes a publisher, an aggregator or a domain the rival conclusion left out. The pass had bought 15 sizings at 2.4¢, 11 of them for domains outside the market (seranking.com, builtin.com, hubspot.com and eight more). (2) **A current rival's comparison is kept.** "otterly alternatives" was dismissed though Otterly is a rival. (3) **The prompt check reads the rivals a prompt names, not their order**, and a pass that changed its own rivals does not fail it where every rival its prompts name is in the new set. Where no pass has yet passed its own checks, the newest pass's numbers are shown, marked as a first measurement, instead of nothing. (4) **A search that is only a rival's brand name is never a topic** ("rankscale ai"); "{rival} alternatives", "{rival} vs …" and "{rival} pricing" are not judged by this. (5) **Comparison searches are measured** in the keyword read D50 already makes. (6) **`npm run pass -- --site <domain> --replay <scanId>`** runs a stored pass again through the pass's own steps, at 0¢ and without production, so a pass is verified without the owner pressing Measure. Targets: a re-measure at or under about 45¢, and a weekly pass under `CAPS.WEEKLY_C` with five rivals.
- 2026-10-07  **How D64 is built** (#1301; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *On-market.* The pass knows a domain by what its market holds: the rivals in force, and the brand keywords read for every product a rival conclusion placed in the market (D50's `demand.brands`, at most 30 days old). A top-ten domain is sized where it is a rival, or where one of those brands is its own name (the domain's label, or the label and its ending said apart: "peec ai" for `peec.ai`) and that brand has 100 searches a month or an AI answer of this pass cited the domain or named the brand. A pass whose site holds no market, or a market with no brands read, sizes its rivals only, so a first pass bands a question on the rivals in its top ten and on keyword difficulty. *One a week.* A sizing is kept 30 days and a site's rivals are usually first sized on one day, so they fall due together, 12¢ in one week. A weekly pass sizes one due rival again (`WEEKLY_RIVAL_RESIZES`) and the others keep their dated size until their week; a deep pass and a re-measure size every rival that is due. *What dismissed "otterly alternatives".* Not D59's prune: the same-question judgement (#1131) called "profound alternatives" and "otterly alternatives" one question, and the collapse dismissed the second. Two searches are one question only where they name the same products, by the rivals' names and the brands the market read; searches that name no product are judged as before. *The prompt check.* A comparison or branded prompt passes where it has the wording the rivals in force yield, with a current rival's name in each place a rival's name stands. A prompt that names a product that left the rivals fails, as before. *First measurement.* Where the four-week window holds passes that asked the prompts and none is healthy, the newest one stands alone as the window: its count and sample are shown, never a rate, with the line `overview.tile.mention-rate.first` on Overview's tile and the AI answers tab. *A rival's brand name.* The search, normalised, is exactly a rival's name, its domain, its domain's label, one of its search names, or a brand the market read. No pass plans a topic on one, and an open row for one that nothing was drafted from is dismissed. *Comparison volumes.* The brand read also asks for "{candidate} alternatives" for every candidate and for the comparisons D50 derives from the volumes already held; a brand read for the first time joins a pair on the next read. They are stored apart from the brands (`demand.searches`), read again with them after 30 days, and where the brands are held and only comparisons are owed the same one call is made for those. A comparison with searches carries its volume when it is proposed, and an open row stored unmeasured takes it; one with no searches stays unmeasured, so D60 still refuses it. *The replay.* A pass is exported through the Supabase connector as one checked document (`scripts/pass/export.sql`, `scripts/pass/assemble.mjs`) and committed under `scripts/pass/snapshot/`. The replay loads it into the repository's local database, puts back the state the pass started from (the rivals' order by the prompts it asked, the brand volumes without what it bought, the topics without its own), and runs the sizing and the deep pass's own step after the report. A call the stored pass made is answered from its `fetches` row; a rival or market conclusion over the same report is answered with the pass's stored answer; any other call is refused and listed. `--live` buys the refused calls inside `--cap-cents` (default 30), writes them to the spend log (D57) and keeps them beside the snapshot, so the next replay is again 0¢. The stages before the report are not run, and a weekly pass is not replayed yet. Proof, 2026-10-07, replay of `d4bb3740` (3.28¢ bought once for the calls the stored pass never made): rivals Profound (6,600), Otterly (880), Omniscient (590), Rankscale (480), Brandi (260); 4 sizings bought where the stored pass bought 15, Profound's served from the cache; 43.87¢ against 70.19¢; a weekly-tier estimate of 36.19¢ (the last weekly pass's 28.58¢, this pass's rival conclusion and keyword read, one sizing); all five health checks pass; "otterly alternatives" stays open; no topic is "rankscale ai"; "profound alternatives" carries 210 searches a month, and "otterly alternatives", "rankscale alternatives", "omniscient alternatives" and the three pairs were read at none; the 25 prompts name only current rivals.
- 2026-10-07  **General rules, never tuned to one site** (owner, with D65, #1303): *"this must never be built or optimized to get the correct answers for reachkit as dogfood but for all variety of customers who signup and use the solution. this must be organically baked in and validated before customers start paying."* No word list, threshold or prompt is tuned to reachkit.app's market. Every rule holds for a plumber, a dentist, a shop, a SaaS company, a UK law firm and a German small business alike. A finding on reachkit.app is an example of a general defect, and the acceptance test of a measurement rule is the customer panel (#1304), not reachkit.app alone. This applies to this change and to every later one.
- 2026-10-07  **Measured searches come from searched terms; the knowledge base keeps real questions and specific audiences; rivals are stable** (owner, D65, #1303; "D65 approved as recommended"). Found in reachkit.app's pass `0e5c5228` (2026-10-07), as examples of general defects: its ten measured Google searches were seeded from the internal category label and held "ai monitoring tools" (mostly another field), "seo content services" (agencies) and "rankscale ai" (a rival's brand), and none of its market's largest searches; its knowledge base held page headings as buyer questions and "Businesses" as an audience (§5 of this date); and one pass swapped Rankscale for Peec AI. (1) **Measured searches.** A pass seeds its measured questions from `searchTerm`, the market's other searched terms and the outcome phrasings of its job (D49). It drops a search that is off-market — it matches `notOurMarket`, or the market's own judgement says it means something else — one that is only a rival's brand, and one that is navigational for another company. It keeps the strongest by volume and fit within the existing budget. This replaces the 2026-10-06 line's "the questions a pass measures are still seeded from the concluded category". (2) **Rival stability.** A current rival is dropped only after two consecutive passes leave it out. A new rival enters at once only where it outranks the weakest current one by search demand; otherwise it waits one pass. The cap of 5 holds. This replaces the 2026-10-02 line's "new ones fill to five". (3) **Locale.** D65 also says a pass's locale follows the site's own country and language. That part is not built: it is #1305, `blocked-on-owner` (below).
- 2026-10-07  **How D65 is built** (#1303; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *Seeds.* The market's searched terms that no other meaning pollutes, most searched first, lead the seed ladder; the first is the seed every pass buys and the others are bought with it inside the same three extra purchases, where the synonyms were. The category and the rest of the ladder follow and are bought only while the pass is short. A market with no searched term, a site with no market and the free scan seed as before. *The terms are measured.* Each of those terms is itself a measured search, at the volume D49 read. The demand and difficulty ceilings of right-sizing (#830, #858) say what a site is offered to write; they do not hold a market's own searches out of its measurement, so a term is measured whatever its volume, and derivation still bands it. The terms take up to six of the twelve places (`SELECTION.maxMarketTerms`), most searched first; the other places go to the searches the seeds and the pool yield, by the score they always had. *Off-market.* A search is never selected where it uses a phrase from `notOurMarket`, read as D59 reads it, or where it is exactly a name a rival or a read brand is searched under (D64's reading); "{rival} alternatives" and the like are not judged by this. *The judgement.* Before the twelve are worded and asked, the ones the market holds no verdict for are judged with their twelve runners-up in one `sonnet` call (`market-searches`, about 1¢), which reads the market as the terms call does and answers one of four verdicts for each: on the market, another meaning, another market, or a company's name. It is a second call beside the terms call, not the same one: the terms are read before the seeds are bought, and the searches are known only after. The verdicts are kept on the market (`judged`, the newest 200), so a search is judged once and a later pass with the same searches makes no call; a new market starts empty. The twelve are then chosen again without the ones judged off the market. A call that does not come back leaves the selection as it was. *Rivals.* A pass still concludes up to ten candidates and D50 still says which five it wants. The site keeps `rivalsLeftOut`, the rivals in force the last pass did not want, and `rivalsWaiting`, the candidates it wanted and held back. A rival left out stays; left out again by the next pass that concludes rivals, it is dropped. A candidate enters where a place is free and either its brand is searched more than the least-searched rival in force, or the pass before wanted it too. A site with no rivals takes the pass's own at once. A pass that concluded none, or that its ceiling stopped (D31), counts as no pass. A founder's own rivals are untouched (D28). A site whose rivals were set before D28 and are not products of its market therefore keeps them for one pass more. *The replay* (D64) gains a step before the sizing: it chooses the measured searches again from the stored market, the stored pool and the suggestions its seeds buy, and judges them; it puts back the rivals as they stood before the pass's own change. `scripts/pass/export.sql` now also exports the pass's suggestion reads and the rival conclusions of the thirty days before. *Locale.* Checked on 2026-10-07: every vendor read is fixed to United States and English at the transport (`SERP_LOCATION`), for every site, and §11 lists locale derivation as not in the MVP. A non-US site is therefore still measured as a US searcher's; #1305 asks the owner how a site's country is known and whether other languages follow, and nothing is built until that answer is a dated line here. Proof, 2026-10-07, replay of `0e5c5228` (10.51¢ bought once, of a 15¢ cap, for the calls the stored pass never made: four suggestion reads, the judgement and the topic calls its new state asks): the twelve measured searches hold "generative engine optimization" (4,400), "answer engine optimization" (2,400), "AI search visibility" (390) and "AEO software" (110), and eight searches judged on the market ("best answer engine optimization tools", 210); "ai monitoring tools", "ai tracking tool" and "seo content services" are judged off it and "rankscale ai" is a rival's name; the rivals stay Profound, Otterly, Omniscient, Brandi and Rankscale, with Peec AI waiting one pass; five of five health checks pass; 23.23¢ against the stored 20.34¢. The pass had bought three suggestion reads for its category and synonyms at 1.8¢ each that returned nothing, as earlier passes had. The older snapshot `d4bb3740` now replays with calls refused, because it holds no suggestion reads and the state it starts from asks different topic calls; `0e5c5228` is the baseline.
- 2026-10-07  **The customer panel's general rules: rival domains, how closely a rival competes, searches that are searches, and AI Mode's wait** (#1314, from the panel's baseline of 2026-10-07 on 7 of 11 sites; each reading is a drafted default the owner may correct. Locale is D66, #1311, and a local business measured nationally is D67; neither is here). Every rule holds for any kind of business, and none is tuned to a panel site. (1) **A rival's domain is checked when it is concluded.** Found: two of austinplumbery.com's five rivals were at domains no site answers at, and savvycal.com's Chili Piper was stored as `piper.com`. Each candidate's home page is read once, at 0¢, before the candidates are weighed (D50), at setup and in every pass. A domain with no address, and a page that answers and never says the rival's name, are not the rival's. The rival is then looked for at a domain this pass's top tens or AI answers hold whose label is its name, at the name as a `.com`, and at the name under the stated ending; the first whose page says the name is kept in the stated domain's place, and a rival found at none is dropped. A page says the name where it holds the name, or a name buyers search it under, as letters, with accents folded and "&" and "and" left out. A home page served from another domain that names the rival there is the rival's at that domain (`mazars.co.uk` is Forvis Mazars at `forvismazars.com`). A page that would not be read — a refusal, a timeout, a rate limit, or a page that says under 200 letters before its scripts run — proves nothing, and the rival is kept. What was put right or dropped is logged as `rival_domains_checked`. A founder's own rivals are not checked (D28). (2) **Demand orders rivals that do the same job.** Found: savvycal.com's second rival was When2meet, a free poll tool, at 110,000 searches a month, because D50 puts the most-searched candidate first whatever it sells. The rival conclusion now also says, for each candidate, whether it is *direct* — a buyer choosing this business would seriously weigh it instead: the same job, the same kind of buyer, a comparable offer — or *partial*: part of the job, another kind of buyer, or a free or single-purpose tool. Among the searched, every direct rival comes before a partial one, in the choice of the five and in their stored order; D50's and D65's other rules stand. A candidate the conclusion did not weigh is read as direct. (3) **A search nobody would type as it stands is never measured and never planned.** Four shapes: a phrase said twice ("best bakery near me near me"); a comparison with nothing on one side ("chartered accountant vs"); a web address ("temporarytattoos.com alternatives"); and a rival called by its domain's label where that is no word of its name or of a name it is searched under ("abcplumbingaustin alternatives" for ABC Home & Commercial Services; "calendly" and "mazars" are words of their names). The first two came from the vendor's own keyword lists, with volume, so volume excuses none of them. No pass selects one to measure, proposes one, or plans a page on one, and an open topic for one that nothing was drafted from is dismissed. A web address is a name under one of about forty common endings with four letters or more before it, so "node.js" and "asp.net" are left alone; the list under-reports by design. (4) **A rival is named by its name.** A comparison ("{rival} alternatives", "A vs B") used the domain's label for a name longer than four words. It now uses the name; for a longer one the short name buyers search it under ("abc plumbing austin"), else its words before "&", "and" or a comma ("radiant plumbing"), else its first four. A name that is itself a web address is said without its ending. (5) **Names keep their letters.** A topic that names the business or a rival keeps accents and letters outside ASCII ("zuni café vs quince", where it read "zuni caf vs quince"); a slug writes them in ASCII and never drops them (`zuni-cafe`, `strasse`). (6) **A topic at business fit 0 or 1 is never ready.** Checked on 2026-10-07: this already held — readiness answers `low_fit` for it and no day is filled by it (2026-09-29, #1134); the row stays open with its reason so no later pass judges it again. The panel counted such a row as a defect because it did not read readiness; it now does, and fails only a low-fit topic left ready. (7) **AI Mode waits as a SERP read does, and its timeouts count.** Found: 16 to 20 `serp/google/ai_mode` reads a site were aborted at 10 s and billed 0.2¢ each, 4¢ a site for nothing, while `serp_timeouts` passed, because D63 gave the 30 s to the organic read alone. AI Mode is a SERP endpoint: on a deep, weekly or re-measure pass each of its requests waits up to 30 s, never past the pass's ceiling, for the twelve, the market's buyer prompts and the tracked prompts alike, and a free scan asks none. `serp_timeouts` and the digest's SERP lines (D63) count AI Mode reads with the organic ones. Proof at 0¢, 2026-10-07: the home pages of the baseline's 35 stored rivals read again — the two domains with no site are dropped, Chili Piper is put at `chilipiper.com`, Forvis Mazars at `forvismazars.com`, and the other 31 are kept as they are; and `npm run panel -- --recheck --date 2026-10-07` on the stored baseline, whose checks now hold a pass to rules 1 to 5 and 7 and to the knowledge base's bound (§5), and unit tests on fixtures taken from the panel's stored results. The live proof is the next panel run on the merged code.
- 2026-10-07  **Each site is measured in its own country and language** (owner, D66, #1311; "D66-D68 approved"; resolves #1305; lifts the 2026-08-28 "MVP is US-English only" line and §11's "locale derivation" for the scope below). Found on the customer panel: every vendor read was made as Google in the United States, in English, so a Düsseldorf bakery was given English terms with US volumes ("bakery near me", 3.35M a month) and five US bakeries as rivals. (1) **A site has a country and a language.** They are read from its country domain, `<html lang>`, currency, address and phone prefix; setup asks once, with the reading filled in, and the founder's answer wins; they are stored on the site. A free scan uses the reading. A `.com` with no other signal stays the United States in English. (2) **Supported now:** English in the US, UK, Ireland, Australia and Canada; German in Germany, Austria and Switzerland. Any other country or language is measured in the nearest supported country in English, and the report says so in one line. The list is data. (3) **Every vendor read follows it**: keyword volumes and suggestions, SERPs, the news search, AI Mode and the ChatGPT scraper are asked in the site's country and language, and every cache key carries them. (4) **German end to end.** For a German site the tracked prompts, the measured questions, the intent shapes, the knowledge-base read, the page templates and writer, the titles (D51, with their own banned patterns) and the report and mail that customer reads are German (the report and mail half is superseded by 2026-10-15 below, #1443). (5) The panel holds each site's true country and language and fails a site measured in another.
- 2026-10-07  **How D66 is built** (#1311; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *The list* is `src/lib/locale/locales.ts`: a country is one row (the vendor's name for it, its language, its domain ending, dialling prefix, currency marks and names), and a language is one row plus its pack under `src/lib/locale/language/` and its translated sentences. *The reading.* The country is the one the strongest signal names, in this order: the country domain, the country the page's structured data states, the phone prefix, an address line, the region of `<html lang>`, a currency that belongs to one country. A two-letter ending sold as a generic name (`.io`, `.co`, `.ai`) names no country; the dollar and the euro name none; a country named in a sentence ("we ship to Canada") is no address. The language is the one the page's own words are in where they say so, else `<html lang>`: many German sites leave `lang` at their builder's `en-US`. *The fallback.* A pair on the list is itself. A German site in a country that is not on the list is measured in Germany. Anything else is measured in English in the nearest supported country (the United Kingdom for Europe, Africa and the Middle East; Australia for Asia and the Pacific; the United States otherwise) and the report names what was not measured. *Stored.* `sites.country` and `sites.language`; a site with neither is read by its next pass, which stores the reading; setup's answer overwrites it. A stored report records its locale only where it is not the United States in English. *The vendors.* The transport writes the location and language from the locale it is handed and refuses one that is not on the list; LLM Mentions takes none, as before. The United States in English keeps its old cache keys, so reads held from before still serve. *The model calls* that write for a market — the profile, the market and its terms, the rivals, the question wording, the buyer searches, the knowledge-base read, page research and the writer — are told the country and language in one added field; for the United States in English the prompts are word for word what they were. *Shapes.* A search's shape ("beste", "vs", "Alternativen", "wie", "Kosten", "Preise") is recognised in every supported language at once; stop words, word endings and compounds are each language's own, so "Sauerteigbrot" is a bakery's word where "Brot" is. Text is compared with its umlauts kept; a topic's address is written in ASCII as #1314 built it, with a German site's umlauts as German writes them (amended 2026-10-18, issue 1514: `baeckerei-hohe-strasse`). *Pages.* A German page follows the same templates; its title formulas, its banned title patterns and four rules of its own (German throughout, "Sie" unless the site says "du", German capitals, German dates and prices) are German, and the fixed lines of a page and of the hosted blog are German. *Copy.* The report, the three site mails (weekly, draft ready, published) and the hosted blog are read in the site's language; a sentence with no translation is shown in English. The app, setup, the lead mails, the account mails and the forms on the report that run in the browser stay English. (Superseded for the report and the three site mails by 2026-10-15 below, #1443: they are English too.) *Not yet proved live* at the merge of #1311: the owner's daily test spend was used up, so the panel's German and UK sites, the AI Mode and ChatGPT-scraper coverage for Germany and the UK, and a German page pack are run the next day.
- 2026-10-15  **ReachKit's own screens and mail are English; a site's language is for measuring it and for its own pages** (owner, #1443: "for local business you are generating in multi language, we should ignore this from a UI perspective. It might be required to use local languages for keywords and AI answers, however never for the UI currently."; supersedes the UI half of D66). (1) **Every screen and every mail is English**, whatever the site's language: the free report and its share image, the app, setup, Settings, sign-in, and the weekly, draft-ready and published mails. Dates and the names of countries and languages on the report are written in English; the report's measurement line says, in English, where it was measured (*Measured in Google Germany, in German.*). (2) **Measurement keeps the site's country and language** (D66 unchanged): terms, searches, the SERP locale, AI answers and the market reading. (3) **What is printed on the customer's own site is in the site's language**: written pages, the report facts a page states, the fixed lines of a page, the hosted blog and the WordPress post. The German overlay is that one partition (`src/lib/presentation/copy/keys/de/page.ts`); the report, mail, laws, bands and offer overlays are deleted.
- 2026-10-08  **A business that serves one area is measured where it serves** (owner, D67, #1318; "D66-D68 approved"; builds on D66). Found on the customer panel of 2026-10-07: 5 of austinplumbery.com's 22 terms and searches named Austin or "near me", the rest were the whole country's ("emergency plumber", 74,000 a month), and its rivals included Roto-Rooter and Mr. Rooter; 1 of 20 for alexander.co.uk, an independent Manchester accountant, named Manchester, and its rivals were Grant Thornton, Forvis Mazars, PwC, BDO and Deloitte; 15 of baeckerei-hinkel.de's 22 said "near me" and none named Düsseldorf. (1) **A site has a serving area**: one city or metro, or none — a business with no place sells nationally or online. It is read from the address, `LocalBusiness` structured data, a service-area page and the phone number, and setup asks it with the D66 question (§5); the founder's answer wins. (2) **Searches and terms are the service plus the place** ("plumber austin", "emergency plumber austin tx", "bäckerei düsseldorf"). Volumes are read at the city where the vendor holds them there, else the country. A "near me" search is measured only where it can be read from the place. (3) **Rivals come from the place's own results**: its local pack and its organic top tens. A national chain is a rival only where it shows there, and brand demand is weighed for the place. (4) **AI prompts name the place** ("Looking for the best emergency plumber in Austin…"). (5) **The panel holds each local site to this**: its `place` is a check, no longer a note for a person to read.
- 2026-10-08  **How D67 is built** (#1318; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *Stored.* `sites.market.area`: the place (its name, its region where read, the search vendor's name for it) and whose answer it is. It is no part of the market that passes re-check, so a market that moves keeps its place. *Read.* The market call (D28) also answers the one city or metro the business serves its customers in, or none; it is told what the home page's structured data states (town, region, areas served, telephone, business type) beside the pages it already reads, and that an office address alone is no serving area. The first pass that reads it decides, once; after that only the founder changes it. A free scan measures no place (superseded 2026-10-15, issue 1441, for a home page that states its town); its report keeps the town a home page states for a business whose structured data marks it a local one, and setup's question is filled from that. *Where the vendor reads.* The vendor's free list of Google locations is read once a site (`placeLocation`, the tenth function of the closed vendor list, at 0¢) and the city's own name on it is stored: "Austin,Texas,United States". Checked on 2026-10-08: organic SERPs and AI Mode are read by city; Labs holds volumes by country alone and the ChatGPT scraper is asked by country, so those two stay the country's. Two cities of one name with no region to tell them apart are not guessed between, and such a place is read from its country. Every cache key of a read made by city carries the city. *Terms.* The terms call is told the place, and every term it returns is made the service plus the place in code: one that names it stands, "near me" gives way to the place's name, anything else has the name added. Terms a market held from before its place was known are read again on the next pass, whatever their age. *Searches.* Every suggestions seed is the service plus the place. A search is measured only where it names the place, or says "near me" and the vendor lists the city; the place's words are no part of what the relevance guard asks the business to sell. A "near me" search's volume is the country's, the only one the vendor holds, and the demand ceiling (§6 right-sizing) reads it as that. *Rivals.* The local pack rides on the organic SERP the pass already buys; its businesses with a site join the evidence beside the top tens. The rivals call is told the place, and answers for each candidate whether it trades across the country. A national candidate that no result of this pass shows is dropped, and one that is shown has its brand volume read under its name beside the place's ("roto-rooter austin"); a local business keeps its own name, which is its local demand. *Prompts.* A prompt says the market's name without the place a searched term holds, then "in" and the place — for every kind of business that is bought in a place (D69's list says which). *Not built here:* topics are not held to the place beyond what the measured searches and the model's instruction give them; Settings has no field for the place, so it is changed in setup.
- 2026-10-08  **AI prompt templates by kind of business** (owner, D69, #1318; "D69 approved"). Found with D66 (#1317): the 25 tracked-prompt templates are software-shaped ("tools", "free trial", "without an engineering team", "Anbieter"). For a bakery, a plumber or a restaurant they read oddly in both languages, so the mention rate would measure questions real buyers do not ask. (1) **The market conclusion records the kind of business**, from a closed list held as data: local service (trades, health, beauty); shop; hospitality (restaurant, café, hotel); professional services (legal, accounting, consulting, agency); software or app; creator, course or education. (2) **Each kind has its own templates in English and German**, keeping D19's split and count (25 in 5/7/4/4/3/2) with subjects that fit the kind: a restaurant is asked about occasions, dishes and neighbourhoods; a plumber about emergencies, cost and trust; a shop about products, gifts and price; software keeps the set it had. Comparison and branded prompts name the rivals as before. (3) **A site's set is rebuilt once when its kind is first recorded** (D54's once-only rule), and the mention rate restarts with D54's mark. (4) Every template is drafted (#759) and listed in the pull request.
- 2026-10-08  **How D69 is built** (#1318; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *The list* is `src/lib/market/kinds.ts`: a kind is one row — what the market call is told it is, and whether it is bought in a place. The market call chooses one; a market that stands takes the kind the first call that reads one gives it. A market concluded before D69, and one whose category the founder set and no call reads, is read as software, the set every site had. *The templates* are `src/lib/locale/language/prompts/`, one file a language, five kinds each; software's stay in the language pack. The market's name is whatever buyers search — one tradesman, several, or a service — so an English sentence takes it after "the best" or "Which" with a verb that does not change with number, and a German sentence puts it first, before a colon, because a German question word takes the noun's gender ("Bäckerei in Düsseldorf: Wer hat die besten Bewertungen?"). The seven use-case prompts of a business that is not software are all asked of its most-searched name: its other names are often the thing done ("drain cleaning"), not who does it; they are still asked by name in the category prompts. The buyer is said without the place the market's name already says. *The rebuild.* The stored set keeps the kind it is worded for. A set with none was worded for software: where the kind first recorded is anything else, the whole set is rebuilt once, unchecked like a first build, and `rebuiltAt` is D54's mark, so the rolling rate reads the new set alone. A software site's set is not rebuilt; its kind is written beside it. A kind that changes later moves the set at the rotation rate only (D33). *The few prompts a deep pass asks beside the twelve* (#1195) use the same wording for a business that is not software. *The panel* (`docs/walk/panel/sites.json`, `kinds`) fails a site recorded as another kind, a prompt in software's words for a business that is not software, and a local site's prompt that does not name its place.
- 2026-10-08  **D67 and D69 on the customer panel** (#1318; `scripts/panel/runs/2026-10-08.json`; 148.93¢ of the issue's 150¢, D34). Four of the panel's six local sites were run. *austinplumbery.com*, local service: its search term is "plumber austin" (6,600 a month), all 17 of its terms and measured searches name Austin, Google was read from `Austin,Texas,United States`, and its rivals are Abacus, Radiant, Reliant and Clarke Kent Plumbing with Roto-Rooter, the one national chain, kept because it ranks there and weighed as "roto-rooter austin" (210 a month). *zunicafe.com*, hospitality: "restaurants san francisco", 22 of 22 local, read from San Francisco, five San Francisco restaurants as rivals. *alexander.co.uk*, professional services: "accountant manchester" (1,900 a month), 14 of 14 local, read from Manchester, and five independent Manchester firms where the baseline held Grant Thornton, PwC, BDO and Deloitte. *baeckerei-hinkel.de*, recorded as a shop: "bäckerei düsseldorf" (3,600 a month), 16 of 16 local, read from `Dusseldorf,North Rhine-Westphalia,Germany`, German prompts that name Düsseldorf — and no rivals: its rivals call was cut off at the call's 1,400 output tokens on both attempts and refused. That ceiling is 2,400 from this issue (`INFERENCE_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`); it was not run again, because the cap was reached. Every site that ran passes the two new checks (its place, and prompts that fit its kind). *Not run, the cap did not reach them:* pdxsmiledesign.com and fletcherelectric.co.uk, and the five sites that serve no one place (tattly.com, savvycal.com, clearleft.com, wesbos.com, streaksapp.com). The next panel run is their proof, and the bakery's rivals': `npm run panel -- --env-file ../../reachkitv3/.env.local --sites baeckerei-hinkel.de,pdxsmiledesign.com,fletcherelectric.co.uk,tattly.com,savvycal.com,clearleft.com,wesbos.com,streaksapp.com --cap-cents 200`. Checked with one live read (0.4¢): AI Mode answers from a city.
- 2026-10-08  **Google reads are queued, not live** (#1352, P0; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found: on 2026-10-08 from 16:00 UTC every free scan's Google read met the 10 s bound (25 of 25), so no free report had a score, and the production smoke test failed. Measured the same evening with 67 calls for 7.66¢: 13 of 15 calls to DataForSEO's live endpoint failed, with or without the AI Overview option and on its plainer `regular` form — `50000 Internal Server Error` after about 16 s, unbilled, on each one whose answer was read — and the two that answered took 22 s and 28 s. Tasks posted to its queue came back: 34 of 37 at high priority in 3–26 s, one in 149 s and two not in 150 s; 15 of 15 at normal priority in 5–29 s. The weekly pass's normal-priority tasks had taken 52–187 s on three Mondays. So: (1) **No pass reads Google's results live.** A free scan and a site's first pass post each search to the queue at high priority (0.12¢ a search, against 0.2¢ live) and ask every 2 s whether it is ready; the weekly pass keeps the normal queue (0.06¢) and its 10 s. AI Mode's surface is unchanged. (2) **A free scan posts all its searches at once and waits for them until 5 s before its 50 s ceiling**, not only for the 13 s of the asking stage, then scores what came back. It keeps the AI Overview option (the free report's own, since 2026-09-03), which doubles the price to 0.24¢ a search: without it 5 of 5 queued reads held an AI Overview with no sources, which would read as an answer that names nobody. Twelve searches reserve 2.88¢ of the asking's 5¢ (4.8¢ live); a scan whose searches come back slowly takes up to 47 s. (3) **A search that was not read is never an absence.** Its row says it could not be measured, and the score, both cards and the rivals are counted over the searches that were read.
