A scan where no search came back has no score and says presence could not be measured, as before: a score made without it would be an estimate (§2). (4) **A queued task is charged when it is posted**, the AI Overview surcharge included, so it is ledgered at all it reserved whether or not it was collected: a free scan's twelve searches cost 2.88¢. A `task_get` the clock or the network lost is asked again while the pass has time; before, it dropped a paid task. D63's 30 s is each request of a queued read, and its second ask is made where 17 s of a free scan are left. Proof: unit tests with every read never ready and with half never ready; the product's own read for twelve searches, asked as a free scan asks them with 25 s to wait, measured 12 of 12 in 6–26 s, each with its AI Overview's sources; the panel and the production smoke test after the merge.
- 2026-10-09  **A paid pass reads each AI Overview's sources** (#1357; a drafted default the owner may correct). Found: the first pass and the weekly pass bought their Google searches without the AI Overview option, and Google loads most AI Overviews after the page. In the customer panel's stored reads, 35 of the 62 AI Overviews on seven sites came back with no sources (clearleft.com 8 of 8, alexander.co.uk 9 of 11), and each was counted as an answer that names nobody, so a site could score higher on its free scan than on its own weekly measure. So: (1) the first pass and the weekly pass buy the option, as the free scan does. It doubles a search's price: 0.24¢ on the first pass (2.88¢ for twelve, 1.44¢ more) and 0.12¢ on the weekly pass (1.44¢ for twelve, 0.72¢ more, and 0.06¢ more for each page's own question), inside the 150¢ and 40¢ caps; the dearest passes of the three weeks before were 76¢ and 31¢. Their AI answers card no longer carries the reused-answers note. (2) **An AI Overview whose sources were not read is not measured.** A market correction still does not buy the option (2026-09-03); where its read holds an AI Overview with no sources, that search is left out of the AI presence rate and out of the card's counts, and gives no rival a citation. The free scan's price and behaviour are unchanged.
- 2026-10-09  **A weekly pass reads the answers it paid for and did not wait for** (#1160; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found: the weekly pass of 2026-10-05 for reachkit.app ended `complete` in 223 s, but its 100 tracked-prompt asks were posted to the standard queue 34 s in and it stopped waiting 185 s later (D29's 25 s before the ceiling). 50 of 50 ChatGPT asks and 23 of 50 AI Mode asks were still queued. All 73 were billed (8.76¢), and none was read, so the week's mention rate rests on 27 AI Mode answers and no ChatGPT answer. The pass of 2026-09-28 had waited 124–177 s for its SERPs alone. The queue's wait, not the pass's work, is what does not fit in 240 s. So: (1) **A queued ask that was billed and not ready keeps its task id** on its ledger row. (2) **The Monday tick is steps**: the due list, then for each site the pass, the late answers and the telling, each in its own job invocation. (3) **The late answers are read before the week is judged and the digest is sent**, in up to three rounds (`TIMING.weeklyCollectRounds`), each waiting at most the pass's own 240 s. Reading a finished task is free, so nothing is bought and no cap or ceiling moves. An answer read this way joins the pass's tracked-prompt answers and is stored on its ledger row; the pass's health (D58) is recorded again over what it then holds. An ask still queued after the last round stays out of the rate, as before. (4) Not read late: a question's Google result and a published page's question. They stay unmeasured for that week (#1352, rule 3), because adding one means scoring the pass again. A prompt rotated in (D33) is still judged only on answers that arrived inside the pass. A re-measure asks the same prompts on the same queue and does not read late. Proof: unit tests on the vendor seam, the ledger row and the tick's steps; the live proof is the weekly pass of Monday 2026-10-12 (D34, rule 7).
- 2026-10-09  **Rivals are chosen from who the pass saw** (owner, #1365: *"in rivals, we also show 0/10 for all rivals, this should not be normal."*). Found on reachkit.app's pass `0e5c5228`: it tracked Profound, Peec AI, Otterly, Omniscient and Brandi, and none of the five held a first-page place on any of its ten measured searches. D50 chose the rivals a market is known for; the searches a pass measures are what buyers type, and the two need not meet. The rule holds for every site. (1) The rivals a site is measured against include who wins its measured searches and who its AI answers name, not only who the market is known for. (2) A forum, a social network, a publisher or a directory is never a rival (D24), wherever it ranks. (3) A rival the pass saw on no measured search and in no answer is replaced where the pass saw another, and is otherwise named as unseen (§4 of this date). (4) A founder's own rivals stand and are measured (D28).
- 2026-10-09  **How #1365 is built** (each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *Seen.* For each on-market candidate of the rival conclusion, the pass counts, from the report it has just stored and at 0¢, the measured searches whose first page holds one of its pages — the ten results, or the local pack above them (D67) — and the AI answers that cite its domain or say its name as the conclusion wrote it. A domain with two results for one search holds that search once. *The choice.* Of the five places, the first two (`RIVAL_PRESENCE.FIRST_PAGE_PLACES`) go to the candidates that hold the most first-page places, most first, whatever their brand volume; only a candidate that does the business's own job takes one (#1314), and a candidate that holds none takes none. The other places follow D50's order, with a candidate the pass saw before one it saw nowhere. This is the five a pass *wants*; D65's stability is unchanged, so a rival in force leaves only after two passes leave it out, and a first-page holder nobody searches by name waits one pass. *The conclusion* is asked to list the candidates its measurements show before the ones it knows only from the market, so a product in the top tens is judged and not crowded out of the ten by better-known names. *Setup* chooses its suggestions the same way, from the free report's results. *The Rivals tab* counts a place in the local pack as a first-page place, as the choice does. Proof on stored evidence, 2026-10-09: for `0e5c5228` the pass wants Rankscale and Analyze AI, the two candidates on its first pages, with Profound, Peec AI and Otterly; Omniscient and Brandi, seen on no first page, are marked as left out and go on the next pass that leaves them out. On the customer panel the same day (`scripts/panel/runs/2026-10-09.json`; 88.78¢ of the 100¢ cap, three sites the day's run had not reached): every rival of zunicafe.com, tattly.com and savvycal.com is one the pass's own top tens or answers show, where on 2026-10-07 three were named from knowledge alone; savvycal.com holds YouCanBook.me, which ranks for its searches and has no brand volume, and no longer holds Chili Piper, seen nowhere. One check failed and is not this rule's: tattly.com's rival at `temporarytattoos.com` was named "Temporary Tattoos", so the category's own 60,500 searches were read as its brand and seven measured searches were judged a rival's name.
- 2026-10-09  **Where a business is, is not where it sells** (#1370, from the customer panel of 2026-10-09; revises D67's *Read*; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found: clearleft.com, a design consultancy with one office in Brighton and clients across the country, was stored as serving Brighton, so its term was "UX design agency Brighton" (10 a month), its one measured search and its 25 prompts named Brighton, and its rivals were Brighton studios. The market call had answered the town from the office's address. (1) **A serving area stands only on the site's own evidence that it serves the place**, for every site. The market call still answers the town; code then looks for one of five things, and with none the business has no serving area, whatever the call answered: *the areas it lists* — its structured data's `areaServed` names the place; *a service page* — a page's title, first heading or address, or the home page's own description, holds the place with a word of the market's name ("North Leeds electrician near you", "Bäckerei Hinkel in Düsseldorf"); *service wording* — a page says the service with the place ("accountants in Manchester", "Austin first plumbing", "Düsseldorfer Bäckerei"), or says it serves it ("serving our neighbors here in Austin", "customers across Leeds"); *the local pack* — a measured search showed the site itself in Google's local pack; *in person* — its kind is carried out where the business is (`inPerson` in `src/lib/market/kinds.ts`: a local service, hospitality) and the site names the town at all. (2) **What is no evidence**: an address, a phone number, a town after "based in", "located in" or "our studio in", and any town on a contact, about, team, jobs, work, case-study, blog, news or events page. A shop and a professional-services firm are not in-person kinds: a shop may sell online and a firm may have clients anywhere, so the site has to say it. (3) **What said so is stored** beside the place (`sites.market.area.because`). A place a pass read before this rule carries none, and the first pass that reads the site decides it again — to the same place with its evidence, or to none; a founder's answer is never touched. Terms that name a place the site no longer serves are read again on that pass, whatever their age. (4) **The free report states a town** (setup's question opens on it, §5) only where the home page lists that town as served or marks itself with a kind carried out in person ("Plumber", "Restaurant"); a plain `LocalBusiness` or `ProfessionalService` with an address states none. Proof on the panel's own pages at 0¢, 2026-10-09 (up to 40 pages of each of the eleven sites read again, each cut to the 600 characters a pass keeps, with the stored market's words and the stored results' local packs): clearleft.com has no evidence for Brighton and gets no area, nor do tattly.com, savvycal.com, wesbos.com and streaksapp.com for a town their pages name; austinplumbery.com, zunicafe.com, pdxsmiledesign.com and fletcherelectric.co.uk keep theirs on a service page, alexander.co.uk on the areas it lists (and on a service page, its wording and six local packs as well), and baeckerei-hinkel.de on its description (and the local pack of "bester bäcker düsseldorf"). The live proof is the next panel run on the merged code. *Not built here:* an open topic drafted for the dropped place ("ux design agency brighton") stays until a pass dismisses it; Settings still has no field for the place.
- 2026-10-09  **A rival named by the category's own words has no brand to read** (#1369, from the customer panel's run of 2026-10-09; the same class as #1285; each reading is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found on tattly.com: the shop at temporarytattoos.com was concluded as the rival "Temporary Tattoos", so the category's 60,500 searches a month were read as its brand, it led the rivals, and six of the twelve measured searches held its "name". The rule holds for every site, in each language of D66. A name is the category's own words where every word of it is a word of the market's category, of another name buyers type for it (its synonyms) or of the term it is measured under, beside words that name nothing ("best", "online", "beste"), and at least one is; the same words closed up ("TemporaryTattoos") count; words are compared without their plural or case ending, by the language's own pack. A name with one word of its own ("Radiant Plumbing", "Bäckerei Hinkel", the joined "Stadtbäckerei") is a name, and so is a web address: the rule under-reports. When the rivals are concluded, after each domain is checked (#1314), such a rival is kept and is **called by its domain**, and no rival's brand volume is read under a keyword that is only those words; one left with no keyword is read under its domain. So its brand volume (D50), its place in the rivals' order, the searches left out as a rival's name (D65), the answers counted as naming it (#1365), the comparisons derived from it (D64) and the tracked prompts that name it all read the domain, never the category. A volume an earlier pass stored under the category's words is dropped from the market's brand volumes at the next rival conclusion; the business's own keywords are never dropped. A founder's own rivals keep the names the founder gave (D28); only their brand volume follows this rule. Code: `src/lib/market/rivals/category-name.ts`.
- 2026-10-14  **A product name that is the category's own words is not the business's brand** (#1423, from stripe.com's free scan of 2026-10-13; the same test as #1369, from the business's side; each reading is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found: the site profile gave stripe.com the brand tokens Stripe, Payments, Terminal, Connect, Billing and Radar, for the category "payment processing platform". A search that names the business is dropped as its own (§6.7), and "Payments" is the category's word, so every payment search in the market was dropped and the scan asked one question. A brand token after the first is not read as the business's name where every word of it is a word of the profile's category, vocabulary or offering type, by #1369's test (`isCategoryName`, `src/lib/market/rivals/category-name.ts`). The first token is the business's own name and always stands, and a vocabulary phrase that says it ("reachkit autopilot") is not a word of the market. One set (`ownBrandTokens`, `src/lib/market/questions/select.ts`) is read everywhere the business's own brand is: the own-brand drop, the parent-topic key, the topics that name only the site, and the broader categories a thin market is offered. "stripe payments" stays the business's own; "payment gateway" is measured. A name with one word of its own ("Radar", "Marketing Hub") still names the business. Stored profiles are read under the rule on their next pass; nothing stored is rewritten.
- 2026-10-15  **A free scan of a local business measures it in its town, and the local pack counts as found** (owner ruling, issue 1441: "go on all 5", change 5; supersedes D67's *A free scan measures no place* for a site that states its place; each reading is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found: a café or plumber was free-scanned on national Google ("plumber", not "plumber leeds"), and the local pack was stored but read by neither the score nor the rivals, so a plumber who tops the map in its town read as invisible beside national directories. (1) **Where a free scan measures.** Where the home page states the town it serves (`statedPlace`, #1370's rule (4)), the free pass measures there as a paid pass does: every suggestions seed is the service plus the town (`localSearch`) and Google's results, organic and AI Mode, are read from the town on the vendor's list (`placeLocation`, 0¢, waited on for at most 6 s and remembered by the instance; a town the list does not hold is searched by name and read from the country). The same number of searches and the same 12¢ cap. A site that states no town is measured as before. (2) **The local pack counts as found**, on every pass: a search whose local pack shows the site counts toward search presence as a top-ten result — a ranked row for it outside the top ten is moved into it, and a search the ranked rows do not hold is one more the site ranks for — and the presence card counts that search as held. The businesses in a local pack are rival candidates as a top-ten result is (§6.6). (3) **The measurement line names the town**: "Measured in Google, Leeds, in English." (`report.measurement.place`, drafted). (4) **Retrofit.** Stored scans were read nationally and their local searches are not bought again; the re-score (2026-10-14) counts the local pack wherever a stored SERP holds one.
- 2026-10-15  **A free scan reads the town more broadly, and asks every search of it** (owner ruling via the master, issue 1441: "yes, read the town more broadly"; supersedes #1370's rule (4) for where a free scan measures, not for what the report states to setup; each reading is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found: on #1438's run none of eight real cafés and plumbers stated a town the way rule (4) reads one, so the line above reached almost none. A free pass reads the town in the order of the evidence and stops at the first that holds: (1) the structured address, as rule (4) reads it, which stands even where the vendor's list does not hold the town; (2) a business's structured `areaServed` (any business type: `Plumber`, `CafeOrCoffeeShop`, `LocalBusiness`, …) with no address, each town it lists in turn; (3) a structured address written as one string, its town read out of it ("12-16 New Station Street, Leeds, LS15DL" is Leeds; a short code beside a postcode is the region, and a country's name is never the town); (4) the town the home page's own words name (a footer address, "based in", "serving"), answered by the profile call the pass already makes as a new field (`town`), at no extra call. (2) to (4) stand only where the vendor's list of the site's own country holds the town (`placeLocation`), so a made-up or foreign town is dropped; two cities of one name are told apart by the region that holds most of the other towns the business lists ("Austin" beside "San Antonio" and "Round Rock" is in Texas). The report records which evidence named the town (`locale.placeFrom`). **Every search is asked of the town**: for a free scan with a town, a "near me" search and a search that names no place are each asked as the service plus the town (`localSearch`); two that become one are asked once, and each keeps the volume the vendor listed for it. The town is where a search is asked, not part of what the business sells: the selection reads a search without the town's words, as D67 reads a paid pass's. A paid pass is unchanged by this line. *Open:* a town whose own market yields no question (flintplumb.com, Buda, Texas) is measured there all the same; whether it falls back to a larger town or the country is the owner's.
- 2026-10-17  **One reading of place for the free scan and the paid pass** (issue 1480, a slice of #1454, from the read-only scan audit of 2026-10-17; each reading is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found in production: stripe.com measured in Australia, close.com (a CRM) measured in Austin, and nifty.pm read as St Pierre and Miquelon. (1) *Country.* After its ending, a site's country is read in its page's language: structured data, a phone prefix, an address or a currency naming a country whose sites are written in another language says where an office is, not where the buyers the page is written for are (monday.com's English page states an address in Israel). Structured data that lists offices in more than one country names none (stripe.com lists 24, Singapore first). An ending names its ISO country, `.uk` names GB, and an ending sold as a generic name (`.io`, `.ai`, `.co`, `.me`, `.tv`, `.pm` and the rest of `GENERIC_CC_TLDS` in `src/lib/locale/read.ts`) names none; the page's other signals still do. (2) *Town.* A free scan measures in a town only by the rule a paid pass holds its place to (#1370's `servingEvidence`, read through `townHolds` in `src/lib/market/rivals/reach.ts`): a profile read as national, international, software or teaching has no town, and otherwise the home page has to say it serves the place. An address in a footer is where a business is. The profile call is no longer told a footer address is the town. A stored profile's town is read the same way (`profileTown`) by the rival rule (#1468), by *What we read*, and by the re-score. A stored scan keeps the place its searches were bought in until it is scanned again: one whose country now reads differently keeps its country, and a free scan's town that no longer stands keeps its town and the rivals read there, and is listed in `stale_scans` (issue 1504, 2026-10-18: dropping it read SERPs bought in Austin as national and gave close.com austintexas.gov as a rival), at 0¢. (3) *Volumes.* The search vendor holds volumes by country only, so the measurement line of a report measured in a town says its volumes are the whole country's.
- 2026-10-10  **A site drawn by JavaScript is read as a browser draws it** (owner's issue #1374, from the customer panel; the numbers are drafted defaults the owner may correct). Found: baeckerei-hinkel.de serves 801 bytes, an empty `<div id="root">` and a script, for every path, `/sitemap.xml` included. ReachKit reads a page as served and runs no browser, so the pass ended with a knowledge base of 0 pages, and §7 had no fact of the business to write from. Site builders that ship single-page apps (Lovable, Bolt, v0, plain Vite or React) make this an ordinary customer. *The rule.* A plain read comes first, on every pass, and costs nothing. Only where it leaves no page with enough text to read (every page held is under the knowledge base's 200 characters) does the pass buy a rendered read: the search vendor loads the page in its own browser with JavaScript on and hands back the HTML it ended with (DataForSEO On-Page *instant pages*, then *raw HTML*; `renderedPage`, the eleventh function on the closed list). ReachKit still runs no browser. A site that serves its text never reaches this and pays nothing more. *The pages.* The home page first; then the pages the crawl listed, where the site's sitemap is a sitemap and not the shell again; then the links of each rendered page, in the knowledge base's own order (§5, 2026-10-07). A legal, account or hiring page is not bought, and a path the site's `robots.txt` closes to ReachKit is not asked for. *Bounds.* At most 12 pages and 60 seconds a pass, at 0.15¢ a page (`PRICE_BOOK.RENDERED_PAGE_C`, the vendor's charge on 2026-10-10), so 1.8¢ at most, inside the pass's own cap. A rendered read is kept for the own-document window of 7 days. *Who reads it.* The rendered HTML goes through the same page reader as any page (§5's rules on chrome, examples and a page's own questions), into the knowledge base and into the passages a page is written from (§7): a pass whose own pages are all shells reads the rendered pages of the pass that bought them. The score, the inventory and §9's checks still read the page as served, because that is what a crawler that runs no JavaScript gets. Proof, 2026-10-10, `npm run panel -- --sites baeckerei-hinkel.de` (`scripts/panel/runs/2026-10-10-1374.json`): 5 pages drawn for 0.75¢, the knowledge base read from 3 pages where it held 0, every check passed, and the site's pass cost 20.26¢. The other ten panel sites were not run again (D34's €2 a day), and none reaches a rendered read: eight had pages read as served in the runs of 2026-10-07 to 2026-10-10, and the home pages of the two that have never run (wesbos.com, streaksapp.com) each serve more than 200 characters of text (read at 0¢ on 2026-10-10).
- 2026-09-17  Measure again now (owner ruling, #837): no founder waits for Monday. A paid pass that is short widens at once (the steps above). If it still ends *market too small*, the app offers 2–3 broader categories derived from the site's stored profile (its category's head term and shorter tails, the profile's category, vocabulary and offering type) and a field for the founder's own words — in the side panel's notice, and on Overview and Calendar when they are empty for that reason. Picking one saves it as the site's category and re-measures right away as a background deep pass (`scan/run` on a fresh claimed row) with the side-panel loader, whose first draft fills the calendar. A category changed in Settings re-measures right away too. Bound (drafted default, the owner may override): one pass at a time per site (a started pass counts as under way for 30 minutes), and at most 3 deep passes started per site in any 24 hours, the onboarding pass included; a refused press says why in one line and starts nothing. No customer-visible line points at Monday for a thin market.
- 2026-09-17  Paid pass ceiling (#855): a paid pass (deep, weekly) is bounded in time by its own ceiling, `TIMING.paidPassCeilingS` (240 s), read between calls — never the free report's 50 s — inside the job invocation its measurement runs in (`jobsCeilingS`, 300 s). Its twelve SERPs are bought at once. It sizes its rivals and scores its twelve itself; ranked rows served from the cache window keep the date they were measured. A pass a ceiling stopped did not finish reading its market and is never *market too small* (§5).
- 2026-09-18  A per-site daily cap (#885; drafted default, the owner may correct the number). `CAPS.DAILY_SITE_C` (500¢) bounds what **one site** may spend in a UTC day — the shape it was sized from is one deep pass plus a week of drafts (465¢), and the largest day the product’s own rules allow a site is 3 re-measures plus the day’s page (495¢). It is checked in the same seam the product-wide `CAPS.DAILY_PRODUCT_C` (5000¢) is checked in — before every paid call, with the site read off the scan row that call is ledgered against, so no caller can spend around it — and it refuses the existing way: the call is skipped, the pass degrades and ends `spend_ceiling`, nothing throws, and the log line names the site. A site’s own day total that cannot be read holds the call, exactly as the product’s does (#792). A free scan carries no site and is bounded by §2’s free bounds instead. The two ceilings are not interchangeable and neither replaces the other: the product’s is the company’s exposure for a day and still stops a runaway no single site causes — many sites at once, or the free path — while the site’s is what stops one customer being the reason every other customer’s pass, draft and publish is refused until midnight UTC. The owner is told once when either is reached, and once when a free bound fills, with the site or the bound named — never the network key and never the domain.
- 2026-09-16  Verdicts (owner, #795): every published page gets a verdict every Monday. Its acceptance test is recorded on the page when it is published — the opportunity’s own, else top 20 for its search. Its target search is read every week whether or not it is among the twelve, from the site’s own ranked keywords the pass already buys; a week that could not read them says *not measured* for that page. A search is never “no longer tracked”, and a page retired for that reason before this rule is judged again.
- 2026-10-06  **Each page records its intent; verdicts check ranking and AI citation; eight unmet weeks queue a refresh** (owner, D60, #1288). Found: a page's Monday verdict checked only Google's top 20 for its target, some targets had no measured demand, and nothing checked whether AI answers cite the page. (1) Intent is recorded when a topic is decided and stored on the draft: the search the page should rank for, with its volume, and the tracked prompt it should be cited for — the closest current tracked prompt by meaning, or none. A topic with no measured volume and no matching tracked prompt is not written; the day falls through (D26). (2) The Monday verdict checks both: the Google position for the target, and whether the stored AI answers to the matched prompt, from that week's pass, cite or link the page's URL. Both are recorded in `page_verdicts.measured`. (3) A page that has reached neither goal after 8 weekly verdicts gets a refresh or retarget topic queued automatically, by the retarget path of D53. A page published before this gets its intent filled from its opportunity at the next verdict.
- 2026-10-06  **How D60 is carried out** (#1288; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *The intent* is `drafts.meta.intent`: the search and its monthly volume, and the prompt's id and wording. *Measured volume* is a volume above zero; a search measured at zero has no demand to rank for. *Closest by meaning* is decided without a model call: the tracked prompt that holds the largest share of the search's topic words — read as every search is read (stop-words dropped, a plural as its singular) — where it holds at least two of them and 40% of them; two prompts level on that are told apart by which is about least else (the share of the two wordings' words that both hold). A branded prompt may be the match. *Not written* is a stored readiness answer, `no_intent`, so the ranking, the calendar and the evening's run read it as they read every other reason and the day goes to the next ready topic; it applies to every topic with a search (not to a fix), and it holds nothing back on a site that has no tracked prompts yet, whose first weekly pass builds them. *The verdict.* The two goals apply to a page whose test is its search. Either goal reached is the page **working**: in the top 20 for its search, or its own address among the pages that any of the week's answers to its prompt cited, on either engine and either run. A week whose asks of that prompt did not answer records the citation as not measured and the ranking decides alone; a citation decides a week whose search could not be read. `page_verdicts.measured` keeps the place as before and adds `ai` — the prompt's id and whether it cited the page; a movement still compares places only. A recorded intent stands while its prompt is tracked; a page with no intent, no prompt, or a prompt that has left the set (a rotation, or D54's rebuild) is matched again against the set as it is that Monday, and the draft is rewritten with the answer. *Eight weeks* (`INTENT_UNMET_WEEKS`) are eight verdicts on the page's current version, not-judgeable weeks left out, none of them working; a new version starts the count again. Monday's refresh planning queues it, ahead of the other causes: a page whose search has measured volume is refreshed for that search (cause `unmet`); a page whose search has none is retargeted — a new search, at the address that search names (D53, issue 1487) — to the closest search with volume that the current report measured and no live page of the site already targets, by the same word measure; where none is close it is refreshed for the search it has. One open update per page and search, as for every refresh.
- 2026-10-18  **The Google half is about the page** (#1518, from the gap review's B1; a reading of #795 and D60, not a new rule). "In the top 20 for its search" counts only rows whose address is the page's own: the address it answers at, the address its row recorded before its host moved, or an address it moved from by a retarget (#1487). Another page of the same site, the home page included, never places it. For a search among the twelve the week's SERP is read first; where it holds no row for the page, the site's own ranked rows the pass already buys are read to depth 20 before the page reads as absent. A movement compares the page's own places: the previous place is read again from that week's stored report by the same rule, so a row written while the place was the site's best is compared as the page's, at no cost; where that report cannot be read, the recorded figure stands. The AI half (D16) and the four standings are unchanged.
- 2026-09-19  Superseded opportunities (#903, owner). A stored row derived from a report that is no longer current, whose parent topic the new pass **covered** — the searches that pass read: its market set, its widened pool and its twelve — and for which that pass proposed nothing, is **dismissed**. It does not stay open competing for a day or counting in the founder's view of what is planned. Three cases stay open, and each is one of them alone: a row on a topic the pass never read; a row the pass's own candidate joined, which is that pass's plan for the topic; and a row the pass itself derived. A `queued` row, whose draft is being written, is never dismissed here. The pass's cluster collapse is where this is decided, and it reads each stored row's band as today's right-sizing law gives it (#884's `rebandFor`) rather than the band the pass that wrote the row gave it — the readiness step that normally corrects a stale band runs after the derivation, so until this the collapse compared an outsized row against a right-sized candidate on the outsized row's own number (#881). A pass that could not read its market covers nothing and supersedes nothing.
- 2026-09-20  AI-answer measures (owner, #908). (a) A rolling four-week rate — answers that name or link the site over all answers asked — beside this week’s n of 12; named and linked are counted apart. (b) Each published page’s own question may join the tracked set: **at most 24 tracked questions in all** (the twelve plus twelve), the twelve page questions being those whose pages rank nearest the first page, and a page’s question leaves the set after **8 weeks** without a mention (both confirmed by the owner 2026-09-21). The set never grows past the cap, so its cost is flat. (c) *Also appearing for* and *cited pages*, from DataForSEO’s LLM Mentions database, bought as D32 below says. (d) On hosted blogs only: pages fetched by an answer engine for a user’s question, and visitors arriving from one, counted by day, page and engine, shown as “at least”. Training crawlers are not progress and are not shown. All of it sits inside the 40¢ weekly cap. There is still one score. D19 below (2026-10-03) replaces the twelve as the AI input; until the issue that builds it ships, a pass still asks as this line says.
- 2026-10-03  **Tracked buyer prompts, approved** (owner, D19 final, #1127, #1205; "as recommended", after #1162's measured run, `docs/walk/d19-cost.md`). The AI measure is about **25 tracked buyer prompts** per site, generated from the structured market (D28): 5 category, 7 use-case, 4 persona, 4 comparison, 3 problem and 2 branded. The branded prompts are reported apart. They are asked on **2 engines × 2 runs** a week, about 100 asks. The report is a **4-week rolling mention rate with its margin**, plus the site's own URLs cited — never an AI position. At most 20% of the prompts rotate per quarter. They replace the twelve keyword questions as the AI input; the twelve Google searches stay for search. The cost is measured at **12.00¢ a week per site**, inside the 40¢ weekly cap. The baseline of 2026-10-03, on one week's unbranded samples: reachkit.app 0% (0–4%), plausible.io 59% (48–68%). The landing's "12 questions" wording and the score's AI input (AIPresence, §0) change with the issue that builds this; the copy keys whose wording changes are `landing.inside.answers.monday`, `landing.feature.ai.body`, `landing.does.item-1.line` and `landing.feature.ai.meter`.
- 2026-10-03  **No pre-check of the whole prompt set** (owner, D33, #1127, #1205). The one-time pre-check of every prompt is dropped: it cost about 6¢ a site and dropped nothing in 50 answers (#1162). Only the prompts added in the quarterly rotation are pre-checked.
- 2026-10-03  **How a weekly pass asks them** (#1162, implementing D19, D32 and D33; the details below are drafted defaults the owner may correct). The set is built from the market in force the first time a weekly pass runs and is stored with the site (`sites.tracked_prompts`); it is not regenerated, so four weeks of answers are answers to the same questions. A prompt the market no longer yields — it names a rival since dropped — gives its place to one the market now yields, at most a fifth of the set in any 13 weeks. A prompt added that way is pre-checked on its first week's answers and leaves if none of them names or cites the site, a rival or any other site. Every prompt is asked on Google AI Mode and ChatGPT, twice each, at once and beside the twelve, on the standard queue; the two engines no longer ask the twelve in a weekly pass, and a page's own question (2026-09-20) still asks both. Each answer is stored in that week's report as what it said — named, linked, the site's own pages cited, the rivals named — never its text. *Named* is the site's name or domain in the answer; *linked* is one of its pages among the answer's citations. The rate counts the unbranded prompts; the margin is the 95% Wilson interval; share of voice is the site's mentions over its own and its rivals' together. An ask that did not answer leaves the denominator. Unbranded answers that are not comparisons also join the evidence the rivals are re-checked from (D28). "Once a month" for LLM Mentions is once in four weeks, so the rolling window always holds one purchase. The free report's Visibility Score is unchanged: it reads the twelve's AI Overviews, which every pass still buys with the searches. `PRICE_BOOK.AI_MODE_STD_C` is 0.12¢, the vendor's charge (#1203). Every DataForSEO answer carries the vendor's own charge for the call; a call ledgered at a different figure logs one `vendor_cost_mismatch` line with both (#1203).
- 2026-10-03  **Measure again now asks them too** (owner, #1162: "nothing should be on hold until Monday"). A re-measure (§6, #837) also asks the site's full tracked-prompt set, 2 engines × 2 runs on the standard queue, about 12¢ inside the deep pass's own 150¢ cap, and stores the answers as a weekly pass does. The onboarding pass asks none. The rolling window reads **one pass a week** — the newest that asked the prompts, weekly or re-measure — so a week measured twice is not counted twice. Each day's asks are their own samples: a pass is never served an earlier day's answers, and a retry the same day is served what it already bought. The re-measure still asks D29's 8 buyer prompts live beside the set (drafted default: they are quick and the set waits on the queue).
- 2026-10-03  **A weekly report holds only that week's reads** (#1170, from the metrics audit of 2026-10-02; implements the two lines above — "never repeat last week" and "every measured number has a new date"). The weekly reports of 2026-09-21 and 2026-09-28 dated every factor from a stored read of the home page — the first wore 2026-09-17 — because a pass was served any own read up to seven days old. A weekly pass is now served nothing read before its site's own Monday 00:00: the site's own pages, its ranked searches and the twelve's Google searches older than that are read again, so each factor is measured that week and dated that week, or is unmeasured and nulls the score (§0). A retry inside the same week is still served what that week already bought. The AI-answers card and the score's AI input are counted from the same stored searches: wherever a search was read, both are stored, so a report never shows a Presence figure beside "couldn't be measured". No cap moves; a week pays for its own ranked searches (4.8¢) and its twelve searches inside the 40¢.
- 2026-09-20  Ahrefs and Semrush data is not used: their terms do not allow showing it to our customers (research of this date, #908).
- 2026-10-03  **LLM Mentions is bought monthly, and only for a site with a footprint** (owner, D32, #1127, #1205; replaces the weekly purchase of 2026-09-22, #1004). It is bought only for a site whose own ranked footprint (§0) is **30 keywords or more**, and then **once a month**, not weekly. A week without it says so. The purchase itself is unchanged: **one `search_mentions` request per site**, 100 rows at most, at $0.10 the request plus $0.001 a row, billed per request and not per target (#920 pilot). It stores what came back: the questions, the engine that answered, the site’s own pages the answers cite, the vendor’s demand figures and its first- and last-seen dates. It is bought **last** in the pass, after the week’s supply: the cost is recorded against the 40¢ weekly cap like every other call, and a week with no room left skips it and records that it did, so a short week loses the supplementary list and never the calendar. No cap is raised. A week is stored whether it was bought, skipped, failed or not due, because each is a different thing to tell a founder and only a stored row tells them apart.

- 2026-09-29  **Topics by business fit** (owner, #1127). What a page is written for is chosen from the business outward, not from the category's keyword list inward. Every candidate — a measured question, a suggestion, a rival's ranked keyword, an AI-answer gap — carries a business fit (§0), judged once by the pass against the knowledge base and stored on the row; derivation reads it and nothing downstream re-judges it (#884). A target with fit below 2 is never a day of supply (`low_fit`). Ranking is fit first, then the existing demand, intent and effort terms; volume orders targets of equal fit and never lifts a lower one. The quota that forced best, top and vs searches into the twelve is removed, and a problem or how-to search may fill a day at fit 3. Sources, in the order they are offered: (1) AI-answer gaps — a measured question whose answer names a rival and not the site; (2) searches anchored on the product: the product against a named rival, a rival's alternatives, the category for one audience or use, price and cost, an integration; (3) problems the product solves; (4) the buyers' questions from the knowledge base; (5) a category's "best X" only at fit 2 or more and written as an honest comparison. Searches whose top ten share four or more addresses with a target already planned or published are one topic: the second is an update of the first, never a second page. The category the scan measures is inferred from the knowledge base, not from the home and pricing pages alone. The numbers are the owner's (2026-09-29, D8).
- 2026-10-03  **Write targets by business fit first** (owner, #1134; the 25% and the call's model are the owner's of 2026-10-02, the rest of the numbers are drafted defaults the owner may correct). A search at fit 3 is a write target whatever its volume — below the 50 / 20 / 10 floor, at no measured volume, a problem or how-to included — so at fit 3 a `keyword_page` is held to neither the volume floor, the `winnable` band nor a commercial intent (2026-09-15 above). Volume orders targets of equal fit and never excludes a fit-3 one. Write targets also come from the market's own vocabulary (#1159): a rival's alternatives and the product against each rival, the category and each synonym for the buyer and at its price, the category against each not-our-market contrast, and the job as a how-to — and from the searches the pass read but did not ask. One rival's alternatives are not another's: where a search's content words are only a shape once brands are removed ("alternatives", "vs", "pricing"), the brands stay in its `clusterKey()` (2026-09-15 above). Such a proposal is written down only at fit 3, with no volume, position or AI-answer gap claimed for it. *The judgement*: one call a pass, on the Sonnet tier (about 5¢ a site), in the step that derives the pass's opportunities, over what the pass derived, what it proposes and the open rows no pass has judged; the scale is anchored — 3 means the searcher would buy this product instead of what the top results list — and at most a quarter of the searches keep a 3, in the order the judgement ranked them. It is stored on the row (`business_fit`, `source`) and read by readiness and ranking; a pass that got no judgement proposes nothing and derives as before. Rival sizing (#901) holds back 12¢ for it and the typing, was 4¢; no cap moves. *AI-answer gap*: only a measured answer that cites one of the site's rivals and not the site; an answer citing a publisher, aggregator or platform is not one, and a category's "best X" search stays a "best X" whatever its answer cited. *Source* is set in code from the search and its evidence, never from an intent kind: a category's "best X" ranks last in its fit, and the rest are ordered by the existing score. The quota is gone from the twelve as well, and "free" and "cheap" no longer pass the relevance guard unsupported. Since #1133 the judgement is also shown the knowledge base: the offerings and their uses, the audience, the problems and the differentiators.
- 2026-10-03  **Write targets in the buyer's words** (#1208, after reachkit.app's pass of this date kept "buyer discovery tracking pricing" at fit 3 and wrote both "meltwater pricing" and "meltwater cost"; the counts and the 14¢ are drafted defaults the owner may correct). The market's category and synonyms are ReachKit's names for the market, not searches, and fit 3 does not exempt a phrase nobody types. A pass has the small tier write up to 16 buyer searches — at least 6 of them problems or how-tos — from the market's buyer and job and the site's tracked buyer prompts (D19) once it has them, naming neither the product nor a rival. Every search nothing measured — those, the templates of the line above, and the open rows an earlier pass wrote down the same way — then passes one small-tier "would a buyer search this?" check before the fit judgement: one that fails is not proposed, a row that fails is dismissed, and a pass that got no check proposes none of them. *A rival's own price* is a topic only as a comparison: a new page on "meltwater pricing" is never written, and the product against that rival at its price ("reachkit vs meltwater pricing") is proposed in its place. *Same question*: price, pricing and cost are one word in a `clusterKey()`, and the same-question judgement (2026-09-29 below) shows its topics by index and never offers a search itself — shown as two lists, the model paired each search with its own copy and every pair was dropped. Rival sizing holds back 14¢ for the pass's model calls, was 12¢; no cap moves.
- 2026-10-18  **Topics from what buyers were seen asking** (issue 1519, from the SEO master's gap review of 2026-10-18, B2; the owner adopted all its recommendations; the numbers are drafted defaults the owner may correct). Each "People also ask" question and related search on the site's stored SERPs of the last 30 days — the pass's own measured searches and D62's store — is a topic the pass proposes, at 0¢: one line, no closing punctuation, at most 12 words (`ASKED_TOPICS.maxWords`), never the SERP's own search, the questions before the related searches and one from each SERP in turn, at most 24 a pass (`ASKED_TOPICS.max`), after the market's own names and before the market set it read, inside `TOPIC_FIT.maxCandidates`. It must be on the market in force (D59), not a rival's brand alone (D64), well formed (#1314) and not a rival's name asked about as a word (#1506 (5)); "would a buyer type this?" (#1208) does not read it, since Google showed it. The fit judgement scores it like every proposal, and only one at fit 3 is written: a Write row whose evidence carries `askedBeside` — the search it was seen beside and when that SERP was bought. Its content type follows its words (a question a Q&A, "how to" a how-to, "what is" documentation) and its page type is `answer`, unless it names a product's alternatives, price or a "vs". D60 reads it as observed demand, so it is never `no_intent`; every other readiness rule, `already_covered` included, still applies. The panel records each topic's `unready_reason` and how many questions its SERPs showed, and checks that where they showed any, at least three Q&A, how-to or documentation topics are ready; a run stored before this is read by hand. It adds no sentence to any screen.
- 2026-09-29  **One URL per question** (owner, #1131, widening the line above after Google's generative-AI optimization guide of 2026-07-10, which calls a page per wording of a search scaled content abuse). Two candidates are one topic when **either** their stored top tens share four or more addresses, **or** a small-tier judgement, made once in the pass and stored on the row (the survivor's `cluster_key` and `absorbed_queries`), says they ask the same question in different words. Joining is transitive, and applies to planned new pages (Write, Earn); an Improve of a page that exists still clusters by its word key. The second candidate folds into the planned or live page as an update and is never written as a new URL; an open row the join outranks is dismissed, and a queued or live one is never touched.
- 2026-10-02  **One question, once** (#1181, part of #1127, from the dogfood audit; the 30 days is a drafted default the owner may correct). Ranking is by score; family and type (Improve, Earn, then 2026-09-15's Write order) only break a tie. The day a page is drafted, every open new-page row that is one topic with it by the stored evidence above — top tens sharing four addresses, one cluster key, or a search it absorbed — folds into it; the pass's join also runs over the rows it already holds, against each other, and over published topics as well as planned ones. No two consecutive days are one topic: each day takes the best row that is not the topic of the day before, unless no other row is left. A page younger than 30 days is not refreshed; a topic it covers is stored `already_covered`.
- 2026-09-29  **Update first where a page is close** (owner, D15, #1146). An update of a live page has first claim on a day when that page ranks 4–20 for its target, or was named or cited in at least one tracked AI answer in the last four weeks. The update adds sourced facts, quotations or statistics within §7's *What every page carries* blocks, and ranks above any new page of equal or lower business fit, inside §7's pace.
- 2026-10-03  **How update-first is carried out** (#1146, implementing D15; nothing here is a new number). *Offered*: a page of the site's that ranks 4–20 for a measured question's search is offered as an update whatever that question's AI answer said; from 21 to 30 an update is still offered only where the answer ignores the site. *Ranked*: inside one business fit, the update of a close page goes ahead of every other row — new pages and other updates — and fit still goes first, so a new page of higher fit keeps its place. Close is read off the row's own stored position, or off the stored answers to the tracked prompts (D19): one of them cited the page's address in the four-week window. Nothing is bought and nothing is judged. A cited page that ranks for none of the measured questions has no update row, and none is invented for it. *Inside the mix*: the weekly mix (§7, D36) still turns the kind each day and holds an update to two in seven days, so a close update takes the first day the mix gives an update; on a day §7's pace turns to updates, a refresh of one of ReachKit's own live pages still goes first (#1139), and the close update leads the rest. *Written*: the update's brief and draft are told the page is close and to add facts with their source, quotations and statistics from the selected facts, inside the blocks it already carries (§7), inventing none; this instruction rides on the stored position, so an update that is close only because an answer cited it is written to its type's own job.

**Done when** After local Monday, every measured number has a new date and a delta. Empty days show a written cause, not filler. A “not working” cluster publishes no new page for it the next week.

## §7 Calendar and daily publish

**User gets** One calendar of planned / in review / live / waiting. At most one page a day. Whole draft to read, 24 h to stop it from the mail without signing in. Markdown edit with live preview. Published assets link into their own site.

**Rules**
- Autopilot only: generate → veto → publish. No Copilot, no mode picker, on no screen and in no mail (2026-09-11).
- Veto default 24 h, range 1–7 days, never zero. Same window for updates as for new pages (2026-09-11).
- At most one publish a day, and the plan's week (`WEEK_PLAN.days`, seven) a week. Kinds: new post, new page, update of an existing page.
- Fail closed (2026-09-16, #792): when the day's or week's publishes cannot be counted, the page is held, not published, the log says why, and the next tick asks again.
- Every asset links to real inventory pages — three to six, the ones nearest its topic (2026-10-10, *Links*, below) — and to earlier assets in its cluster. No link known to go nowhere. Drafts follow the stored voice. A fact about the business comes only from their site, the profile or the knowledge base; a fact about the market comes only from a public page cited beside it (2026-09-29).
- A closed Markdown subset (with the 2026-09-20 blocks below); one serialiser for screen, copy-as-HTML and copy-as-Markdown. One fix call before review (D41, below); never after. Edits save with no save button.
- A page is written from its context pack and its type's template, and from nothing else (D41, D42, below). A pack short of what the template asks writes no page.
- Publish is one idempotent call to the destination on the customer’s domain. At +24 h: reachable, indexable, in a sitemap, AI-readable.
- Public veto link redeems on GET once. MVP paid service ends at a page on the customer’s own domain (2026-09-11).
- Opportunity status (2026-09-15): a written draft queues its opportunity; a vetoed draft dismisses it.
- 2026-09-17  Target choice (owner walk, #858): a Write or Earn target's competition band reads the search's own keyword difficulty where the vendor gave one — the measurement of that very top ten — beside the top ten's ranked counts, taking the better of the two; above the difficulty ceiling (§6) the target is outsized for every type. So a cold-start site's long-tail question whose small domains nobody sized is still a winnable or reach target, never an unmeasured one.
- 2026-09-16  Draft edits (owner, #789): the editor saves the title, the body and the meta description. Every save re-runs the stage-4 checks (D41) on the edited text. An edit that breaks a rule is still saved, the page says so, and the page is held from publishing on every route until a later save passes. The state and the veto window do not change.
- Stopped drafts (2026-09-16, #788): a date keeps one draft row across every attempt. A draft the checks still stop after its one fix call moves to needs_attention with the checks that stopped it. Regenerate writes that date's draft again on the next hourly draft tick.
- 2026-10-02  **A stopped draft is repaired from its failures, not re-rolled blind** (#1178, part of #1127; the fix call is D41's). A page a check stops is first repaired in code and checked again, at no model cost: an unlinked number is linked to the pack source that states it beside the same word, or its sentence is dropped; a case-study sentence is dropped; a table's placeholder cell takes its column or its product out (#1135, #1250); an opening list is joined into a paragraph and an over-long opening is split after the sentences that fit. The repair is kept if the page then passes, or (since #1252) if it then fails fewer checks and no new one; the one fix call is told each check the page still fails and the sentences that failed it. Every repair is recorded on the row (`drafts.meta.repairs`), per check, cleared or not. Nothing a repair does writes a word.
- 2026-09-16  (#813) A stage that could not run after the date's row exists ends the same way: it is tried once more, then the row moves to needs_attention naming the stage. No second row; Regenerate restarts it.
- 2026-09-18  **Who decides what, once** (owner ruling, #884: *"right-sizing should only occur once, during the deep scan. The calendar content generation should occur based on the information that is passed to it"*). Three layers, and no rule lives in two:
  - **The pass** measures and stores. The ceilings of §6 are applied while it selects its questions, and what it measured — volume, keyword difficulty, the site's own footprint, each rival's size — is written into the report. Nothing downstream re-measures.
  - **Derivation** decides and stores. It bands every target against the site's footprint (`fit_band`) and records whether the target may fill a publishing day at all (`ready`, `unready_reason`) — including §6's right-sizing law, whose reason is `outsized`. A row banded under an older policy is brought up to date from its own stored evidence on the next pass; no vendor call is made to do it.
  - **The calendar, the ranking and the daily job** only read. They order and render what the row already states; none of them re-applies a bar, re-bands a target or holds a filter of its own. A row that reaches the ranked list is a row the stored answer called ready, and a disagreement is a defect to be asserted against, not repaired at read time.
- 2026-09-18  A `not-yet` target never fills a day (owner's dogfood, #881; the enforcement half of §6's right-sizing law). It is excluded from the ranked list rather than weighed 0 in it — a zero weight orders and does not exclude, and where every candidate scored 0 the tie-break handed the day to the biggest keyword: ReachKit wrote "best seo software" (1 000/mo) for a site ranking for 3. An outsized row stays on file, because the site may grow into it, but it is not a day of supply and is not counted towards pages planned; a site whose every target is outsized has zero days of pages, and the calendar and Overview say that is why rather than calling the market used up or unmeasured. A re-measure adds rather than replaces, so a pass's right-sized candidate now takes the place of an outsized row already held on the same topic — a queued row still survives, since its page is already being written.
- 2026-10-02  **A stopped draft frees its date** (owner, D26, #1186; rewrites #833 of 2026-09-17). When a draft still fails after its fix call, the same run tries the next-ranked opportunity for the same date, at most once more, inside `CAPS.DRAFT_C`. The stopped draft keeps its row in needs_attention but no longer holds the date. A topic stopped twice, or skipped by the founder, rests for 28 days (#1179). The founder still resolves a stopped draft from the draft: Regenerate rewrites the one row; Skip rests its topic as above.
- 2026-10-03  How D26 is carried out (#1179). *Stopped* is either cause that rests a row: the checks after the fix call, or a stage that could not run twice (#813). A fix page, which takes one attempt, counts the same. The next topic is tried only where the stopped page wrote a row and the day's `CAPS.DRAFT_C` is not spent; both pages are paid from that one ceiling, and each row records its own cost. The stopped row gives up its date (`scheduled_for` is empty) only when the run goes on to the next topic, so a stopped page nothing replaced still shows on its day, and a second stop holds the date — a re-delivered tick writes no third. A dateless stopped page is found under *Waiting for you* and on Overview, not on the calendar; rewritten or edited until it passes, it takes the first free date from the day its veto window ends. A resting topic stays on file, open and not ready (`resting`, `opportunities.rest_until`), and is not counted as a day of supply; the rest is counted from the stop, and again from a Skip. A rewrite or an edit that passes queues the topic as any written draft does. A page skipped after it entered review still dismisses its topic (#712).
- 2026-10-02  **What the hard rules are for** (owner, D27, #1186): *"If a draft has failed twice, i would be highly concerned to why. the pass criteria should mainly be there to guard the content for obvious violations, and sticking to a structure."* The hard rules exist to stop obvious violations — invented facts, people or reviews; false claims about the business; adverts posing as answers; unsafe markup — and to enforce the page structure. They are not stylistic filters, and a rule that stops good pages is a defect in the rule. A draft that fails twice is an alert to investigate, never a normal outcome: its causes are reported per week (#1184), and the rules are reviewed against this purpose (#1178 and the issue that review files).
- 2026-10-03  **The rules, reviewed against that purpose** (#1187; D27 above, and D34: stored drafts and stored evaluation pages only, nothing spent). Each rule states what it guards and was re-run on the 12 stored drafts and the 10 kept evaluation drafts; the table and every reading are `docs/walk/rule-review.md`, rewritten by `npm run eval:content -- --rules`. The fifteen that remained of the sixteen measured are wrong on none of the 22 pages; D41 below keeps those that code runs and retires the rest (`required_blocks`, #1137, joined after the measurement and asks nothing of a stored page). **Removed:** `no_private_figure` — it failed any number that also stood somewhere in the stored report and was wrong all 14 times it fired ("24-hour", "12 questions"); a measured figure no source states is a number no fact holds, which `traceable_numerals` stops. **Narrowed:** the brand gap is the page's first sentence and the headings above it, no longer 300 characters, and decides nothing where the target search names the business; `page_frame` is the title or half or more of the headings, and the count of mentions is gone (one heading naming the business is the section *What every page carries* requires) — both still retire with #1136; a testimonial is a stated opinion, rating or recommendation or an unlinked block quote, never the word "review"; a case study has to be claimed or offered as evidence (bylines and dates left that rule with #1137); a cited page's title inside its link is not the page's own claim of a test; a figure the page cites beside a link may be repeated without one, a year is not a number, and digits glued to a letter are a name; with no answer box, an opening inside 60 words answers even past 320 characters. **The alert:** when a draft still fails after its fix call the owner is mailed the site, each check and the sentences that failed it, on the ops mail of §8; the weekly count by cause is #1184's.
- 2026-10-02  Fixing a stopped page (owner, #1180; D26 above): the draft screen of a page that rests in needs_attention without ever entering review offers *Edit*, *Write it again* and *Skip* — the exception to #882's "every other stage opens as a read". An edit saves and re-checks as #789 says; a save that passes every stage-4 check moves the page into review on a fresh veto window, told by the draft-ready mail as a generated page is. A page that entered review and came back to needs_attention is read and offers *Skip* only.
- 2026-09-16  Daily decision (owner, #777): each day chooses between a *new post* (Write/Earn) and an *update of an existing page* (Improve/Fix), whichever ranks higher for this site. Update candidates are the site’s own pages — its ranked URLs and crawled inventory, not only the home page — matched to the market’s questions; both sides are right-sized per §6. An empty day is still a stated cause, never filler.
- 2026-09-16  (#781) An update is offered only where the site's destination can deliver it: otherwise it is not ready (`destination_cannot_address`) and never fills a day. A hosted destination updates a page ReachKit published there — a new version at the same address, served in place of the old one and listed once; a page outside ReachKit is not updatable there. WordPress keeps updating any page of the site.
- 2026-09-17  (#855) A `fix_page` the site's destination cannot deliver — every one on a hosted-only site — is never the day's work and never supply: it is stored not ready (`destination_cannot_address`), no draft is written from it, and supply depth does not count it. It fills a day only where a WordPress destination can deliver it.
- Hosted index (2026-09-16): the root of a hosted host lists every live page, newest first, with a search; a site with nothing published says so instead of answering 404.
- 2026-09-18  Why a page needs you, and the act that matches (owner walk, #880): a page resting in needs_attention states its own cause, read off its last move into that state — the writing rules that stopped it, a step that could not run, a delivery no retry clears, or a publish tried to exhaustion. One written sentence per cause, the same on the calendar and on Overview. *Reconnect* is offered only where the page is waiting on delivery **and** the destination carries a credential **and** that credential is broken; a hosted destination is never offered it and is never shown a WordPress sentence — it is pointed at where its pages publish. A page the rules stopped is read or written again, never reconnected. The pass records the cause; no surface derives it from a stage.
- 2026-10-03  Why pages stall, counted on the calendar (#1184; the sentence is drafted, the owner corrects it). Under the statement of supply the calendar carries one line: the pages written for the last seven dates, how many of them stopped before review, and the writing rule that stopped the most pages. It reads the same record as the line above (#880) and shows nothing in a week where no page stopped.
- 2026-09-18  Every written page is readable (owner walk, #882): from the calendar, a page that has a draft opens the page as written, at every stage — in review, needs-you, scheduled and live alike. A page still generating has no text yet and offers none; an empty day offers nothing (REQ-043 c11). The stage in review keeps its own word, "Read the full page"; the others state plainly that they open the page as written. The screen it opens offers only what that state allows: a page in review can be approved, edited and vetoed, and every other stage opens as a read.
- 2026-09-18  A hosted page follows its site's host (#888; drafted default, the owner may correct it). A hosted page is its `publications` row and that row belongs to a site, so every live page is served at whatever host the site's hosted destination carries **now** — a host that changes under live pages (the owner's test destination on a deploy, a customer repointing their record) re-serves all of them at the new address. The address the page declares as canonical, the entry its sitemap carries, §9's recorded live URL, the link in the `published` mail and the address the 24-hour check fetches are that one current address, never the one stored when it was published. The old host is no longer a host ReachKit serves: it answers **404** — never 410, which would claim a page was taken down when it is live, and never a redirect or another site's page, because a host we can no longer prove is this customer's must never serve their content. A page is never recorded `page_not_found` because its host moved. A WordPress page is on a host this product does not own: its stored address is the only one there is and is never recomposed.
- 2026-09-18  A published page is inert (#888). The body is model-written markdown on the customer's own domain: the one renderer escapes every text node and emits only its own closed set of elements, so `<script>`, an `onerror` attribute and an embedded iframe render as the visible text a reader would expect, and a link whose scheme is not one of the four addressable ones renders as its label with no address at all. There is no sanitiser to call and no allow-list to keep: nothing from a body reaches the served page as markup.
- 2026-09-17  Plan horizon (owner, #857): the calendar plans from today to the day before the next weekly pass — through that week's Sunday in the site's own zone, and on a Monday through the coming Sunday whether or not the pass has run. Only those dates carry a planned page or an empty day's account. A past date shows what happened on it (published, stopped, skipped, held) and nothing else. A date after the horizon carries no planned page and no line; one quiet marker (“Planned after Monday’s scan”) stands on its first date. A supply state is stated once, at the top of the calendar, and never repeated per cell or in the day panel; a law cause (ReachKit stopped) is stated on the first empty date in the horizon only. The daily draft job agrees: it writes one draft, for tomorrow, from the current pass's ranking — so Sunday evening's draft for Monday is written before Monday's pass, and nothing further ahead is ever drafted.
- 2026-10-15  **The plan is the next seven days** (issue 1455, owner: *"the calendar is essentially empty"*; replaces #857's horizon, the Sunday before the next weekly pass, which on a Saturday planned one day). The calendar plans today and the six days after it in the site's own zone, each with its planned page or update and its stage. A date planned past Monday's pass is planned from the ranking that stands today, and the pass may plan it again. On autopilot no date in the plan is empty unless the customer stopped publishing or supply has truly run out, which the calendar states once. The quiet marker on the first date past the plan reads *Planned later* on the card and *Planned a week ahead, closer to the day* in the panel (drafted; the owner corrects the wording). A pending market change (§6, `sites.market.pending`) holds no draft: it is adopted or dropped by the next pass, and the draft tick does not read it.
- 2026-10-08  **An untouched page publishes on its calendar day** (#1337; a drafted default the owner may correct). A page publishes at the first publish time at or after its veto window ends, so a page written at one fixed evening hour went out a day after the date the calendar gave it. The daily draft job now writes a site's page for date D in the hour whose start plus the site's veto window is at, or less than an hour before, D's publish time in the site's own zone, and the window is counted from the start of that hour. This replaces the 18:00 local hour, and #857's "one draft, for tomorrow": with a window of more than a day the page is written that much further ahead, still one a day. A page whose window opens at any other moment (the first draft after onboarding, a setting just changed, a page written again or edited until it passes) takes the first date whose publish time is at least the window away; where that is later than the date its row holds, the row moves to it, or to the first free date after it. The calendar, Overview and the draft-ready mail name that day, which is the day `told.publishesAt` falls on. A page already in review when the fix shipped is not re-dated. Not covered here: an approval, which still publishes at the first publish time after it, and a settings change to a page already in review.
- 2026-10-08  **Autopilot asks for nothing** (owner: "this must be autopilot, not requiring user approval or interaction"; #1337). No screen and no mail says or implies the customer must act for a page to publish. A page in review is stated as *ready to read*: it goes live on its own at the stated time, and can be read or stopped until then. The side panel's card and Overview's card state the moment the customer was told (`told.publishesAt`), falling back to the window's end. Wording is drafted; the owner may correct it.
- 2026-10-08  **No Approve, autopilot only** (owner: "remove the approve button entirely, autopilot only. this should also be reflected in notifications and messaging to ensure we never imply to the user that their approval is required. they can solely edit or stop the content"; #1337). This replaces *Approve* in the 2026-09-20 line "Every action answers" (§4). On an unpublished page the customer's only actions are **Edit** and **Stop**. There is no Approve control, server action or route (the customer's approve route under the drafts API is removed). The system's own path is unchanged: the veto window ending moves a page `in_review → approved → published`. There is no copilot: a stored `sites.mode = 'copilot'` is read as autopilot, a save that asks for copilot is refused, and the copilot telling, its mail lines and the settings re-deadline rule for it are removed. A page told under copilot is owed a new telling before it publishes. Every screen and mail about a page that has not gone out says it goes live on its own at the stated time and can be edited or stopped until then; the stage word is *Ready* on Plan and *Ready to read* on Overview. A test reads every copy key, in English and German, for approval wording against a commented allowlist.
- 2026-10-10  **The draft screen shows every check** (owner: "go"; #1394). The Checks headline reads *Checks — N of T passed*, where T is the number of hard rules in code and N is the number the last recorded run asked of this page and passed. Under it is one closed note, *How we decided*, holding every hard rule in plain words in three groups: Facts, Honesty, Structure. Each rule is read from what generation recorded (`drafts.rule_failures`, and `drafts.meta.checks` for which rules the run asked and which it failed first and then cleared). A rule the run did not ask of this page — the comparison rule on a page that is not a comparison — is drawn as *Not needed for this page* and is never counted as passed; a rule it failed is drawn as *Not passed*. A page the run fixed says how many rules it fixed, as *N fixed automatically*. A page written before the run was recorded states no count and draws only the rules every run asks. The never-claim rule and the grounded fact are answered live, as before. No number is typed into the copy: the total is the length of the rule list and the near-repeat ceiling is `NEAR_DUPLICATE_MAX`. A drafted default the owner may correct: the headline is followed by the counts of rules not needed and not passed, so that a total below T is explained at a glance.

- 2026-09-19  **A page answers the question it targets** (owner, #900, from the live re-measure of reachkit.app). Both drafts this product had ever written were advertisements for the site that pays for it, grounded in its own price list; three hard rules stopped both, which is the safety net working and the writing failing. Three things follow, and none of them weakens an existing rule:
  - **What the page is for is stated, per type.** Each content type's template opens with one sentence saying what its page must do (D42, below), and the writer is handed it. For a listicle or comparison on a question a reader brings from the market — "best X", "which X", what to choose — that job is to help the reader choose: what the options are, what distinguishes them, which case each suits. How the customer's own product appears is set by the page's type (2026-09-29, *honesty by page type*).
  - **Grounding carries more than one page.** The business facts a context pack holds are read from the customer's pages in turn — one passage per page per round, never one page emptied before the next is opened — and where a target search is known, the pages whose words it shares are offered first. A page on a market question then stands on a fact about the question rather than on eight sentences off the price list.
- 2026-09-20  **What a page is written from.** The confirmed business profile — what the business does, sells and promises, who it serves, its vocabulary — joins the writer’s inputs, beside the voice, the target search, the facts read from the site and the site’s own pages. Facts follow the two pools of 2026-09-29; no invented people, reviews or figures.
- 2026-09-20  **Known content.** Before a page is planned or written ReachKit knows every page it wrote for the site, every post on a connected WordPress (read through the connection when it is made and every Monday — all posts, not only ours), and the main site’s crawled pages. A planned page that overlaps one of them becomes an **update** of that page where the destination can publish to it; otherwise the topic is skipped and the existing page is linked from related pages. New pages link to known content where it helps the reader. The page’s checks say it is not a repeat and name the closest existing page.
- 2026-09-20  **Plan.** A month with a closable day panel. A day card holds the date and exactly one number (searches a month before the page is live, its position and change once it is), the title cut to two lines, and one stage word (Live, Review, Scheduled, Planned, Needs you) with a mark for an update. It is the same height at every width and nothing wraps past it. A day with no page says why in at most two words; the day panel carries the full reason. The day panel is label-and-value rows — search, AI answers, deadline, actions — never sentences. On a phone the month is a list of days and the day opens as a sheet. (The phone half is replaced by 2026-10-08, below; the day still opens as a sheet.)
- 2026-09-20  **The page as it publishes.** The review screen shows the whole page exactly as readers and search engines receive it, in the customer’s own page frame, with beside it: why this page; how it appears in search results; *written from your business* — each fact, quote and promise used and where it was read; what gets published (address, title, description, structured data, links, length); the checks. A switch labels what each part of the page is for and underlines the lines an AI answer is most likely to quote.
- 2026-09-20  **Richer blocks.** The published subset gains a table, a short-answer box and related guides, in the first release of the new viewer; a `:::takeaways` block joins the closed set for the key takeaways (owner, 2026-09-29, D4). One serialiser still serves screen, hosted page, WordPress and copy-out, and every character of text is still escaped. No images in the body; the generated share image of 2026-09-29 is not a body image.
- 2026-09-20  **Editor.** Text on the left, the live page on the right, a desktop/phone switch, a formatting bar, title and search-result description with a length count. A sentence that breaks a hard rule or matches the never-claim list is marked where it stands as it is typed; the save-and-hold behaviour of 2026-09-16 (#789) is unchanged. On a phone: Text / Preview tabs.
- 2026-09-20  **Wording.** Screens and mail say *Stop this page*. “Veto” remains the internal word and the name of the mechanism in this file.
- 2026-09-20  Hosted pages count answer-engine fetches and answer-engine referrals (§6 of this date) — server-side, no cookie, no visitor identity, no client script. Nothing else about a visit is kept.

- 2026-09-21  **Plan opens on today from `md` up** (owner, #961). REQ-043 c7 is a rule about a screen with room for a panel beside the month: on a phone an address with no `?day` is closed, and a tap opens the day as a sheet. A phone that opened a sheet over the month on arrival would hide the thing the reader came to see. (Since 2026-10-08, below, the phone ends at the large breakpoint, so this is from `lg` up.)
- 2026-09-21  **Settings › Your business marks what is new, in place** (owner, #969). A fact the weekly re-read newly finds appears in its own list marked *new* until the customer touches anything on the screen; there is no separate suggestions area to review. A curated list stays curated: the mark is how a new finding arrives, not a second place to find it.
- 2026-09-24  Plan and the page take the calm layer, keeping their content (owner, #1059 (2026-09-24)): on Plan, empty days are flat and unbordered, filters are quiet chips, the month control is one group, and the *couldn't find enough searches* notice carries its own two actions; on the page, a sticky *Decide* panel holds the live countdown, *Approve* as the one solid button, *Edit* and *Stop* beside each other, and the checks as they finish.
- 2026-10-07  **Every screen is a short dashboard; a plan day leads with its actions** (owner, #1326). The owner's complaints about one screen apply to the whole application: every screen is a short dashboard, the first thing on it is the number or the action, and anything longer opens from that.
