On Plan, a day that has a page shows the title and then its actions. The search rows and the AI answers rows start closed behind one line, *Why this page*, and open in place. A day with no page is unchanged: it states its one account and offers no publish.
- 2026-10-07  **The page stays the page, and the notes under it start closed** (owner, #1325). The concrete complaints apply to the whole application. Every screen is a short dashboard. The first thing is the number or the action. Anything longer opens from that. A skeleton is the shape of the screen that arrives, on a phone and on a desktop. On the review screen the article is what *Read it* opened: it is drawn whole, behind no second tap, and is never truncated. What the 2026-09-20 line puts beside it — why this page, how it appears in search results, *written from your business*, what this page adds, what gets published — and the page record are notes: each is one closed line under the article, named by its own title, and opens in place (`docs/DESIGN.md`, *Closed sentences*). Below the `xl` breakpoint the *Decide* panel stands between the article and the notes, so the decision is never under them, and below `md` its three controls stay the bar at the foot of the screen. From `xl` the panel stays beside the page, and the notes stay on the page's side. The waiting state is the page in its frame, the panel and the closed lines, in those places at each width. *What this page adds* opens by itself only where it asks the founder for a fact, because that sentence is the action (a drafted reading the owner may correct).
- 2026-10-08  **On a phone Plan is the recent days and the days ahead** (owner). *"the plan does not need to show a full month but rather the recent and future days. loading screens should show the actual skeleton, not the desktop skeleton."* Below the large breakpoint the list is the site-local week that contains today: Monday through the plan horizon, the Sunday `planHorizonEnd` already returns. Days outside that week are not in the list. The waiting state below the large breakpoint is that list, not the month grid and not a thirty-day list. From the large breakpoint up the month grid and its skeleton stay. A day opened by its own address still opens.
- 2026-10-09  **Plan and the page screen open with their numbers** (owner, #1364; §4's line of this date). *Plan*: under its name stand four counts, *Live*, *Ready*, *Scheduled* and *Planned*, the same counts the stage chips carry: of the month from the large breakpoint, of this week below it. A count of pages is a count, so a zero is drawn, quietly. The statement of supply, the chips and the days follow. *The page screen*: under the title of a page written for a search stand two numbers the pass measured when it chose the page, *Searches a month* and *Difficulty* beside this site's limit, and the article follows whole, as the 2026-10-07 line (#1325) has it. A page with no market target (a fix, or a target that could not be read) draws no row. The note *What this page is for* stays closed below and states the same two readings. The wording is drafted (#759); the owner may correct it.
- 2026-09-29  **Two pools of facts, honesty by page type** (owner, #1127). A context pack holds two pools. *Business facts*: passages from the customer's site and the knowledge base, word for word, linked to the page each was read from (a founder's answer is attributed to the founder). *Market facts*: claims a research step reads, at writing time, from public pages — the target's top results, the named rivals' own pricing, feature and documentation pages, and primary studies — each kept with its address and the date it was read, and cited beside the sentence that uses it. A market statistic older than three years is not used. Every number on a page traces to a fact in either pool; a year is not a number for this rule. A quotation is admitted only word for word from a cited source or from a testimonial on the customer's own site, with its link. A business fact used word for word renders as a quotation with a small link to its source and is never paraphrased (owner, 2026-09-29, D5). A page cites no other company's scan score until #1134 re-measures (owner, 2026-09-29, D6). How the business appears follows the page's type: on a page anchored on the product (the product against a rival, alternatives, for an audience, price, a problem it solves) the product is the subject; on a market comparison (owner, 2026-10-02, D20, #1136, #1166) favourable framing is allowed but never a self-ranked "#1 best": the customer leads the comparison table and the verdict and its strengths are stated first, the verdict recommends it for the audience and use it genuinely fits per the knowledge base, rivals' strengths are still stated, every claim is sourced, nothing is invented, and the page never claims a fit the knowledge base does not support. The owner's note on #1136: *"fairly compare companies and give strong advice but simultaneously promoting our own users app mostly. We must not fake anything."* Why no self-ranked list: SaaS sites carrying 191–340 self-promotional "best X" lists lost 29–49% of their visibility (Lily Ray, Feb 2026), and self-promotional lists get 19% of AI citations against 81% for independent ones (Semrush). `page_frame` and the 300-character brand gap are retired for this rule.
- 2026-10-03  **How the two pools are carried out** (#1135; the bounds and the two sentences are drafted, the owner corrects them). At stage 2 (D41), a research step reads public pages for the page's target: up to three readable results of the stored top ten (never the customer's own), each of up to four named rivals' **own** pricing and feature pages (on a listicle up to eight, an alternatives page's subject among them, owner 2026-10-18, issue 1508, below), and at most two studies found by one search. A rival's page is tried at its usual pricing and features addresses; where one does not answer, the rival's home page is read once for a link to it (owner, 2026-10-02, round 1: *"the app clearly has a pricing page to reference"*). (Since D56, below, no address is tried: the rival's own links and sitemap name the pages.) A claim is kept only where its words are on the page it names, word for word, with that page's address, title and read date; a statistic whose data is older than three years is dropped. Every read is ledgered and free, and a page is not read again within seven days; the claim calls and the one search are paid inside `CAPS.DRAFT_C`, and a read that fails never stops a page — it is then written from its business facts alone. The pack carries every kept claim, and a page that compares options is handed at least three where research found them, the rivals' own pages first. The rules follow the pools: a number passes where a fact of either pool states it, and a year is not a number; a figure about a rival passes beside a link to a page one of the page's facts was read from, and beside no other link; a block quote passes only word for word from a fact the page was handed, with its link; the word "review" alone stops nothing; and a number a fact states is not a private figure whatever the report also counts (that rule, `no_private_figure`, was removed the same day: #1187, above). A rival's figure left without its page is linked in code to the fact's page that states it (#1178's repair). Market facts are cited inline, and code — never the model — ends the page with a *Sources* list of each market page the body links, with the date it was read; the rules do not read that list. **A table never ships with an empty or "not stated" cell** (owner, 2026-10-02, round 1): the product or the criterion with the largest share of empty cells is taken out until none is left. Nothing is converted, because no exchange rate is a fact in either pool; a price keeps the currency its page states (D56, below, which replaced "a table keeps one currency"). The market facts a page was handed are recorded on its row (`drafts.meta.marketFacts`), and an edit is checked against them. The two sentences are new keys (`body.sources.heading`, `body.sources.read`).
- 2026-10-03  **The report's measurements are business facts** (#1163; the sentences are drafted, the owner corrects the wording). What the site's latest complete report measured joins the business facts: its Visibility Score, for how many of the buyer questions AI answers named the site, the up to three rivals those answers cited most, and how it was measured — the engines that pass asked, and that Perplexity is not measured (§11). Each is one sentence composed from the stored figures, dated by the measurement and sourced to the report's own address; nothing is estimated, an unmeasured figure states nothing, and a pass that stopped early is not quoted. They are offered after the site's own pages and never alone: a site whose pages yield no fact is not written from its measurements. The founder switches one off like any other fact. The four sentences are new keys (`fact.report.score`, `.answers`, `.rival`, `.method`).
- 2026-10-17  **The site's own measurements appear only on research pages** (owner, issue 1490: *"Only on research pages."*; amends #1163 above; the readings are drafted defaults the owner may correct). Found: ordinary pages printed the site's own measurements ("0 of N", "18/100"), which publishes the customer's weakness. The four sentences of #1163 join a context pack only where the page is a research page, the template that aggregates them (`reportFactsAllowed` in `pipeline/report-facts.ts` decides by type); on every other type they are not in the pack, and D12's business fact is then a fact of the site's own pages or a founder's answer, never a measurement. `traceable_numerals` fails a page that is not research where a sentence states one of the report's measurements — a count of a total ("4 of the 12", "4 von 12", "18/100", "18 out of 100") or a score ("a Visibility Score of 18") — linked or not, and the repair drops that sentence; a bare number is not read as a measurement (#1187). A live page that is not research and states one is due a refresh for a cause of its own, `own_figures`; the refresh is written without those sentences and at 0¢ of new measurement, and §6's floor stands for it. D6 stands: no other company's scan score, on any page.
- 2026-09-29  **What every page carries** (owner, #1127). A byline: the founder's name and line from setup, else the business's name; ReachKit's own pages read "Tim Clifford · Founder, ReachKit" (owner, 2026-09-29, D1). The published date, and an updated date once a page is refreshed. Under the title, a short-answer box of 40–60 words that answers the target plainly, then three to five key takeaways. Headings written as the questions a reader asks, H2 then H3 in order, each section opening with its own two- or three-sentence answer. A table wherever options, prices or plans are compared. A section on how the business itself handles the question, with links to its product, pricing and the cluster's hub page. Three to six questions buyers ask, stored as `drafts.meta.faq`. A sources list naming every market fact's page. A closing line that points to the one action this page's reader should take. Which of these blocks a type must carry, and its sections in order, is that type's template (D42, below): one fixed template a type.
- 2026-10-03  **How *What every page carries* is carried out** (#1137; the owner's round-1 review of 2026-10-02: *"Enforce these in code, not only in the prompt"*; sentence case, the two-repeat cap and the banned-text list are drafted defaults the owner may correct). The writer returns the page in the template's parts and code lays them out: the `:::answer` box, the `:::takeaways` box, each section under its heading in the template's order, the closing line last, and the buyer questions in `drafts.meta.faq`, never in the body the writer returns (code draws them on the page, 2026-10-17 below). The short answer is counted in words on the finished page; outside 40–60 it fails the blocks check. That check, `required_blocks`, stops a generated page that lacks a block its template requires, has its sections out of the template's order, or prints a prompt's fact label (`[F7]`, `[M39]`). It asks nothing of a founder's own edit. Cut in code before the checks read the page: fact labels (one used as link text becomes the source's host); the word "actually"; a pull-quote that is not a pack passage word for word; a sentence of legal, removal or refund text, unless the target search asks about it; a demo caption; a byline or date line in the body; and a third or later sentence repeating one promise of the business. Titles and headings are sentence case across the site. Only an invented case study is stopped as provenance; a byline and the page's dates are the page's own. The byline is recorded on the row (`drafts.attribution`) when the page is written — the founder's line from setup (#1133), else the business's name — and printed with the published date and, once the page is refreshed, the updated date: under the title on the hosted page, and as the first paragraph of a WordPress post. The sources list is #1135's, written in code under the closing line; `## Sources` is the one heading that need not be a question. Strings: the takeaways box's label and the published-and-updated line (`body.takeaways.heading`, `hosted.updated`).
- 2026-09-29  **Length and the writer** (owner, #1127; rewritten by D41). Each type's template states its length in words of body text (D42, below): a comparison 1,500–2,500 and a how-to 1,200–2,000 are the owner's of this date, and an update keeps within 20% of the page it updates. The page is one call on Sonnet 5; research may stay on the small tier. Words are counted in code on the page as it is laid out, and the writer, the length check and the evaluation's grader read the one range. A page outside its range fails the length check and takes the fix call like any other failure. The per-page cap stays `CAPS.DRAFT_C`; no cap is raised. The floors stay: what a page adds, not its length, is what protects rank (owner, 2026-09-29, D11). The lead's free first page (§2) is paid under `CAPS.FREE_C`.
- 2026-09-29  **Pace: new pages and refreshes** (owner, #1127). A week publishes at most four new pages; the other days carry updates of pages already live — ReachKit's own first, where a page is 90 days old, has lost its position, or a newer fact exists — or the site's own pages the destination can update. Every day still carries one asset (§0: a new page or an update), so "one page a day" stands, and a refresh counts as the day's page (owner, 2026-09-29, D3). A site with no page yet old enough to refresh fills the week with new pages.
- 2026-10-03  **How the pace is carried out** (#1139; the seven-day window and what counts as a newer fact are drafted defaults the owner may correct). The four are counted over any seven days, like the mix above: a day whose six days before it hold four new pages takes the highest-ranked update — a refresh of one of ReachKit's own live pages first, then an Improve of the site's own pages — and takes a new page only where no update is ready. On such a day the mix's two-a-week count and its turn of types give way, so update days may follow one another. Each Monday, after the verdicts, a refresh is planned for every live page ReachKit wrote whose current version is 90 days old, whose newest verdict records a worse place, or that has a newer fact: a page of the site one of its recorded facts was read from has been read again since and no longer says it, or a later page recorded a claim, read after this version went live, from a public page this one cites. §6's 30 days stand for all three. The refresh keeps the page's search, band, fit and acceptance test, is one open row a page, and publishes as a new version at the same address, which is what sets the updated date (#781, #1137). Plan shows it with the update mark it already has. No new string.
- 2026-09-29  **The hosted blog carries the customer's identity** (owner, #1127; its board joins `docs/DESIGN.md` with #1140). Every hosted page has a header with the customer's logo and name, linking to the blog's index, and a button to their main site; an article ends with a short about-the-business card that links to the product; related guides are shown at its foot; a breadcrumb names the site, the blog and the page. A leading heading in the body that repeats the title is dropped, and the body's headings sit under the title. Metadata reads in the text face, not mono, and carries reading time and the updated date. Headings carry ids the renderer mints from their text, for a table of contents that is sticky beside the article from `lg` up and a collapsible block below it. The index opens with the business's name and one line, then the newest page, then the rest as cards, paged. Each page has a generated share image (title, the customer's logo and domain), share tags, `BlogPosting` and breadcrumb structured data with dates, an RSS feed, and the index in the sitemap. Nothing on a hosted page names ReachKit.
- 2026-10-04  **How the identity is carried out** (#1140; the boards are *Hosted article* and *Hosted index* in `docs/DESIGN.md`; the numbers and the choice of line are drafted defaults the owner may correct). The name is the one the site calls itself (`site_profiles.site_name`), else the domain. The logo is the one the scan kept, served from the customer's own host (the `logo.img` route under `(hosted)`), else the name's first letter on a tile; a hosted page asks neither ReachKit's address nor the main site for a picture. The one line about the business is the founder's answer to *what you do that rivals don't*, else the first thing the knowledge base says sets it apart, else the first claim the profile kept; a business with none shows no line. Reading time is the body's words at 220 a minute, never under one minute. The table of contents lists the body's sections and the level under them, and is drawn only where there are two or more. Related guides are up to three of the site's other live pages, closest in topic to this one first and then newest. The index shows twelve cards a page under the newest page, with `?page=` on the address; a search has no featured page. The share image, share tags, `BlogPosting` and breadcrumb data, the feed and the sitemap entry are #1141's.
- 2026-10-04  **How the share image, the data and the feed are carried out** (#1141; the feed's fifty pages, the image's hour and the index's fallback sentence are drafted defaults the owner may correct). Every article and the index name a share image on the customer's own host (the `share.img` route under `(hosted)`): the page's title, the customer's logo and name, and their host; the index's carries the index's heading and the business's one line. It is drawn by `next/og` and is kept for an hour. A logo the renderer cannot draw — any kept format but PNG, JPEG or GIF — is the first-letter tile, as in the header. The head of both carries Open Graph and Twitter card tags (title, description, address, the business's name, the image; an article also its published and updated times) and a link to the feed. The structured data restates what the page shows and nothing more — it is hygiene, not a lever (owner, 2026-09-29, after Google's AI-features and optimisation guides: no special markup, no `llms.txt`, no Markdown copy). An article carries `BlogPosting` — headline, the two dates it prints, the share image, its author and its publisher — and a `BreadcrumbList` of the three parts its breadcrumb draws; its `FAQPage` restates the questions the page draws (2026-10-17, below). The author is the byline: a recorded one is a person, named by the part before its ` · ` with the rest as their line; a page with none is authored by the business. The publisher is the business in the header, with its logo only where one is kept. The index carries `Blog` with its `WebSite`, naming the pages that page of the list shows. The index's description is the business's one line, else *Articles and guides from {business}.* (`hosted.index.description`, drafted). The feed is RSS 2.0 at `rss.xml` on the customer's host, written by the `feed` package: the fifty newest live pages, each with its title, address, description and date, never cached, and naming no generator. The sitemap lists the index first, dated by its newest page, once the site has a live page. **No CDN caching yet**: a hosted page is chosen by the request's Host and counts answer-engine reads in its render (§6, 2026-09-20), so it is rendered on every request; caching it needs both moved out of the render, which is #1242.
- 2026-10-17  **The FAQ is shown on the page, and the structured data states only what is visible** (owner ruling of 2026-10-17: *"Show the FAQ on the page."*; issue 1489; supersedes #1137's "never in the body" for the buyer questions only). Found: pages whose `FAQPage` held questions no reader could see (SEO review of 2026-10-17, item 2). The writer still never writes the questions into the body: code draws `drafts.meta.faq` as a section of its own, headed *Questions buyers ask* (`body.faq.heading`, drafted) at the body's top level with each question one level under it, above the sources list, else last — on the hosted page and as part of a WordPress post's content alike (`render/faq.ts`). A question the body already asks as a heading, as a Q&A page's do, is not drawn twice. Every JSON-LD field that states text — `FAQPage`, `BlogPosting`, `BreadcrumbList`, and `HowTo` should one ever be emitted — restates text the page shows: `FAQPage` is built from the very blocks the section draws, and `BlogPosting` no longer carries the meta description, which the page does not print. A page stored before this line shows its questions on its next render, at no cost; a WordPress post shows them when it is next delivered. The voice gate, the hard rules and the pace are unchanged.
- 2026-10-09  **A live hosted article is cached for a minute** (#1242, from #1141 item 7; the minute and the script policy are drafted defaults the owner may correct). A live article is rendered once and then served from the cache for up to 60 seconds. A publish or an unpublish on its site clears it at once. Three things are decided on every request, before the cache, in `src/middleware.ts`. A page taken down and a site whose access ended answer 410. The index, an address with no page, a host the site has moved away from and a read that did not answer in time are rendered for that request and are not cached. An answer engine's fetch or referral (#921) is counted there, so a read of a cached page is still counted. Only a page found live on that request is sent to the cached copy, and the cached copy has no other way in. The cached article is answered under `script-src 'none'`: a nonce must be new on every request and a cached page is the same on every request, the article needs no script, and so none runs on it. The sitemap, the feed, the robots document, the share image and the 410 are not cached by this line.
- 2026-10-11  **A published page says how it was made** (owner, 2026-10-10: "go"; #1397; the sentences, the place and the choice about the link are drafted defaults the owner may correct). Under its byline a hosted article carries one line: *Written with AI from {n} facts on {site}, checked against {N} rules before publishing.* Where the body ends with its *Sources* list, *Sources are listed below.* follows it and links to that list. `{n}` is the business facts the page states or links that were read from the site's own pages (`drafts.meta.factsUsed`, counted by address; a fact from the report or from a founder's answer is not one). `{N}` is the number of rules the page was held to when it was written, stored with it from this date (`drafts.meta.rulesChecked`); a page written earlier has no stored count and carries no line until it is next written (master session for the owner, 2026-10-11: a customer's public page states no number its row cannot back). `{site}` is the customer's domain. A page whose row records no fact from the site carries no line, and a page with one fact says *one fact*. The line is inside `<article>`, names no model, no vendor and not ReachKit, and is said in the site's language (D66). A WordPress post carries the same line as a paragraph of its content, after the byline, without the link. The structured data is unchanged. *Not built:* the link to a ReachKit page about how pages are made, and the setting that turns it off. No such page exists to link to; the link follows once #1404 ships it, and until then the line carries no link of ours (§5 and this section put none on a customer's page). *Known:* a WordPress post's byline is still English on a German site.
- 2026-09-29  **A new page adds what the web does not say** (owner, D12, #1143; a check over the pack since D41). Before the write call, code counts the pack's facts whose words are on none of the top results the research step read. A new page needs at least three, and at least one is a business fact: the customer's price, process, result, a founder's answer or one of the report's measurements. Short of that, nothing is written: the day becomes an update of the closest existing page where one is live, old enough to refresh (§6, 30 days) and the destination's to update; otherwise the page is set aside (`step_failed:adds`), its topic rests, and the day takes the next topic (D26). One line on the draft says so and asks for nothing (owner, 2026-10-10, #1393; until then it asked the founder for the missing fact). The facts counted are recorded on the row (`drafts.meta.adds`) and the review screen lists them under *what this page adds*. An update is not held to the bar.
- 2026-10-10  **An account or legal page is never a source** (owner, #1393). A published page cited `reachkit.app/signin` as where a fact was read. A sign-in, sign-up, account, basket, checkout or legal address, on any site and in German, French or Spanish as well as English, is never read for facts about a business, and the context pack drops any fact, market claim, site page or link that carries one. The words are a list in `src/lib/site-profile/knowledge/priority.ts`, a drafted default the owner may correct.
- 2026-10-10  **Written from, said truthfully** (owner, #1393). Settings › *What every page is written from* closes with: facts about the business come only from its site, and facts about the market come from public pages, each linked (*Two pools of facts*, 2026-09-29).
- 2026-09-29  **No near-identical page across customers** (owner, D13, #1144). Before a draft enters review it is compared with every draft and live page ReachKit wrote for other sites in the last 12 months, with the same near-duplicate bar as the same-site check. At or above it the draft is written again once with the other page named as one not to resemble, then held. The founder never sees another customer's text, only that the page was held for being too close to a page elsewhere; that line is a new key, drafted and named in #1144's pull request.
- 2026-10-04  **How the cross-customer check is carried out** (#1144; the reading of "then held", the page ceiling and the sentence are drafted, the owner corrects them). At stage 4 a page is compared, by the same five-word measure and the same 0.85 as the same-site check, with every draft and live page written for another site in the twelve months before — a page that was skipped or taken down is neither. At or above the bar the page fails the check and takes its one fix call (D41), on the same row and inside the same `CAPS.DRAFT_C`: the writer is told to keep away from the other page and is handed the sentences the two share, which are this page's own, so no sentence of another customer's reaches a prompt that had not already written it. A fixed page that clears the bar goes on as any page does; one still at or above it rests in *Needs you* (`step_failed:elsewhere`), its topic rests like any stopped page's (D26), and the day goes to the next topic. *Write it again* gives it one more rewrite on the same terms. The row records how close it was (`drafts.meta.elsewhere`) and nothing about the other page — no address, title or site — and the check is not one of the writing rules, whose failures name the page they found. One read holds at most 1,000 pages, newest first. An update and a refresh are checked like a new page; a founder's own edit is not. The new key is `publish.needs-you.elsewhere`.
- 2026-10-03  **A content mix, every page aware of the others, set and forget** (owner, D36, #1209, #1210): *"we should ensure we have different types of content we post ie listicles, info, documentation, content updates etc. all generated content should have context of all other content published to never publish something too similar. content should generally not require users input to publish, primarily a set and forget concept."* Three rules:
  - **Mix.** A site's pages follow a rolling weekly mix of the seven content types of D42 (below). Counts (#1209; drafted defaults the owner may correct): in any seven days at most two pages of one type, never the same type two days running, and once a site has had pages for seven days at least one of them is an update where one is ready (§6's 30 days stand). The type is decided in code at stage 1 (D41) and judged by no model. The mix orders and never empties a day: where no row fits, the count is given up first, then the type, then §6's topic rotation. D52's cap on comparisons (below) is the one count that is not given up. A stopped, skipped or taken-down page counts for no day.
  - **Context of all content.** Every context pack carries all of the site's published and planned pages, so nothing too similar to one of them is published (*Known content*, 2026-09-20). The list is the pages ReachKit wrote or is writing for the site and the next seven the ranking plans, each with its title, target question, address and type — at most 100, newest first, never a page's text (#1209).
  - **Set and forget.** A page publishes without founder input, behind the veto window, which stays.
- 2026-10-03  **How honesty by page type is held** (#1136, implementing the 2026-09-29 line and D20 above; the wordings are drafted defaults the owner may correct). Every Write and Earn row stores its page type — *product*, *market comparison* or *answer* — decided in code from its type, its search and its source (§6), never by a model; an Improve page is the customer's own and has none. The writer is told how the business appears on a page of its type. A product page has the product as its subject and names the business in the title where natural, and no rule stops it for doing so. On a market comparison one stage-4 check, `honest_row`, stops a page whose comparison table — a table that names one of the site's rivals — has no row for the business (whether the page owes a table is `required_blocks`' question, #1137), or that ranks the business first by its own word: "#1", "number one", "best overall", "is the best X", or "best", "top" or a leading "1." in a title or heading that names it. "Best fit for small teams" is a recommendation for a case and passes. The issue's "at least one limit" and "not ranked first by its own page" are superseded by D20. The model-judged fit check (`fit_claim`) runs on a listicle or comparison only (D41, D44): every other type skips it, because a page is written from its pack only, so the knowledge base in the pack is all a claim of fit can stand on. The house rule "never this business" and the opening without the brand are gone with `page_frame` and `brand_gap`; a row that recorded either name still reads. One sentence is new for the editor's mark, `draft.editor.break.honest-row`; `draft.editor.break.brand-gap` and `.page-frame` are removed.
- 2026-09-29  **The dogfood goes live on its own host** (owner, D10). ReachKit's own article is published on `content.reachkit.app` once #1045 attaches that host, and the three test "best SEO software" pages are retired then.
- 2026-10-04  **The pipeline is five stages** (owner, D41, #1251; built by #1252): *"generation of content should be straightforward, ie decide on a new content to write or update, use context including users knowledge base and generate based on defined structure we want to follow."* A page takes at most four model calls — two for research, one to write, one to fix — inside `CAPS.DRAFT_C`.
  1. **Decide** (code only). Today's page is new or an update, and it has one of the seven types of D42. Both are read off the ranked topics: business fit (#1134), buyer phrasing (#1208), no repeats (#1181, #1131), update-first (#1146), the pace (#1139) and the weekly mix (D36, #1209).
  2. **Context pack** (one or two model calls, for research only). One JSON object per page, stored with the draft and readable on the draft screen under *written from your business*. It holds: the knowledge base and the founder's answers (#1133), with the never-claim list; the market and the rivals (`sites.market`, #1185); the target question; every published and planned page of the site with its title, question and address, for no-repeat and for links; the research claims with their source addresses and read dates (#1135); and, for *current insights*, web results published in the last 30 days (drafted default). Code then holds the pack against the template's *pack must hold* line. A pack short of it writes nothing: the page stops naming what is missing, and the day goes to the next topic as D26 says (drafted default).
  3. **Write** (one call, Sonnet 5). The type's template, filled from the pack only.
  4. **Check** (code only, plus at most one fix call). Five checks:
     - **Blocks.** The template's required blocks are present, in its order and inside their limits, the length among them (`required_blocks`, `first_block_answers`).
     - **Numbers.** Every number traces to a pack source (`traceable_numerals`, `rival_source`), and nothing is denied of a rival that no stored source denies (`rival_negative`, issue 1485).
     - **No placeholder cell** (#1250). No table cell is empty or reads "not stated", "not linked", "n/a" or "unknown".
     - **No near-repeat.** Not of a page the site has (`near_duplicate`), and not of a page written for another site (D13).
     - **No demo or navigation text** (#1249). No sentence from the site's demo-phrase list or its menus.

     Beside them stand the guards D27 names, each already code: nothing invented (`grounding`, `no_invented_people`, `no_unsourced_testimonial`, `no_invented_test`, `no_invented_provenance`), no unsafe markup (`no_hidden_text`, `no_machine_address`), D20's `honest_row`, the never-claim list matched by its own words (D45, below), and on a listicle or comparison the one fit check D44 keeps. A failed check gets one fix call with the failures listed. A page that still fails goes to *Needs you*, and the owner is alerted (D27).
  5. **Publish.** The veto window, then live.
  - **Retired:** the brief, the outline, the per-section calls, the critic, the answerability pass, the answer-box rewrite call, the separate non-commodity listing (D12 is a check over the pack) and every rule code cannot check — the model-judged claim check, the fit check on every type but a listicle or comparison (D44, below), and `no_new_question_heading`, which had an outline to guard. D20 and D27 stand.
- 2026-10-04  **Seven content types, one fixed template each** (owner, D42, #1251): *"we should have a variety of content types ie listicles, how to, documentation, research, q&a style, current insights ie latest information from the web and content updates."* A template is data, one file a type (#1252). Every type carries the byline and dates, the answer box (40–60 words), three to five takeaways, a closing line with one link, and a sources list where a market fact is used (*What every page carries*). *Blocks* below names what a type requires beyond those. The owner's are the seven types, the comparison and how-to lengths and the update's 20%; every other number, title pattern and section list is a drafted default the owner corrects before #1252 starts.
  1. **Listicle / comparison.** *For:* helping a buyer choose among options, with the customer as an honest row (D20). *Title:* "Best {category} for {audience}: {n} options compared" or "{A} vs {B}: which fits {case}"; never the customer as "#1" or "best". *Sections:* what to look for · the options at a glance (table, and since D52 the date its prices were last checked) · how the options were compared (D52) · each option in turn: what it does, its price, who it suits, one limit · which to choose for which case, naming where a rival is the better pick (D52) · how {business} handles it. *Blocks:* a table with the customer's row and at least two rivals; FAQ, 3–6. *Length:* 1,500–2,500. *Pack must hold:* three options or more, the customer among them; for each rival a claim from its own pricing or feature page; the customer's price and one point of difference from the knowledge base.
  2. **How-to.** *For:* getting the reader through one task as cited outside sources describe it — the platform's or authority's own documentation first — with the business's product or process as at most one step (issue 1486, which rewrote "with the business's product or process"; the owner may correct it). *Title:* "How to {task}" or "How to {task} with {product}". *Sections:* what you need first · the steps · how to check it worked · mistakes to avoid · the next step. *Blocks:* 3–10 numbered steps, each an action and its result, at most one of them the business's own; FAQ, 3–6. *Length:* 1,200–2,000. *Pack must hold:* claims off two public pages or more (`CITES_MARKET`), one business fact on the topic, and the product page to link (issue 1486; until then the process in the knowledge base or a founder's answer, three steps or more).
  3. **Documentation.** *For:* a reference page on one feature or concept the knowledge base covers. *Title:* "{Feature}: what it is and how it works". *Sections:* what it is · how it works · options and limits · an example · related features. *Blocks:* a table where there are two or more options, plans or limits; FAQ, 3–6. *Length:* 800–1,500. *Pack must hold:* four knowledge-base facts or more about the feature, each with its page. No research call.
  4. **Research.** *For:* publishing original data: what ReachKit measured in this site's market, anonymised and aggregated, with no other customer named. *Title:* "{Finding, with its number}: {market}, {month year}". *Sections:* the findings · how it was measured: questions, engines, dates, and what is not measured · the data (table) · what it means for a buyer · where {business} stands. *Blocks:* a data table; a method section; FAQ, 3–6. *Length:* 1,000–1,600. *Pack must hold:* the site's latest complete pass with ten measured questions or more, its dates and engines, and figures that changed since the site's last research page. No other company's scan score (D6).
  5. **Q&A.** *For:* direct answers to the questions buyers ask on one topic. *Title:* "{Topic}: {n} buyer questions, answered". *Sections:* 5–8 questions, each opening with its answer in two or three sentences, then the detail · how {business} handles it. *Blocks:* the 5–8 questions, which are also the page's FAQ data; no further FAQ. *Length:* 800–1,400. *Pack must hold:* five questions or more that the pass measured or buyers were recorded asking, none invented, and a fact for each; and, since issue 1486, claims off two public pages or more, so the answers cite outside facts and the business is its one section.
  6. **Current insights.** *For:* what changed in the market lately, each change dated and sourced. *Title:* "{Market}: what changed in {month year}". *Sections:* what changed, in short · each change, 3–5: what happened, when, the source, what it means · what to do now · how {business} responds. *Blocks:* a date and a source on every change; FAQ, 3–6. *Length:* 700–1,200. *Pack must hold:* three web results or more published in the last 30 days, each with its address, its publication date and a claim word for word.
  7. **Update.** *For:* refreshing a live page at the same address. *Title:* the live page's own, changed only where a fact in it changed. *Sections:* the live page's own, in its type's order; only a section with a new fact is rewritten. *Blocks:* its type's blocks, and a dated "Updated" note under the byline saying what changed. *Length:* within 20% of the live page. *Pack must hold:* the live page's text and recorded facts, why it is refreshed (#1139), and one new fact or more. (Amended 2026-10-18, issue 1521, `docs/spec/decisions/2026-10-18-1521.md`: a `close`, `unmet` or `lost_position` refresh may add up to three sections from the pack's `gaps` and grow up to 40%.)
- 2026-10-04  **One command tests a page** (owner, D43, #1251): *"the generation should be straightforward also from a testing side."* The standard test is `npm run page -- --site <domain> --type <type> [--topic "<q>"]`. It builds the context pack from production data and caches it on disk, writes one page, runs the stage-4 checks, and prints the page's path, each check's result and the cost. A rerun reuses the cached pack unless `--fresh` is given. Round 2's remaining pairs are skipped (#1164). The pipeline's proof is one page of each type for reachkit.app, about €1–1.50 in all (D34), kept under `tests/fixtures/walk/pipeline-v2/` for the owner's read (#1252).
- 2026-10-04  **A page is written for the site's current market** (#1248, found in round 2 of the content test, #1164). The brief, the outline and the draft read the market from `sites.market` (§6, D28): the category, who buys, the job, the rivals and what the site is not. A founder's own category or rivals stand in their place (D28). The stored report's category is used only where the site has no stored market. Each draft records the market it was written for, and which of the two it came from, in `drafts.meta.market`.
- 2026-10-04  **The templates stand, and one model check stays** (owner, D44, #1252): *"templates OK, D44 and D45 as recommended"*. The seven templates of D42 are approved as drafted. One cheap model fit check is kept, on **listicle and comparison pages only** (about 0.5¢): it checks that the page never claims the business fits a case its knowledge base does not support (`fit_claim`). Every other type skips it. It is the one model call of stage 4 beside the fix call, so a listicle may take five calls where D41 says four.
- 2026-10-04  **The never-claim list is a literal word match** (owner, D45, #1252). In stage 4 and on every save the list is matched by its own words, whatever the case or spacing, in the title and the body; the same list goes into the writer's instructions. A paraphrase is not caught: that risk is accepted, and the veto window and the owner alert (D27) are the backstop. The model-judged half of the claim check is gone, so the check can no longer be "unrun"; a row that recorded that state still reads.
- 2026-10-04  **Two types wait, the other five ship** (owner, D46, #1252, after the proof in `tests/fixtures/walk/pipeline-v2/`: five of the seven types wrote a page that passed, the listicle was stopped on its table and current insights had no recent result to write from). The listicle and current-insights types are left out of the weekly mix (both are back: current insights with D48, #1259, the listicle with D47, #1258, and the list is empty): a topic of either fills no day and is not counted as a day of supply; it stays on file, and ranks again when its type is let back in. An Earn or format page whose search has no other shape is a listicle (*How the five stages are carried out*, below), so those wait too. The types left out are one list (`CONTENT_MIX.PAUSED_TYPES`) (the list was deleted on 2026-10-23, #1626, once both types were back). The lead's free page (§2) is not the mix's and is unchanged.
- 2026-10-04  **The listicle's table is built in code** (owner, D47; built by #1258). The comparison table is laid out by code from the pack, not written by the model: a value a rival's own page does not state drops that cell's row, never the rival, and at least two rivals are kept. The listicle returns to the mix when this is built and proved.
  - **How it is carried out** (#1258; every reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). The rows are the options: the business first, then each rival whose own pricing or feature page yielded a claim. The columns are the criteria the pack holds a kind of fact for — *Pricing* and *What it does*. A rival's cell is the claim read off its own page, word for word and linked to that page; the business's is its price and the first feature of the offering that price belongs to, from the knowledge base, else a passage of its own pages that states a price. A criterion **most** options lack leaves the table. One that fewer lack stays, and the option that lacks it keeps its row: because no cell may be empty or say a value is missing (2026-10-02), the table is then laid out with one cell an option under *What its own page says*, each cell naming the criteria that option states ("Pricing: … · What it does: …") and leaving the other out. Prices in more than one currency took *Pricing* out of the table until D56, below; they now stand side by side. The writer is handed the table and writes no table of its own; code places it under the opening paragraph of *the options at a glance*, in place of any table written there. **Fewer than two rivals with a fact** — or a table the pack cannot fill for the business's own row — is no comparison: the page is written as a Q&A from the same reads, told to answer rather than compare, and its row records that type. A business with no price of its own on file is written as that Q&A too, never stopped to ask for a price (R-D1, 2026-11-06, `docs/spec/decisions/2026-11-06-2405.md`). *Pack must hold*'s "three options" is now this: a table code can build with the business and two rivals. The listicle left `CONTENT_MIX.PAUSED_TYPES` with this change (the list was deleted on 2026-10-23, #1626, once both types were back); its proof is `npm run page -- --site reachkit.app --type listicle`, at most 25¢ (the owner allowed it on 2026-10-04, a one-off exception to D34's daily cap). The four headers are new keys (`body.table.option`, `.pricing`, `.does`, `.stated`).
- 2026-10-06  **Rival research yields real price and feature facts, and prices keep their own currency** (owner, D56, "D54–D56 approved as recommended"; built by #1282, which absorbs #1276). Found with D50's rivals: Peec AI's pricing page gave "Trusted by 3000+ brands" and its features address is a 404, Otterly's only "feature" was a statistic about ChatGPT, and dollar prices beside ReachKit's euros took the whole price column out, and with it a rival whose only fact was a price. *The pages.* A rival's pricing page and its product or features page are found in its own navigation links and its sitemap, never at a guessed address, and read. *The claims.* A claim is a feature only when it says what the product does; a statistic about another product or the market is not one. *The prices.* A cell states its price as the rival's page states it, with no conversion; a table with mixed currencies keeps the column and adds one line saying the prices are in the currencies each vendor publishes. A pricing claim with no figure, such as "tailored pricing", is a cell ("Custom pricing"), so the rival keeps its row.
  - **How D56 is carried out** (#1282; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *The pages.* The rival's home page and its sitemap (`/sitemap.xml`, else the one its `robots.txt` names; an index is followed into its first three sitemaps) are read, free. A page is the rival's pricing page where the first segment of its address starts with *pricing*, *price* or *plans*, or its link is labelled exactly *Pricing* or *Plans*; its product or features page likewise for *features*, *product* and *platform*. the pricing page itself is taken before an address that only starts with the word (an agencies price list), a shallower address before a deeper one, a link before a sitemap entry, and only the rival's own host counts. A rival that names neither page is not read and is not offered to the table. *The claims.* The claims call says of each claim whether it states the price of the page's own product, says what that product does, or neither. A rival's claim is kept as a pricing or a feature claim by that answer, whichever page it was read from, and one that is neither is not kept. *The cell.* A rival's *Pricing* cell is a claim that states a figure; else, where a pricing claim says the price is agreed with the vendor (custom, tailored, bespoke, on request, a quote, contact sales), the cell reads "Custom pricing" and links that page; a pricing-page line that states neither is no cell, and nor is a line about one plan ("Enterprise: custom"), which does not say how the product is priced. Of several figures the cell takes the one the page starts from ("from", "starts at"), else the first. The *What it does* cell prefers a claim read off a page that is not the price list, and of those one that names the product. A page that shows its prices only after a script runs states no figure to this read, and that rival's row then has no *Pricing* entry. The line under a mixed-currency table stands above the *Prices last checked* line, and the page pass no longer takes a mixed-currency column out of any table. The two strings are new keys (`body.table.custom`, `body.table.currencies`).
- 2026-10-04  **Current insights reads a news search** (owner, D48; built by #1259). Its recent results come from a search of the news (DataForSEO's Google News endpoint), after its cost is checked against the caps. The type returns to the mix when this is built and proved.
- 2026-10-04  **How the news search is carried out** (#1259; the bounds are drafted defaults the owner may correct). The vendor charges 0.2¢ a search (cost check, 2026-10-04), and a result names no publisher, so its domain stands as the source. At stage 2 a *current insights* page buys a news search for the market's category and then for its first two synonyms (`sites.market`; none where the founder set their own category, and the target search stands in where no category is known) — at most three searches, ten results each, live, ledgered inside `CAPS.DRAFT_C` and asked again no sooner than a day later. It takes the place of the web search for studies, so a page still makes at most two research calls. A result is kept where the search dates it inside 30 days and it is not the customer's own page; the kept results are read in turn across the searches until five are held. A page whose own markup states a publication date is held to that date, and dropped where it is older; otherwise the search's date stands. Each kept claim carries its address, its date and the publisher the search names, and the writer is handed all three. Current insights is in the weekly mix again: `CONTENT_MIX.PAUSED_TYPES` held the listicle alone until D47 was built (#1258), and is empty since (the list was deleted on 2026-10-23, #1626, once both types were back).
- 2026-10-04  **A news result is kept only when it is about the market** (#1262, found in #1259's proof: one of reachkit.app's three items was a security vendor's launch; the bounds and the reading of "about" are drafted defaults the owner may correct). A news search matches words. So at stage 2 the dated results are read in turn until eight are held, and one small call on the small tier judges each of them against the site's market (`sites.market`): its category, its synonyms, the job the product is hired for and the markets the site is not in. The call is shown each result's title, its source and its first 120 words. A result is about the market when it reports something in it that a buyer or seller there would read as news of their own market; one that only shares words with the category, or is about a neighbouring market, is not. The first five judged about the market are kept, and only those are read for claims. A result the call does not answer for is not kept, and where the call cannot be made or does not answer, none is. With fewer than three kept results the pack is short of *recent results*, the page is not written that day and the day goes to the next topic (D26). The pack records every result that was judged and the verdict (`pack.news`). This is one more model call at stage 2, on a current-insights page only: it may make three research calls where D41 says two, about 0.2¢ more, inside `CAPS.DRAFT_C`.
- 2026-10-04  **How the five stages are carried out** (#1252; every reading below is a drafted default the owner may correct). *Decide:* the type is read off the row — an update where the row updates a page; otherwise the first shape its search has, in this order: a comparison ("best", "vs", "alternatives", "top", "reviews"), a how-to ("how to", "steps", "set up", "checklist"), current insights ("news", "latest", "what changed", "trends"), research ("statistics", "data", "study", "report", "benchmark"), documentation ("what is", "how does", "guide", "features", "docs"); and with no shape an Earn or format page is a listicle and every other page a Q&A. *Context pack:* stored whole on the row (`drafts.meta.pack`) and shown on the draft screen under *written from your business*; it carries at most 40 of the site's own passages, the 24 research claims, 100 of the site's pages and eight questions. Research is at most two model calls over at most thirteen pages; documentation and research pages read none. For *current insights* the one search is the target's own words, up to eight of its results are read, and a result counts only where the page's own markup states a publication date inside 30 days. An ordinary search seldom returns three such pages — on 2026-10-04 reachkit.app's returned none, and the page was rightly not written — so since #1259 the type reads a search of the news instead (D48, *How the news search is carried out*). *Pack must hold*, as code reads it: "three options" is two rivals whose own pricing or feature page yielded a claim; the customer's price is a price in the knowledge base or a passage of its pages that states one; a point of difference is a differentiator in the knowledge base or a promise the profile kept; "the process, three steps or more" and "four facts about the feature" are that many business facts — passages or knowledge-base items — one of them at least sharing a word with the target, since whether a topic is the business's own was judged when it was ranked (#1134); a Q&A page needs five questions, chosen as *A Q&A's questions* below says (#1263), and five such facts; a research page's measured questions are the tracked buyer prompts where the pass asked them; a research page's figures have changed where the site has no earlier research page, or the score or the count of answers naming the business differs from the one that page was written from; an update needs its live page's text and one fact that page does not state. A short pack writes nothing: the row keeps the pack, rests in *Needs you* (`step_failed:pack:` and what is missing) and names it on the draft, and the day goes to the next topic (D26). *Write and fix:* one call each on Sonnet 5, up to 8,192 output tokens, two minutes; the fix call is not started later than 165 seconds into the page's making or past the cap, and a page it cannot be made for rests on the checks that stopped it. A write call that does not answer is tried once more (#813). *Check:* a block quote that is a pack passage word for word and came back without its link is given it in code; a link to a page one of the page's facts was read from stands; the length is counted on the page as laid out, and the fix call is told the count; an update's 20% is counted over the words under the headings on both pages, so a page written before the answer and takeaways boxes existed is not stopped for gaining them. `no_placeholder_cell` and `no_demo_text` are the two new checks; the site's demo phrases and its menus (`nav`, `header`, `footer`) are what the second reads, whole sentences of three words or more. An update's note of what changed is recorded on the row (`drafts.meta.updateNote`) and no check reads it, so it is not printed: the page states the updated date under its byline (#1137), and printing what changed beside it is still owed. *`npm run page`* reads a site's production rows from its committed export (`scripts/page/snapshot/<domain>.json`), because the box holds no database key; its research, search and model calls are live. New strings: `publish.needs-you.pack` and one label for each thing a pack can lack (`publish.needs-you.pack.need.*`), the pack's headings on the draft screen, and the editor's marks for the two new checks.
- 2026-10-04  **A Q&A's questions** (#1263, part of #1127, after reachkit.app's page for 6 October answered "How does AEO Engine compare with MaxAEO?" with "no fact-based answer can be given" and asked "How much does SEO software cost?"; the source rule and the fact rule are the issue's, how code reads them is a drafted default the owner may correct). A Q&A takes its 5–8 questions from two places only: the site's tracked buyer prompts (§6, D19) and the stored report's measured questions that are on the site's **current** market (`sites.market`) — a question whose wording or search shares a word with the market's category or one of its synonyms, or names a rival in force. Words every search shares ("best", "tool", "software", "cost") match nothing. A site with no concluded market keeps its report's own questions, which is a lead's free first page (§2). The questions the knowledge base recorded off the site's pages are no longer a source. A question goes in only where the pack holds a fact that answers it: one that names a product — a rival in force, one the conclusion names that the founder's own choice of rivals left out, or one of the stored report's — needs a claim read off that product's own page, its pricing page where the question asks what it costs; any other needs a business fact or a market claim that shares two of its subject words. A question short of that is dropped and is never written as one that cannot be answered. Fewer than five leaves the pack short (`step_failed:pack`, *buyer questions*), and the day goes to the next topic (D26). `npm run page` caches the research, not the pack, and `--pack-only` stops after stage 2 at no cost, so a change to how a pack is read is tested without buying the research again (D34).
- 2026-10-07  **A Q&A's questions are real buyer questions, and its headings differ** (owner, D61, #1295; "D61 approved as recommended"). Found: reachkit.app's Q&A of 7 October went live with eight question headings that were its tracked prompts word for word, seven of them one template with a different ending ("Which tools help me understand where AI and search send buyers on a small budget / without hiring an agency / as a team of one"). The tracked prompts are templated on purpose, so they can be measured (D19). This rewrites the source rule of #1263 above; its fact rule stands. (1) **Sources.** A Q&A takes its questions from what buyers were seen to ask: Google's "People also ask" for the page's target, and the target's related searches that have a measured volume. A tracked buyer prompt is a measuring tool and is never a question, wherever it is stored. (2) **At most one question from any one prompt template** is kept, wherever a templated phrasing could still arrive. (3) **A new stage-4 check, `distinct_headings`**, stops a page where two H2s share most of their words (token overlap of 0.6 or more) or open with the same six or more words. A failing page goes to the one fix call, like any other check. (4) A Q&A still needs five questions with facts (D42, #1263). With fewer real questions the type is not written that day and the day goes to the next topic (D26). *How it is carried out* (each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). The research step of a Q&A — and of a comparison, which D47 may write as one — buys the target's own SERP once (the advanced SERP the product already buys carries both lists; 0.2¢) under a cache key of its own, since a SERP stored earlier kept neither, and one keyword-overview call (about 1.1¢) for the volume of up to ten related searches; a related search with no measured volume is dropped, and so is one of a word or two, which is a name or a topic and not a question ("hubspot", "apollo ai"). Google lists what people also ask beside any search, on the market or off it ("What is the 30% rule for AI?"), so each of these questions is held to the site's current market the way #1263 holds a measured one. A related search is worded as the question it asks, the way a measured search is ("how to plan team projects" → "How do I plan team projects?"). The stored report's measured questions on the current market stay a source after those two, because each is a Google search with a measured volume; a lead's first page has no other. No AI fan-out or related questions are stored today, so none are read. The order is People also ask, related searches, measured questions, those sharing a word with the target first. The seven use-case prompts count as one template; every other prompt shape is its own. Of two questions the check in (3) would call alike, the pack keeps the first, so the writer is not handed a pair the page would be stopped for. *Overlap* is the words two headings share over the words of the longer one, compared as written in lower case, with nothing stemmed or left out. `distinct_headings` reads a Q&A's H2s only: a comparison's options and an update's live sections are headed alike on purpose ("What does A cost?", "What does B cost?"), and a founder's own edit is not held to it. It adds no sentence to any screen.
- 2026-10-07  **A thin SERP borrows "People also ask" from the market's searched terms** (owner, D62, #1297; "D62 and D63 approved as recommended"). Found by #1295: for "tools to track where ai sends potential customers", Google's "People also ask" held four general AI questions and the related searches were names ("hubspot", "apollo ai"), so the pack held 0–2 real questions and the Q&A could not be written. Where the target's own SERP leaves a Q&A with fewer than the five questions it needs, the research step also reads "People also ask" and the related searches beside the market's searched terms (D49): `searchTerm` first, then the other searched terms, most searched first. It reads one term at a time and stops as soon as five questions are kept or the terms run out, at about 0.2¢ a term, inside the page's cap. A borrowed question is held to the same market filter, the same one-per-template limit and the same `distinct_headings` measure as one from the target's own SERP, and still needs a fact that answers it. With fewer than five after that, the type is not written that day, as before (D61, D26). The 7 October page is not rewritten as a Q&A: it was queued on 2026-10-07 for a rewrite in place as a how-to for "how to rank on chatgpt" (140 searches a month in the US). *How it is carried out* (each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *Kept* is the pack's own count, after every filter, so the terms are read after the claim calls: a question is kept only where a fact answers it. A term is searched as the market names it (D49: without a closing "tool" or "software"); the target itself and a term listed twice are not read again. A category the founder set is the one term; a site with no concluded market has none and borrows nothing. A term's "People also ask" is read at once. The terms' related searches need a measured volume (D61), and a keyword-overview call is about 1.1¢, five times the SERP it follows; so they are measured in one call for all the terms read, up to ten searches, and only where the terms ran out with the Q&A still short. A comparison that D47 writes as a Q&A borrows the same way. The research log and `npm run page -- --pack-only` state the terms read and how many kept questions they added.
- 2026-10-07  **A day says ReachKit stopped only where a stop is recorded for it** (owner, #1321, looking at the plan: 9 October said "We stopped" and no stop was stored for that day). The stopped-work lines belong to a recorded stop and to nothing else. A plan day with no page shows the page supply holds for it; a day empty for a cause the calendar knows (supply, a hold, past the horizon) uses that cause's own words; a day the product cannot explain has its own two words on the card and its own line in the panel, and neither says ReachKit stopped. Found in the stored plan: 7 and 8 October held written pages, the ranked list held one page and it was the page for 9 October, and the calendar counted the list from today, so the two dates that already had pages used it up. The list's first page now goes on the first date without one. The publishing card on every signed-in screen follows the same rule (owner, same day, on #1321): it says ReachKit stopped only where a stop is stored; with no publish scheduled it states the cause the shell has (publishing off, nothing approved, nothing planned), and where none of those is true it has its own line and invents no stop. The three sentences for the unexplained cases are drafted, the owner corrects them.
- 2026-10-10  **Links** (owner, #1391: "go ahead with all"; the numbers and readings below are drafted defaults the owner may correct). Found: a page linked a fixed set — pricing, about, features and two product pages; a link's words were the target's own title, so a slogan ("One plan. One page a day.") stood as link text, and one live page carried a link whose text was "1"; older pages never linked newer ones; no link was checked when a page went out; and the draft screen showed a link count only.
  - **Deep links.** A page carries three to six links into the customer's own site. Code ranks the whole crawled inventory by what the page is about: the words a page's title, heading and address share with the target, each weighed by how few of the site's pages carry it, so the site's own name ranks nothing. One place is kept for the page a reader acts on (the product, features or pricing page nearest the topic). The home page and legal pages are never linked. Crawled is still the only meaning of exists: nothing is linked that the crawl did not read. Up to six are offered to the writer, who links a page inside the sentence that speaks to it; where the finished body links fewer than three, the best of the rest are listed until it holds three.
  - **Descriptive link text.** A link's words name what the reader finds at the other end. Code names each offered page by the heading or title segment that says what its address says, else by the address's own words (`/pricing` is "Pricing"), never by a title that is a sentence about the business. On the finished body code checks every link: a bare number takes in the words it counts ("1 of the 11 buyer questions"); a link into the customer's site that reads "here", "click here", an address, or the target's slogan reworded is unwrapped, the sentence kept; such a link to another site is named by its host. The repair that links a figure to its source (#1178) links the figure with its words, never the figure alone.
  - **Live check at publish.** When a page goes out, every address it links — the customer's pages, their articles, the sources — is asked once (one bounded GET through the guarded fetcher, which has no HEAD; 5 s an address, 12 s in all). *Gone* is a 404 or a 410 and nothing else: a timeout, a bot wall or a server error leaves the link and is recorded as unchecked. A gone link into the customer's site is unwrapped and logged. A gone source takes its claim with it: the sentence that cites it, its table row and its entry in the sources list. What was found is stored with the page (`drafts.meta.linkCheck`), and the body stored is the body delivered. The check never stops a page from publishing.
  - **Links in both directions.** When a new page goes live, the one or two nearest older live pages ReachKit wrote are each planned an update that links it — a fifth cause of §7's refresh (*Pace*, #1139), `link`. Code chooses the pages (same cluster first, then shared words weighed as above); the writer adds one sentence with the link and changes nothing else. It is an update like any other: it takes a day of the calendar, has the same veto window, and is a new version at the same address, so the updated date moves only because the text did. §6's 30-day floor stands, so a version younger than that is not updated. A page the customer edited, or whose update they stopped, is never chosen.
  - **Links on this page.** The draft screen lists every link the body carries under *Your site*, *Your articles* and *Sources*: its words, its address, why it was chosen, and whether the address answered — "Checked when the page publishes" until it has. Links the check removed are listed with the reason.
  - **Site link map.** Settings › Publishing shows which of the customer's pages their live articles link and how many articles link each, and which articles stand alone (no other article links to them, or they link to no page of the site). It states and offers no control.
  - **Not built.** No `llms.txt` and no structured data beyond what a page already declares: the research found no citation gain from either.

**Done when** The draft-ready mail link stops that page with no session. An untouched draft publishes at window end on their domain. No two assets share a date. An empty day states its cause and offers no publish.
- 2026-10-05  **Titles follow per-type formulas and a code check** (owner, D51, #1269; built by #1266). The formulas, by type:
  - listicle: `{N} best {term} for {audience} ({year})`;
  - comparison: `{A} vs {B} ({year}): {criteria or who it fits}`;
  - alternatives: `{N} {rival} alternatives for {audience} ({year})`;
  - how-to: `How to {outcome in the buyer's words}`;
  - Q&A: the buyer's question as typed;
  - documentation: `What is {term}? {plain qualifier}`;
  - research: `{finding with its number}: {scope} ({year})`;
  - current insights: `{platform} {change} ({month year}): what it means for {audience}`;
  - an update keeps its live title and changes the year only where the body changed.

  Stage 4's `title` check holds the `<title>` to:
  - 30–65 characters (aim for 45–60);
  - the target's search term within its first five words;
  - at least one specific: a number, an engine, a searched rival, a price, an audience, or a question;
  - a year only on listicle, comparison, alternatives, research and current insights, only the current year, and never as a bare tail;
  - none of the banned patterns ("questions, answered", "buyer questions", "ultimate guide", "everything you need to know", "unlock", "comprehensive", square brackets, pipes, a word repeated);
  - token overlap under 0.8 with every title the site has published or planned.

  A failing title goes into the one fix call. The H1 may be the longer, conversational form, and the `<title>` is the search form. The meta description is 120–155 characters and answer-first. Sentence case (#1137) stands. Evidence: `docs/walk/titles-and-vocabulary.md`.
- 2026-10-06  **How the title rules are carried out** (#1266; D51 above; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). The check is a stage-4 rule, `title`, on a page ReachKit generates; it asks nothing of a founder's own edit. *The search term* is D49's `searchTerm` where the market holds one, or the target query: the title passes where, for either, the term's first three words that are not filler ("best", "for", "the") are all in it and the first of them is among its first five words. *A specific* is a number that is not a year, an engine's name (ChatGPT, Perplexity, Claude, Gemini, Copilot, Google, AI Overviews, AI Mode), a rival the pack names, a currency amount or "free", a word of the buyer the market or the knowledge base records, or a title that asks ("How to", "What is", a question mark). *A bare tail* is a title that ends on the year outside brackets. *A word repeated* is a word of three letters or more, filler aside. *Token overlap* is the words two titles share over the words either has. The meta description is fitted in code and never stops a page (owner R-A, 2026-10-31, `docs/spec/decisions/2026-10-31-2136.md`); answer-first is asked of the writer and not checked. An update's title is set in code: the live page's, with a year in it moved to the current year where a section changed; the check does not read it. The H1 is stored beside the page (`drafts.meta.heading`) where it differs from the title, which stays `drafts.title`; the hosted page prints it as its `<h1>` and its article headline, and a founder's edit of the title replaces both. A WordPress post has one title field and takes the `<title>`. `npm run page` prints the title, the H1 and the description with their lengths.
- 2026-10-10  **The H1 is the title, a title carries one specific from the pack, and the writer proposes three for code to pick** (owner, issue 1389: "go ahead with all"; revises D51 and *How the title rules are carried out* above; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found on reachkit.app's 10 October page: the `<title>` was "How to rank in AI Overview for your business" and the H1 was "How to get your business named in AI Overview and other AI answers", which dropped the search, promised two things and carried no detail. (1) **The H1 is the title.** The writer no longer writes an H1: code prints the chosen title as the page's `<h1>`, and stage 4 stops a page whose H1 is another wording unless it is the title's near twin (token overlap 0.8 or more) and holds every rule the title holds. A `<title>` may add the brand after it. (2) **One specific, from the pack.** A number the pack states or the page counts out (its options, its questions, its numbered steps), a year aside; a price is such a number, and "free" where the pack says it; an AI engine's name; the place the business serves; and, on a comparison only, a rival the pack names. A name the search itself carries is the search and not a detail added to it, so "How to rank in AI Overview" carries none. A question no longer counts, and neither does an audience. (3) **Length.** The hard limits stay 30–65 characters; the writer is asked for 50–60, and of two titles that are otherwise equal the one inside 50–60 is taken. The search's own wording, or a question buyers were seen to ask about it where the pack holds one, starts inside the title's first 30 characters (this replaces "within its first five words"). (4) **A statement that answers.** A title is a question only where the search is one: the search as typed, or the target's sentence where the page has no search. The formulas of the how-to, the documentation, the Q&A and the current-insights page now end on a figure, a price or a place from the pack, and a comparison's on the price or number that separates the two. (5) **More banned patterns**, on the title and so on the H1: "complete guide", "unleash", "elevate", "navigate", "demystify", "why X matters", "in today's …", and a how-to that promises two things ("How to X and Y"), beside D51's list; German has its own counterparts. A banned word the search itself carries is the searcher's word and is allowed ("how to unlock an iPhone"). (6) **The writer proposes, code picks.** The one write call returns three titles. Code takes the one that fails no rule and sits closest to the search and its follow-up questions — the share of the nearest search's words it carries, plus the mean share of each follow-up question's — then the one inside 50–60 characters, then a statement before a question, then the writer's order. No model judges a title. Where none passes, the one that fails fewest is kept, stage 4 stops the page on it, and the one fix call is told what it failed and proposes three again. The titles proposed, with what each failed, are stored beside the page (`drafts.meta.titles`), and `npm run page` prints them. The writer is told which rivals and place count and is shown up to twelve figures the pack states. (7) **An update** keeps its live title, as before. *Not built:* "People also ask" is read only for a Q&A today (D61), so every other type is picked by its searches alone; reading it for every page is one more search read, about 0.2¢ a page, and is the owner's to decide. *(2) does not stop a page:* see the line of 2026-10-11 below.
- 2026-10-11  **A title's specific is tried for and never stops a page** (ruling recorded by the master session for the owner, who is being told and can overrule; under the owner's standing autopilot rule of 2026-10-08: the system runs on its own, and a page must not be lost for something the business cannot supply). The owner approved "one specific detail" in issue 1389 as a rule of title quality. *Required* means the picker and the fix call must try for it: a title that carries one is taken before one that does not, a page whose chosen title lacks one takes its fix call, and the fix call is told to add one. It does not stop a page. Where the specific is all the title still lacks after the fix call — or where the fix call could not start for money or time — the title goes on and the page proceeds; the title is marked `specific_unmet` among `drafts.meta.titles` and the run is logged (`draft_generated`, step `title`, outcome `specific_unmet`). Every other title fault stops the page as before, and so does a missing specific beside any other fault. Among titles that all fail, code now keeps the one failing fewest of the rules that stop a page, so a title lacking only its specific is kept before one that is too long. Found on the panel proof: a local business whose search already names its place, and whose pack states no figure, has no specific to carry (tattly.com, baeckerei-hinkel.de, fletcherelectric.co.uk). **A serial number is not a specific:** a number that follows "episode", "ep.", "issue", "volume", "vol.", "chapter", "season" or "part" (German: "Folge", "Ausgabe", "Heft", "Band", "Kapitel", "Staffel", "Teil") counts for nothing in the pack or in the title, and the writer is not shown it as a figure — clearleft.com's title had passed on "podcast ep. 16". The word list is a drafted default the owner may correct. A year never counted. Code still cannot tell whether any other figure matters to the search.
- 2026-10-05  **Self-promoting pages are capped and honest** (owner, D52, #1269; built by #1267). At most one page in seven — counted over the site's last seven published or planned pages — is a listicle or comparison that includes the business; a topic over the cap falls through to the next (D26). Each such page says how the options were compared, the date the prices were last checked, and a verdict that names where a rival is the better pick. The business is never ranked first by default. Why: Google's December 2025 core update cut templated self-ranking listicle, alternatives and vs pages by 29–49%, and most self-promotional listicles AI cites do not get their author recommended.
