- 2026-10-06  **How D52 is carried out** (#1267; D52 above; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *Which pages.* Every page of the listicle / comparison type includes the business — its template gives the business a row and a section — so the cap counts that type; an update of one is an update and is not counted. *The count.* It is read as the mix and the pace are: a day takes no comparison where the six days before it hold one, so no seven days running hold two, and at a page a day that is the last seven pages. A stopped, skipped or taken-down page counts for no day. *Over the cap.* The topic falls through to the best topic of another type. Unlike the mix, the cap is never given up: where every topic left is a comparison over the cap, the day has no page (§7: supply is the cap) and the plan ends there; the topics stay on file, open and ready, and are taken the first day the seven days have room. *How we compared.* The template gains a short section after the table that says the options were compared from each one's own pricing and product pages, and that nothing was tried or tested. *Prices last checked.* Code prints one line under the table, "Prices last checked {date}." (`body.table.checked`), dated the day the oldest of the rivals' pages the table stands on was read, so the date holds for every row. *The verdict.* The which-to-choose section is asked to name the case where a rival is the better pick; stage 4's blocks check stops a comparison whose verdict names none of the table's rivals (`required_blocks`, *verdict*), and the one fix call is told so. That the sentence recommends the rival is asked of the writer and not checked. *Never first.* `honest_row` (D20) stands as it is.
- 2026-10-15  **The cap never empties a day** (issue 1455; corrects #1267's *Over the cap* reading above, which the owner may correct in turn). Found: reachkit.app wrote no page on 15 or 16 October. Every ready topic it held was a comparison or listicle, the 14 October page was one, and #1267 left the day empty "where every topic left is a comparison over the cap". Now, where every topic left is over the cap, the best of them is written as a Q&A of its buyer question (D42's Q&A template, as D47 already writes a comparison short of two rivals). The page answers the question and ranks nobody, so D52's one comparison in seven days still holds. The mix and the cap count a page as the type it was written as (`drafts.meta.contentType`), so the Q&A uses up no comparison's week. Regenerate writes it again as a Q&A. A retargeted live page whose new search is a comparison is written as one and counts as one, as D53 says.
- 2026-10-15  **No day is silent** (issue 1455). In a site's due hour the draft tick keeps how that date's draft ended and why: drafted, no page with the step that stopped it (`no_opportunity`, `no_scan`, a check, a step), or the error that stopped the run. These are one `draft_days` row per site and date, which no later tick overwrites. In the first due hour that starts at or after a paying, publishing site's publish time, a date with no page delivered is mailed to the owner once, whether or not a later page is going out, through the incident mail, with the site, the date and the reason kept (*"A site's publish time passed with no page for the day. The cause is the one its draft run recorded."*, drafted). Only a page delivered (since taken down or not) or one the customer stopped accounts for a date and is not mailed; a page the system set aside (the daily re-check's `retired:` skip) does not, and is told by where it stopped, and neither is a site's first day, before it has a page dated earlier.
- 2026-10-17  **A short pack does not empty the day** (issue 1477; refines #1252's *Pack must hold* reading and D26's one more topic, as drafted defaults the owner may correct). Found: at 07:00Z on 17 October both of reachkit.app's packs stopped on `buyer_questions,facts_on_topic`. Every ready topic was a comparison over D52's cap, each was written as a Q&A, and no page was drafted for 18 October. Two readings of the pack were wrong for every kind of business, and the run gave up too soon. *Plurals:* a word and its plural are one word when the pack matches a question or a topic with a fact. "engines" is "engine", "services" is "service" and "prices" is "price", by the language's own rule, where a cut "es" had made "engin". *A rival's name is not the topic:* "one fact at least sharing a word with the target" reads the target without the names of the rivals it names. Where only those names and the words every search shares are left ("peec ai alternatives", "profound vs otterly"), the market's category and the job it is concluded to do stand for the target. The needs themselves are unchanged: five questions, each with a fact that answers it, and five facts. *The run:* a topic whose pack was short wrote nothing and cost its pack alone. It does not use up D26's one more topic, so the run goes on to the next ready topic inside the same cost cap and the same 120 seconds. On the stored rows of 17 October, "profound vs otterly" now holds six questions and is written. "peec ai alternatives" is still short, because its own results page stored no question, and the day goes past it.
- 2026-10-05  **What is live is cleaned** (owner, D53, #1269; built by #1268). The hosted index's description is written from the business, never a raw setup answer. An article's author is a Person whose name and job title are read from the byline. reachkit.app's 3 October page is rewritten in place, as an update targeting "best AI visibility tools for small businesses", once D51 is built. Its 5 October page stays until a D50 comparison replaces it.
- 2026-10-06  **How D53 is built** (#1268; the three readings below are drafted defaults the owner may correct). *The line.* A hosted blog says one line about its business: under the index's heading, in an article's about card and on the index's share image. That line is what the business does: the founder's own wording of it where they reworded it in Settings, else the job the site's market was concluded with (`sites.market`), else the first claim read off the site. It is never an answer to one of setup's five questions, which are notes for the writer. *The description.* The index's description is that line as a sentence, followed by "Articles and guides from {business}." where the two fit in 155 characters and the line alone is under 120. A line over 155 is cut at a word. A business with no line keeps the one sentence, and nothing is padded to reach 120. The feed and the index's structured data carry the same description. *The author.* A byline is split at its first " · ", dash or comma into the name and the line. "based in", "located in" or "living in" opens a place: "Tim, Reachkit Founder based in Berlin, Germany" is the Person "Tim", job title "Reachkit Founder", work location "Berlin, Germany". *The update path.* `npm run retarget -- --url <live address> --target "<search>"` prints the one statement that adds an open `refresh_page` at that page's address for the new search (`docs/RUNBOOK.md` §4). It is published at the address its new search names, and the old address answers 301 to it (issue 1487). It is written as the new search's own type and titled for it, not as a refresh of the live text, and its acceptance test is the new search's.
- 2026-10-06  **A retarget is stored at the top fit** (#1285; a drafted default the owner may correct). The search an operator retargets a live page to is their own choice of target, so its row is stored at business fit 3 and no pass judges it. Found on reachkit.app: the 3 October page's retarget, "best AI visibility tools for small businesses", was left for the pass to judge, was scored 1 (`low_fit`) and could never be written. The pass's judgement already read the market by its `searchTerm` and searched terms (D49); what scored it 1 was the scale's own line that a generic "best" list is 1 at most. The scale now says that the market's category and synonyms are the names of one market, and that a "best" list for any of them is the product's own category: 3 where it names the product's buyer, 2 otherwise.
- 2026-10-17  **A retarget moves the page; a refresh keeps it** (issue 1487; the half-of-the-words reading and the refusal are drafted defaults the owner may correct). Found: content.reachkit.app/tools-to-track-where-ai-sends-potential-customers served "How to rank on ChatGPT when buyers search your market" — its address, canonical and published date were the old page's, and the 10 October page still linked it as "tools to track where ChatGPT sends potential customers". Now a retarget (D53, D60) changes what a hosted page is about, so it publishes at the slug its new search names, as a new page's is. The versions at the old address leave the live set through the state machine (`published → unpublished`, reason `moved`, by the actor that published the new version; no unpublish outcome: the page was not taken down), and the old address answers **301** to the new one on the same host. The canonical, the sitemap entry and the published date are the new page's, with no updated date: no earlier version stands at that address. No live page's text is edited in place (ADR-080). Every live page of the site that linked the old address is planned a `link` refresh (*Links in both directions*): its new version links the new address, named as *Links* names a page (`pageName`), the sentence kept — code points the link and the writer returns the page as it stands. It is an update like any other, so §6's 30-day floor stands, and a younger page is planned on the first hour it is old enough. *Repair.* Each hour, in the publish tick and before any page is attempted, a live hosted retarget still at its old address — published before this, or whose move did not complete — is recorded at the address its new search names, as delivery would have recorded it, and the same move code runs; its draft stays `published` and its text is untouched. It is left where it is when a newer version is live at the old address or another live page holds the new slug. 0¢: rows only. A refresh keeps its search and keeps its address. A retarget whose slug is another live page's is refused (`destination_rejected`): that page is already the search's. A live address and its title describe one topic where at least half the slug's words are the title's. *How it squares with #888.* The redirect never leaves the site's own host and is composed from the host the site answers at now; an old host is still not one ReachKit serves and still answers 404. A WordPress post keeps its own address: its host is the customer's.
- 2026-10-06  **Context stays relevant: facts** (owner, D59, #1287). A context pack drops a rival pricing fact read more than 30 days ago. The draft screen's *written from your business* lists each fact with its age.
- 2026-10-06  **How D59's facts are built** (#1287; each reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). A rival's price read 31 days before the pack is built is not in the pack; one read 30 days before is. A rival left with no claim of its own is no longer a product the page may name, so a comparison left with fewer than two is written as a Q&A (D47). The age is whole days from the day the fact's page was read to the day the draft screen is opened: "read today", "read 1 day ago", "read {n} days ago" (`draft.written-from.age.today`, `.one`, `.days`). It stands beside each business fact and each public page's claim; a page written before the pack recorded read dates shows none.
- 2026-10-17  **A rival's claim keeps its plan and its own page, and no unsupported negative** (issue 1485, from the SEO review of 2026-10-17; owner on the issue: *"Unpublish it now"* for content.reachkit.app/profound-alternatives; every reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found live: that page set Profound's free trial out as the product ("50 prompts run daily for 7 days", three engines) where Profound's own pricing page offers a trial and an Enterprise plan of nine engines; it cited a reviewer's "$99" beside Profound's own page; and its FAQ said Profound has no free tier. (1) *The plan.* The claims call says of each claim on a rival's own page the plan, tier or trial it belongs to, as the page names it, or none where it is true of every plan. A plan the page does not name drops the claim. Every rival claim is stored with that plan and its page (`drafts.meta.marketFacts`). No call is added: the same claims call, inside `CAPS.DRAFT_C`. (2) *Its own page first.* The call also says which named rival a claim off another page is about; where that rival's own pages yielded a claim, the other page's claim about it is not kept. Where they yielded none, it stays, cited to its own page. (3) *`rival_source` reads both.* A sentence that names a rival and links another page that one of the page's facts about it was read from, where the rival's own page was read, fails; so does a sentence that uses a rival's claim of one plan without naming that plan. A price-list line recorded before plans were names its plan only where its own words do ("Standard $ 189 /month"); a line that says the price is agreed ("tailored pricing") is how the whole product is priced. (4) *`rival_negative`*, a new stage-4 rule, is the one rule this adds. It fails a sentence of the body, or of the FAQ stored beside it, where a clause names a rival and says no, not, lacks, without or the like, unless a stored fact about that rival denies something too and shares a subject with the sentence; where the rival's own page was read only that page can. The draft screen names it *Nothing a competitor lacks unless a source says so* (`draft.checks.rule.rival-negative`), in *Honesty*; a stopped draft drops the sentence in code, and the FAQ goes to the fix call. (5) *The table.* A rival's cell names the tier it shows where its claim is of one plan ("Free trial: …"), and *What it does* prefers a claim true of every plan. (6) *Live pages.* Each hour, before any page is attempted, every live page written before these rules (no `meta.rivalScope`) and not edited by the customer is read again from its own row at 0¢; one that fails either rule is withdrawn through `unpublish()` by the system actor `content/recheck`, every live version at its address, and a hosted address then answers 410 as any page taken down does (issue 1458). The page is not corrected by a direct row edit (ADR-080). Read against the stored rows of 2026-10-17, only profound-alternatives fails.
- 2026-10-17  **A how-to or a Q&A is built from cited outside facts** (issue 1486, classified from the content review of 17 October, item 3; every number and reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found: reachkit.app's how-tos of 7 and 10 October read as product manuals. Page D's pack held 24 claims off six public pages and its body cited none; seven of its ten steps were ReachKit features. Now: (1) **Research.** On a how-to or a Q&A, the one search that found studies looks for the target's own guidance instead ("{target} official guidance"; German "{target} offizielle Anleitung"), so no call is added. A result whose address is a platform's or an authority's own documentation is read first and kept as a `primary` source. That is a government or intergovernmental host, or a host or first path segment that names docs, developers, help, support, learn or a knowledge base. It is read off the address alone, for every kind of business, and a rival's help page is never one. (2) **The pack** of a how-to or a Q&A holds claims off at least `CITES_MARKET.min` (two) distinct public pages (`outside_sources`). A how-to then needs one business fact on its topic, down from three. (3) **A new stage-4 check, `cites_market`**, stops a generated how-to or Q&A whose body links fewer than that many of the pages its pack read claims off. Each page counts once, and the *Sources* list code appends does not count. A failing page takes the one fix call. (4) **The business is at most one step.** A how-to step that names the business or links its own site is its own, and `required_blocks` stops a how-to with more than one (`own_steps`). A Q&A keeps the business to its own section, as its template already said. (5) **D12 on these pages**: the report's own measurements are not information gain, so a site's score never makes a how-to or a Q&A "add" something. On every other type they still count (#1163). (6) **A short pack** is the #1477 short pack: the topic writes nothing, costs its research alone, and the day goes to the next ready topic in the same run. A how-to short of outside sources is never written from business facts alone. *Left alone:* listicles and comparisons; documentation, research and current insights; an update; a founder's own edit. The lead's free first page (§2) reads no research under `CAPS.FREE_C`. Its how-to is therefore short and falls back to the Q&A form, as any short lead pack does, and that Q&A is held to every need but outside sources, as before. *New strings:* `draft.checks.rule.cites-market` ("Links the outside sources it is built from") and `publish.needs-you.pack.need.outside_sources` ("public pages, such as the official guidance on this task, with facts to cite").
- 2026-10-18  **Six consistency rules, and live pages mended through a refresh** (issue 1507, from the SEO review of 2026-10-17, item 8, as amended by the master's SEO review of 2026-10-18 on the issue; every reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). Each was found on a live page. (1) *Description figures*: every figure in the meta description is one the body states, both read in the page's language (D66) — "$1,597", "1597" and a German "1.597 €" are one number, "5" is the body's "five", and a figure whose separators each stand before three digits is also read as grouped; a year passes where it is the page's own (the year it was published or updated); a single digit with no currency or percent mark is a count the page sets out — a `title` fault (`description_figures`) that an update is held to as well, since its description is new. (2) *Steps*: every numbered list runs 1, 2, 3; an item written 1 starts a new list (`required_blocks`, `steps_order`); code counts a list again before stage 4 reads it and after a repair drops a step. (3) *Links*: the link pass never links one page under two addresses (the scans list under several sort orders) nor the same words to two pages; the later link goes — a list item that is only that link with its line, else unwrapped to its words. The same link said twice, and a source named by its host, are not repeats. (4) *No link inside a word*: the numeral repair never links digits glued to a letter ("B2B", "GA4"), and the link pass unwraps one it finds. (5) *Link markup*: a `[` or `]` that is part of no whole link fails (`required_blocks`, `link_markup`); code removes the stray bracket before stage 4. (6) *One H1*: `body_md` holds no level-1 heading, so the rendered page has exactly one `<h1>`, its title; a body heading that repeats the title or the answer box's first sentence word for word (case and the closing stop aside, as the renderer reads the title) fails too (`required_blocks`, `h1`), and the live repair drops it, keeping what stood under it. *Live pages.* Each hour, after the rival re-check, every live page of ours the customer did not edit is read against the six from its own row at 0¢. One that breaks any is planned a `refresh_page` with cause `structure`, naming what it breaks; the customer's stop and §6's 30-day floor stand as for every refresh. A refresh whose every defect code repairs is *mended*: no model is asked (0¢); the new version is the live text with them repaired — counted again, deduplicated, unwrapped, bracket removed, headings one level down, a repeating heading dropped — entering review like any page, its claim checked against the customer's list. A code-only repair keeps the version's published and updated dates, `dateModified` and `lastmod` (`drafts.meta.datedAs`, the date of the version it mends; the refresh causes count a mended version's age from it too); only a refresh that changes what the page says moves them. Any other refresh of our own page is written from the live text with those repairs already made, so a defect code cannot mend (a description figure) is the writer's. No live row is edited (ADR-080). No call is added: the refresh is the pace's own update. A WordPress post's modified date is WordPress's own.
- 2026-10-18  **An alternatives page counts real options and never its subject; N in the title is the options** (issue 1508, from the SEO review of 2026-10-17, item 6, as amended by the SEO master review of 2026-10-18 on the issue; every reading and number here is a drafted default the owner may correct, except the bound the owner ruled on, below). Found: page E, "3 Profound alternatives", counted Profound as one of its own three, and left out Peec AI, a stored rival with its own pricing facts, where the pages it competes with list five to thirteen. (1) **The baseline.** On a listicle whose search asks for alternatives to one product, in any supported language ("squarespace alternatives", "alternatives to puregym", "Hilti Alternativen", "Alternative zu Hilti"), that product is the baseline: a rival in force, a product the top results name, or a site of the stored top ten, matched where a run of the search's words spells its stored name, its domain's own label or its whole domain. Its own pages are read. It has a row in the table under the business's, marked "{name} (for comparison)" (`body.table.baseline`), and a short section saying what it offers (`baseline`), and it is not one of the options: not counted, not an `option` section, and not a rival the verdict may name (`required_blocks`). Any other page carries no such section. On a "best {category}" page there is no baseline, and the business is one of the counted options (D52's cap still holds). (2) **The options** are the rivals in force and the products named on two or more of the first five top results the research step already fetched (`RESEARCH.namedFrom`; the claim calls still read three) — a site linked by words that spell its domain, never the result's own site, the business's, or a listing site or platform (`LISTING_HOSTS`, `PLATFORM_DOMAINS`). A product found this way is an option only where its own pages share a word with the market's category or synonyms (D49) and, for a business that serves one place, name that place or its region (D67); a rival in force was chosen for both already. Each is kept only where its own page yields a claim, scoped to its plan (issue 1485). Each product's first page goes to the claim calls before any product's second, inside the same at most two calls. (3) **Too few.** A listicle counts at least four options, the business among them (`RESEARCH.optionsMin`). Two rivals or more but fewer than four options is not ready: the pack is short of *options*, nothing is written, and the day goes to the next topic (D26, #1477); fewer than two rivals is still a Q&A (D47). With four, the page is written and its method section says why only four qualified (under `RESEARCH.optionsAim`, five). It never pads. (4) **The count.** On a listicle, a bare whole number among the title's first three words is its count of options, and `title` fails one that is not the table's options (`count`); the writer is handed the counted options and the baseline by name. (5) **The bound** is the own pages of eight products on a listicle, an alternatives page's subject among them (`RESEARCH.options`, below); every other page keeps four (`RESEARCH.rivals`).
- 2026-10-18  **A listicle reads the own pages of up to eight products** (owner on issue 1508: *"follow all recommendations from our seo master"*, and on 2026-10-18, shown the measured cost below: *"go with 8"*; amends #1135's bound of four for that type alone). The research step of a listicle reads the pricing and feature pages of up to eight products (`RESEARCH.options`), an alternatives page's subject among them; every other type keeps four. The reads are free and the claims stay in the same at most two calls of seven pages, so a draft's claim calls are handed at most fourteen pages where they were handed thirteen. *Cost, measured at 0¢:* the 18 claims calls stored in production from 3 to 18 October cost about 2,100 input and 255 output tokens for each page handed in, 0.34¢ a page at the small tier's price. On the panel's recorded pages the bound adds 0 to 4 pages a draft, 0 to 1.4¢; the most it can add is seven pages, 2.4¢, where every rival in force has one page and every new product two. At D52's one listicle in seven days that is at most about 10¢ a site a month. A draft whose delta is over 1¢ is reported to the owner by the master before its pull request merges (the owner's condition on the ruling).
- 2026-10-18  **A topic is one people search or ask AI about, in the business's own market; a live page on one that no longer qualifies is moved, kept or retired by what it already earns** (owner on issue 1506: *"file it and start it"*, and on 2026-10-18: *"follow D53 for A and B"*, *"follow all recommendations from our seo master"*; from the SEO review of 2026-10-17, item 7, as the SEO master review of 2026-10-18 amended it on the issue; every other reading here is a drafted default the owner may correct). Found: reachkit.app's live pages "best answer engine optimization services" (volume 20, a top ten of agencies, for a software product) and "reachkit vs aeo engine" (no measured volume, a rival the market dropped), and a queue of nothing but "best X", vs and alternatives. (1) *A "vs" between products* — the business against a rival, or two products — is ready only with a measured volume above zero; where a tracked prompt, or the AI answer the row was found in, names one of its rivals (amended 2026-10-18 by issue 1520: a tracked prompt counts only where its measured answer names every side); or where buyers were seen asking it beside the market's searches: a related search, or a "People also ask" question holding every word of it, in a SERP the site's research stored in the last 30 days (D62's store, 0¢; the search's own SERP does not count). Otherwise `no_intent`. A product is the business's own name, a rival in force, or any product a pass read a brand volume for. A "vs" between two category terms names none and is not held by this. (2) *Another kind of business.* A row whose stored top ten — the scan that found it, bought no more than 30 days before; nothing is bought — holds six or more results of another kind of business is `low_fit`: agencies for a software product (an agency's words in a result's title), directories for a shop that sells everywhere (a listing host; a shop that serves one place is found through them, so they are its market), publishers for a local service (a round-up or article result, or a "best" beside a number in any language). A business of that kind itself is exempt: hospitality, professional services and creators have no other kind, and a market read before kinds (D69) says nothing. (3) *The mix* (D36): of the site's last seven new pages, refreshes not counted, at least three answer — a Q&A, a how-to or documentation — where one is ready. A day whose six new pages before it already hold four others takes one, after the update and the pace and before the count and the turn of kinds, never ahead of the topic's rotation, and it never empties a day. (4) *"X vs Y" between two category terms* is an `answer` page, its source `category`. (5) *A People-also-ask question* that names a rival and asks about the word itself — its meaning, a synonym, how it is said, in any language ("Profound alternative word?") — is not a buyer question, whatever the market. (6) *Live pages.* Each hour, beside the rival recheck, every live page the customer has not edited is read again from its own row against (1) and (2) at 0¢. One that fails waits where a retarget of it is already planned, and is retargeted where the site holds a ready topic in its cluster that no other live page targets: that row becomes a retarget of the page (D53), written through the refresh path and published at its search's slug, the old address answering 301 (issue 1487). It is *merged* into another live page of the site — every live version at its address leaves the live set through the state machine (`published → unpublished`, reason `moved`, by `content/recheck`, no unpublish outcome), each row names that page (`publications.merged_into`), the address answers 301 to it wherever it lives now, and the pages that linked it are planned #1487's `link` refresh — where another live page of its cluster passes these rules (it has replaced it), or where another live page already targets the topic a retarget would take (one page a search). A page on the customer's own host has no 301 of ours: it stays live and is named in the run's report. Otherwise it is withdrawn only where nothing says it is worth keeping: it is at least 90 days old or a verdict has judged it past `too_early`; no Monday verdict was `working`; the stored ranked keywords since it went live show its address in no top-30 position; and `publication_reads` holds no answer-engine read or referral. Nothing records any other referrer, so none is ever on file. A page with any of these signals is kept and planned as a refresh (§6's 30 days stand). So is every page on a host the weekly measurement cannot see (issue 316: today, every hosted page on its content host), never retired blind. Where another live page of the site is in its cluster, the page is merged into it rather than withdrawn: a 301, never a 410, while a page is that close. Only otherwise is it withdrawn through `unpublish()` by the system actor `content/recheck`, every live version at its address, and a hosted address answers 410; from then on every hosted page of the site shows a link to that address as its words without the link, at render (the sitemap and the feed already leave it out; IndexNow is #1511's). Never a direct row edit (ADR-080). *Pages A and B follow D53* (owner, 2026-10-18): A's open retarget runs through #1487's move (new slug, 301), and B stays until a D50 comparison replaces it, then is merged into it with a 301. Both are dry-report fixtures, not withdrawal fixtures. Read against the stored rows of 2026-10-18, A waits on its move, B is too young to judge (13 days, every verdict `too_early`, on the content host), and the other two pages pass. (7) The panel check *a ready "vs" between products has measured demand* runs in `npm run panel`. *Note for the owner:* the draft of 18 October, "peec ai vs otterly", would still be ready under (1) as worded, because the tracked prompt "Peec AI or Otterly: which is the better choice…" names both. It adds no sentence to any screen.
- 2026-10-18  **The content host is tied to the main site as one business** (owner on issue 1511: *"follow all recommendations from our seo master"*; each reading is a drafted default the owner may correct). (1) *Main-site link.* Every scan records, for each host ReachKit serves on the domain, whether the crawled pages that are not ReachKit's (`readOurPages`) link it: *linked*, *linked, not followed* (every anchor's `rel` holds `nofollow`, `sponsored` or `ugc`), or *not linked*. Only server-HTML anchors count. ReachKit's own hosts are recorded and never crawled, so they spend none of the 100 pages. Overview states it once, as an optional finding with a plain `<a href>` to copy, whose words are the blog's name in the site's language (D66). It never blocks anything, is never a needs-you item, and is not drawn once the link is found, for a WordPress destination, or before the host is live. (2) *IndexNow.* Each publish, update and unpublish on a hosted destination whose host is live sends exactly one IndexNow request for the address(es) it changed and the index; a move (2026-10-17) sends its old address, its new one and the index in the one request. The key is the site's own, stable and not a secret, served as a text file named after the key at the root of its host (`src/app/(hosted)/hosted-page/indexnow/[key]/route.ts`). A WordPress destination sends none, because ReachKit cannot serve a file at its root. A refusal, a 429 or a network failure is logged and dropped, never retried, and never holds a publish. (3) *WebSub.* The feed names Google's public hub, `pubsubhubbub.appspot.com`, and each of those changes pings it once, failures ignored; this is the lowest-value of the five. (4) *One entity.* The hosted Organization (author and publisher) has `url` = the main site's canonical home as the scan read it (after its redirects and at its own canonical, so a `www.` or a language path), and `@id` = the main site's own declared Organization `@id`, else `{home}#organization`. Its `name` and `logo` are the main site's own where its markup states them. `sameAs` lists only the profile pages the main site's home links to and its own `Organization.sameAs`, never an invented one. The header button, the about card, breadcrumb item 1 and a plain footer link all use that same home. (5) *No internal link to a redirect.* A link to an address of the host that moved is drawn at its final address when the page renders. The stored body is not edited (ADR-080), the dates do not move, and the `link` refresh still follows. A WordPress post keeps its address on a move, so it has none to resolve. All five are 0¢ and hold for every customer type, except where a condition names its destination.
- 2026-10-10  **A page does not read as machine-written** (owner, #1390: *"go ahead with all"*). Found on reachkit.app's how-to of 10 October, about 1,470 words: one sentence about the free scan four times, "rivals instead of you" five times, nine steps each ending "— result:", and 19 dashes. Six things follow, and each is general: none is tuned to one site, and none is a step for the customer.
  1. **A prose check in stage 4, in code** (`plain_prose`). It counts what a reader knows a generated page by: dashes that join two halves of a sentence; a sentence said three times; a run of words said over and over, with a tagline capped at two; a list whose items all turn on one label; sentences all one length; and, on an English page, the turns of phrase a model reaches for ("this matters", "is more than a … it is", "less X, more Y", "not X, it's Y", "looking ahead", "serves as", a summing-up ending, signposting, stock vocabulary). A heading, a table, a pull quote, the sources list, a fact stated word for word and the page's own target are not counted. This is the one rule that reads how a page is worded; D27 stands for every other rule, and a prose check that stops a good page is a defect in it.
  2. **Its hits go into the one fix call.** There is no new call. The fix is told each count and the page's own words. A page that still fails is stopped and its reason logged, as for every other check; it is never published as it is. The check binds what ReachKit writes: a page the customer edits is theirs to word.
  3. **The writer's house rules.** Plain "X is Y" sentences, mixed sentence lengths, one stated opinion of the business's own backed by a fact from the pack, no summing-up ending, no signposting, no dash as a joint, and a short avoid-list of the habits the check counts.
  4. **Three voices, chosen for each page.** *Ours* (the default): the business speaking, "we", and "you" to the reader. *Guide*: a neutral guide in the third person, for a formal or regulated business. *Short*: a short answer of 600–900 words for a simple question. The customer may fix one in Settings → Your voice; it is never a step, and setup does not ask.
  5. **Headings from real buyer questions.** A section is headed by a question buyers were seen to ask (Google's "People also ask", the target's related searches with a measured volume, the report's measured questions, as D61 reads them for a Q&A), not by a fixed set. The required blocks stay: the answer first, the steps of a how-to, the table of a comparison.
  6. **Statistics and quotations.** A page aims for two to four named statistics or quotations, each from the pack with its source's link, and never an invented one.
- 2026-10-10  **How the six are built** (#1390; every number and list here is a drafted default the owner may correct). *The thresholds* are `PROSE` in `src/lib/config/constants.ts`, set against pages people wrote on the panel's sites and the pages ReachKit had written (`docs/walk/prose-gate.md`, rewritten by `npm run eval:content -- --prose`, 0¢): more than 7 joining dashes per 1,000 words and at least 4; a sentence said three times; a run of seven words said three times, or of four said six times; a label three items of one list turn on; a sentence spread under 0.3 over 20 sentences; one construction three times, or seven tell phrases together. *Measured and left out:* groups of three and sentence spread do not separate people's pages from ours, so threes are only asked of the writer and the spread stops only a page of sentences all one length. *Libraries.* `slopless` (MIT) reads an English page for the constructions, through textlint's kernel, loaded on the server when a page is checked; a run of it that fails flags nothing and the counts still hold the page. No `retext` plugin was added: on the same pages each flagged people's writing more than ours. A page in another language is held to the counts alone. *The voice* is `src/lib/generate/voice/register.ts`: *guide* where the market's category names law, tax, accounting, finance, insurance, banking or medicine (English and German roots), or the site's voice text says it writes formally; *short* where the page is a Q&A or documentation and its question opens as one a fact settles (is, can, does, what is, how much, when, where) in ten words or fewer and asks for no comparison; else *ours*. The customer's choice is `sites.writing_voice` and stands, except that a comparison, how-to, research, insights or update page is never cut to a short answer. The page's voice is recorded in its pack. The strings are `settings.voice.writing.label`, `.auto`, `.ours`, `.guide`, `.short` and `.note`. *The questions.* Every page that is researched now reads its target's "People also ask" and related searches (one search and one keyword-overview call, about 1.2¢ a page; a Q&A and a comparison already did), and every new page's pack holds the questions a fact in it answers. A how-to and a documentation page may carry up to three more sections, each one such question no other section answers. An update keeps its live headings. *Statistics* are asked of the writer and counted by no check: `traceable_numerals`, `rival_source` and `no_unsourced_testimonial` already stop an invented one.

## §8 Mail

**User gets** Mail only on the occasions below, one shell (why it arrived, how to stop it), plain-text twin. Three switches for stoppable kinds plus one address-wide opt-out. No newsletter.

| name | block/warn | outcome protected | ruling |
|---|---|---|---|
| Onboarding mails | warn | Payment succeeding starts `magic-link` (unstoppable), `account`, `setup-reminder` at +24, 72 and 168 hours, and `draft-ready`. The sequence stops once setup is done and the first draft is in review. | SPEC, 2026-09-12 |
| Free-scan nurture | warn | An address given on the report gets `report`, `first-page` (or its unavailable twin) and `nurture` three times at +24, 72 and 168 hours. It stops on subscribing, on the opt-out, or after the third mail. A missed window is dropped, and the sequence starts only within 7 days of capture. | SPEC, 2026-09-12 |
| Weekly mails | warn | The local Monday re-measure sends `weekly`. It stops when its switch is off or access ends. A week that measured nothing still sends and says so once, and states no numbers. | SPEC, 2026-09-12; owner, 2026-10-07, #1329 |
| Retention mails | warn | Retention is payment-failed, cancellation (with its end date), hosting-end and the win-back once at +30 days. Each stops when its cause resolves: payment succeeds, or the plan resumes. There is no inactivity nudge and no veto reminder, and the tick does not ask for them. | owner, 2026-09-15, #568; D3, 2026-10-19, #1593 |
| Unwritten keys send nothing | warn | A mail with a sentence that is not a written registry key is not sent. | SPEC, 2026-09-12 |
| Two stop stores never merge | warn | The address-wide opt-out and the three per-kind switches are separate stores, and neither reaches the other. Unstoppable kinds still arrive with every switch off, and the sign-in mail is never reachable from any stop control (ADR-042). | SPEC, 2026-09-12; 2026-10-23, #1741 |
| One sender | warn | Every mail is from `hello@reachkit.app`. | SPEC, 2026-09-12 |
| One-click unsubscribe | warn | A mail that carries a stop control also carries RFC 8058's `List-Unsubscribe` and `List-Unsubscribe-Post` headers for the same token as the link in its body, applied on one POST with no session. The address opt-out answers at `/api/opt-out/{token}`. The Monday mail's is its kind-scoped token at `/api/unsubscribe/{token}`. A mail with no stop control, such as the sign-in link, sends neither header. | 2026-09-18, #889; 2026-10-23, #1741 |
| The page-ready mail | warn | The page-ready mail gives the search, its searches a month, who answers it today, the chances, what it was written from and the first lines, then *Read the full page* (a deep link to the page in the app) and *Stop this page*. A page is named by its search, never by its model-written title. | owner, 2026-09-20, #908; O6, 2026-10-31, #2172 |
| Page-ready until the first Monday mail | warn | A customer who never set the page-ready switch gets the page-ready mail until their first Monday mail is sent (`scans.digest_sent_at` on any of their sites), then not. A customer who set it keeps their choice. The switch never holds a page. | owner D3, 2026-10-19, #1593 |
| The first page's live mail only | warn | The published mail is sent for the site's first live publication only, including where that page was taken down later. Every later page is told in the Monday mail. Its subject follows the recorded outcome, it keeps its site-condition notice, and Settings › Mail names its switch "When your first page goes live". | owner D3, 2026-10-19, #1593; O6, 2026-10-31, #2172 |
| Plain words and a preheader | warn | Each mail names one preheader, shown in the inbox after the subject and hidden in the body; the plain-text twin does not carry it. No subject carries a model-written title. No mail repeats in a fact row what the line above it said, and none says a plain-text version is attached. Figures come from the constants that run them. The nurture mails, the win-back line and the legal lines stay word for word until commercialization and trust-compliance have reviewed them, so the nurture and renewal-refunded mails carry no preheader yet. A cancellation with access still running ends on "Changed your mind? Press Resume my plan in Settings › Account."; the refund arm does not. | owner O6, 2026-10-31, #2172 |
| React Email templates | warn | Every kind is `react-email`'s own components in one document, rendered by that library's `render()`, reached through the package. Rendering takes a block list and returns both bodies, reaching no vendor, clock or database. `shell/text-frame.ts` writes the plain-text twin. `npm run mail:preview` serves every kind from `emails/`, beside the files `npm run mail:samples` writes. | 2026-09-23, #934 |
| The render check | warn | Rendering in Gmail and Apple Mail is proved by rendering every kind in the register to files (`npm run mail:samples`) and sending each one once, its subject prefixed `[render check]`, through Resend to the first address in `OWNER_EMAILS` and no other. The owner replies with what renders wrong, and the issue closes on their word. | owner, 2026-09-22, #339 |
| The Monday mail's order | warn | The Monday mail reads as board `MondayMail`: the greeting with the account's name (none where none is stored), Plan's week of service and the site's address; the calm line with the site's publish time, left out when a page needs the customer or the address waits to be connected; Overview's numbers; *This week's pages*; the pace line; and *Open your overview*. What ReachKit changed about the market, the questions that now mention the site, the pages judged, the spares and the one needs-you item follow as facts, and no sentence repeats a number. The mail draws no "We asked AI the question each page answers.", no "We check these once a month.", no "Up to 7 pages a week", no 7-day strip and no "Thu–Sun · 4 more". | owner, 2026-10-20, #1741; 2026-11-01, #2284; 2026-10-07, #1329 |

A step nobody read says it was not measured; before any page's own question is asked the AI row says it is too early; before any page shows in results the steps past Found are one too-early line. No line says a page moved the site's AI visibility unless that page's own tracked question is answered with a link to it (§4). | owner D16, 2026-09-29, #1147; 2026-10-04, #1147 |

## §9 Technical issues

**User gets** Faults in plain words, with a count and a severity, on the report, the dashboard and mail. Who fixes each (them in ten minutes, or ReachKit). Copyable fix lines on the free report. Re-checked every Monday.

| name | block/warn | outcome protected | ruling |
|---|---|---|---|
| The checks | warn | Checks run across the crawled profile pages, up to 100: missing or duplicate title, missing or duplicate meta description, `noindex` on a page that should be indexed, no sitemap, slow pages, broken internal links, not usable on a phone, missing structured data, and answer readers blocked (in `robots.txt`, or by a snippet control on a page). | SPEC, 2026-09-12 |
| Severity and who does it | warn | Severity is Critical, Worth fixing or Nothing to fix. Who does it is "Free fix · 10 min", "ReachKit writes" or "ReachKit rewrites". | SPEC, 2026-09-12 |
| Severity steps | warn | A zero is Nothing to fix. A site-wide fault (a `noindex` home page, no sitemap, an answer reader blocked) is Critical whenever present. A per-page count is Worth fixing from one page and Critical at 25% of the set it was counted over. | SPEC, 2026-09-14 |
| Slow page, two reads | warn | A page is slow where ReachKit's own fetch took 3 s or more. Such a page is read once more in the same crawl, under the same bounds; it is slow only where both reads took 3 s or more, and the quicker of the two is kept on the report and shown beside the page. A second read that does not come back leaves the page not slow. A page served from the cache has no timing and is left out of that count's set. 0¢: the second read is the crawl's own fetch. | SPEC, 2026-09-14; owner, 2026-10-19, #1550 (drafted) |
| Answer readers and training crawlers are two lists | warn | *Answer readers* (Googlebot, OAI-SearchBot, Claude-SearchBot, PerplexityBot, `AI_READER_AGENTS`) are what an AI answer reads; *training crawlers* (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, Google-Extended) feed training. The blocked-readers check counts answer readers blocked in `robots.txt`, and pages carrying `nosnippet`, `data-nosnippet` on the main content, or `max-snippet:0`. A training opt-out is shown as the founder's choice, never as an issue. Robots fix lines name the answer readers only. The hosted robots policy allows all seven. | owner D14, 2026-09-29, #1145 |
| How the blocked-readers check counts | warn | A snippet control counts when it is in a robots meta tag addressed to every reader or to an answer reader by name. `data-nosnippet` is on the main content when it wraps the page's `<main>` or covers at least half of its text. The count is blocked answer readers plus such pages; a blocked reader is Critical, and with none blocked the pages follow the per-page steps. Only training crawlers the `robots.txt` names are shown as the founder's choice; a blanket `Disallow` is not. The score's access gates read the four answer readers; snippet controls do not move the score. A stored report is re-read under the two lists from its own stored access rules. | 2026-10-04, #1145 (drafted) |
| A `noindex` home page scores zero | warn | A `noindex` home page is Foundations = 0, not "unmeasured". | SPEC, 2026-09-12 |
| A crawled page is a page the site means to be found | warn | The crawl counts an address once. A query-string variant is a page only when its own `<link rel="canonical">` keeps the query; otherwise it is the page its canonical names (or, with no canonical, the address without the query), and that page is read instead. One variant of a path is read to learn which; the rest are not fetched. A page whose canonical points to another site is skipped. A page that tells every reader `noindex` is skipped unless it is the home page or the site's own sitemap lists it; those two are what "a page that should be indexed" means in the `noindex` check. A skipped page is not in the inventory, so no check counts it, no Fix opportunity is made for it, and it is neither known content nor a cross-link target. Answerability is the home page and the detected pricing page, with the measured pages a caller passes, each at its own canonical address. | owner, 2026-10-03, #1169 ("unless the sitemap lists it" and the no-canonical address drafted) |
| Fix opportunity | warn | A ReachKit-fixable issue becomes a Fix opportunity: one per crawled page that failed a ReachKit-fixed check, naming every such check it failed, and none for a page the hosted destination serves. It ranks just ahead of the first new writing in its cluster; while clusters are not derived, ahead of the first new writing at all. | SPEC, 2026-09-14 |
| Fix delivery | warn | A Fix is a metadata-only update: a new title and/or meta description written from the page's own words, content untouched, through the same veto path. It is ready only where the WordPress destination can update the page (its own host, a page with a slug, an SEO plugin for a description). Otherwise the opportunity stands and is not ready. Structured data has no WordPress field and is not ready. | SPEC, 2026-09-14 |
| A check that could not run | warn | It is absent with one why-line, never "no issues found". It is not paywalled. | SPEC, 2026-09-12 |
| Who fixes each check | warn | "Free fix · 10 min": `noindex` on an indexable page, no sitemap, slow pages, broken internal links, not usable on a phone, answer readers blocked. "ReachKit rewrites": missing or duplicate title, missing or duplicate meta description. "ReachKit writes": missing structured data. Copyable lines only where the line is fixed: the viewport meta line (phone), the robots `Sitemap:` line (sitemap), the robots `Allow` records (answer readers). | SPEC, 2026-09-14 |
| Approved issue copy | warn | The technical-issue titles, the not-run line and the Needs-you issue controls are owner-approved. | owner, 2026-09-15, #716 |
| Needs you holds the customer's fixes | warn | On the dashboard, "Needs you" holds only the issues the customer fixes ("Free fix · 10 min"), Critical first, after drafts waiting on them; ReachKit's own rewrites are Fix work. It reads the newest stored report (Monday's, or the deep pass before the first Monday). A check is a needs-you item only where it has a fix sentence and, for a count of pages or links, the addresses it was counted from; broken internal links keep the addresses that answered with an error. A report written before the addresses or the second read were kept states the count on the report and raises no item. | SPEC, 2026-09-14; owner, 2026-10-19, #1550 (drafted) |
| A needs-you issue opens Technical issues | warn | A technical issue in Overview's needs-you opens *Technical issues* at `/app/issues`, inside the account, at that check, never the free report at `/scan/{domain}`. The view holds one card per check waiting on the customer: its title, its count over its set, its severity, one sentence saying what to do, and the addresses it applies to (or that it applies to the whole site). It is not in the navigation; Overview's needs-you and its count of the rest open it. | owner, 2026-10-19, #1550 (drafted) |
| A hosted address with no published page carries the host name | warn | Its title is the host name and nothing else: no ReachKit name, no sentence of ours, and no word in any language on a domain that is not ours. A published page keeps its own title. | owner, 2026-09-21, #980 |
| Only a cause critical for the customer is told | warn | Every reason written on a move into `needs_attention` is a closed `StopReason` (`src/lib/publish/record/needs-you.ts`), and `critical()` classifies it. Critical: `reason_needs_customer` (an expired or invalid credential, no destination, DNS not pointed, a destination that refused), the customer's own words (`live_edit`, and a `rules:*` or `step_failed:*` page with `meta.first_edited_at`, which no re-check touches), and a reason this build cannot read. These are told once: the Overview alert, the badge and the Monday mail's one needs-you line. Not critical: `rules:*` and `step_failed:*` on a page the customer has not edited; the daily re-check rescues or sets them aside, and they get no alert, no badge, no Monday line and no cause sentence. `publish.needs-you.live-edit` is drafted; the owner corrects it. | owner O7, 2026-10-31, #2163 |
| Delivery is re-attempted for seven days | warn | `retries_exhausted` is re-attempted by `{system, publish/retry}` once a day, 23 h after its last claim, through `needs_attention → publishing` with every guard on that edge. After `DELIVERY_REATTEMPT_DAYS` (7) from its first rest it is no longer re-attempted and is critical. | owner O7, 2026-10-31, #2163 |

**Done when** A `noindex` home, no sitemap and a blocked answer reader show as three named issues. A pasteable fix is on the free report. Fixing one drops it next Monday.

| name | block/warn | outcome protected | ruling |
|---|---|---|---|

Any other country or language is out: such a site is measured in the nearest supported country in English, and its report says so. Every screen and every mail is English, the free report and the site mails included. | owner D66, 2026-10-07, #1311; owner, 2026-10-15, #1443 |

Verified 2026-09-18: the Supabase org is on the **Free** plan, which has no point-in-time recovery and no scheduled backups, and dev and production are that one project — so a dev experiment is a production write and there is nothing behind a mistake. The owner holds the account: the plan change, the PITR add-on, and the one restore drill are all theirs. docs/RUNBOOK.md §9 names the screens (the org is Vercel-Marketplace-managed, so the plan changes on Vercel), the retention to ask for (7 days, ~$100/mo on top of Pro and Small compute), what PITR does and does not cover, and the drill step by step: restore to a point five minutes ago into a scratch clone, confirm one named row is back, record the wall-clock time. Until that number is written down the product's recovery time is unknown. No guard exists today beyond care.
